# TakeTray: Decisions

A log of the meaningful decisions behind TakeTray: what we chose, why, and what we gave up. Newest decisions go at the bottom of their section. When a decision changes, don't delete the old entry; mark it **Superseded by D-xx** and add the new one.

**Format of an entry**

```
### D-xx · Title
- Date: YYYY-MM-DD · Phase/Task: …
- Decision: what we chose.
- Why: the reasons.
- Alternatives considered: what else, and why not.
- Consequences: what this commits us to, or makes harder.
```

---

## Product

### D-01 · Film with the phone's normal camera, never an in-app camera
- Date: 2026-10-22 · Phase: brainstorming
- Decision: TakeTray never records video. Users film with the built-in camera app; TakeTray detects the new videos.
- Why: the built-in camera keeps full quality and every mode (4K/60, slo-mo, cinematic). Android's camera "capture intent" gives a stripped-down camera and one clip per round trip.
- Alternatives considered: capture intent (lower quality, slow); a custom camera (months of work, can't match the stock camera).
- Consequences: we depend on detecting new gallery videos reliably (see D-08).

### D-02 · Tag after shooting, with a manual Shooting mode switch
- Date: 2026-10-23 · Phase: brainstorming
- Decision: while Shooting mode is on, every new video lands in To tag; the user tags each clip into one or more reels, or skips it. Videos shot before switching it on are ignored.
- Why: creators tag in spare moments (bus, train, evening), not at a fixed time; an "active reel" mode was rejected because clips for different reels get mixed within minutes.
- Alternatives considered: active reel assigned by time (rejected by the owner); always-on watching (fills To tag between trips).
- Consequences: the tag player must be fast (auto-advance, "Same as previous", scenes).

### D-03 · Videos only in version 1
- Date: 2026-10-23 · Phase: brainstorming
- Decision: v1 handles videos; photos later.
- Why: creators shoot hundreds of photos, which would bury the to-tag list; videos are the reel material.
- Consequences: data model should not block photos later.

### D-04 · Never copy, move or delete the user's originals
- Date: 2026-10-23 · Phase: brainstorming
- Decision: reels store references to gallery videos. A clip can be in several reels. Removing it from a reel or deleting a reel never touches the video. Only exception: temporary VN copies (D-05).
- Why: no storage cost, no risk to the user's footage, trust.
- Consequences: references can break (deleted videos → grey "Missing clip" tile); fingerprints guard against pointing at the wrong video.

### D-05 · Editor handoff: direct share for CapCut and Edits, temporary copy folder for VN
- Date: 2026-10-23 · Phase: brainstorming
- Decision: CapCut and Instagram Edits receive the original clips via multi-video share (no copies). VN gets copies in `Movies/TakeTray/<Reel>`, deleted only after the user confirms export ("Done editing").
- Why: VN accepts one shared video at a time and opens a new project per share (tested by the owner); its picker does show gallery folders (tested). CapCut and Edits accept several videos at once (tested).
- Alternatives considered: moving originals into a folder (breaks for clips in several reels, Google Photos backup risk); generating editor project files (formats are private).
- Consequences: storage cost only while editing VN; space check and reminders needed.

### D-06 · Reference reels are links only
- Date: 2026-10-23 · Phase: design add-on 1
- Decision: an Instagram reel shared into TakeTray is saved as a link (plus an optional label) on a reel. Never downloaded, never previewed.
- Why: Instagram doesn't allow other apps to download or fetch reels; link-only keeps us within the rules and offline.
- Consequences: reference cards are designed placeholders, not thumbnails.

### D-07 · Name: TakeTray
- Date: 2026-10-23 · Phase: naming
- Decision: the app is called TakeTray ("takes" collected in a tray). Tagline "Shoot now. Sort later." Icon: the tray concept.
- Why: short, memorable, no app found with that name; avoids Instagram trademarks ("Insta", "Gram").
- Consequences: the project folder stays `reel-helper` (owner's choice).

### D-08 · Reel statuses: Collecting, Ready to edit, Published
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build brainstorming
- Decision: the first status is "Collecting" (was "Recording").
- Why: "Recording" blurred with "Shooting mode".

## Privacy and business

### D-09 · Fully offline; release build has no INTERNET permission
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build brainstorming
- Decision: no analytics, crash reporting or network calls. The release manifest removes `INTERNET`.
- Why: the promise "your videos never leave your phone"; Android itself then guarantees it; Play data safety can say "no data collected".
- Alternatives considered: Firebase Crashlytics / Sentry (data leaves the phone, must be declared).
- Consequences: bugs are found through a local error log the user can share (D-10).

### D-10 · Local error log, shared manually
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build brainstorming
- Decision: a rotating log file (2 × 512 KB) in app storage; Settings lets the user send it via the share sheet.
- Why: replaces crash reporting without breaking D-09.

### D-11 · Version 1 is free, no payments
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build brainstorming
- Decision: no Play Billing in v1; code must not make adding it later hard.

## Engineering

### D-12 · Flutter, Android first
- Date: 2026-10-23 · Phase: tech choice
- Decision: Flutter + Dart; Android only for v1; iOS later from the same code (`docs/ios-plan.md`). Minimum Android 11 (API 30). Package `app.taketray`.
- Why: one codebase for both platforms later; the owner's preference.
- Consequences: platform-specific parts (gallery, background, editors) are written natively and bridged.

### D-13 · Architecture: feature-first, MVVM per Flutter's guide
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build design
- Decision: `app/lib/features/<feature>/{data,view_models,views,widgets}`; shared `core/`, `domain/`, `data/`, `platform/`.
- Why: Flutter's recommended app architecture; features stay independent and testable.

### D-14 · Riverpod for state and dependency injection
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build design
- Decision: Riverpod with code generation; view models are Notifiers.
- Why: compile-safe, testable (providers overridable in tests), widely used.
- Alternatives considered: Bloc (more boilerplate), Provider (weaker DI and testing).

### D-15 · Drift (SQLite) for the database
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build design
- Decision: Drift with versioned migrations and migration tests.
- Why: relational data (one clip in many reels), reactive queries, in-memory DB for tests.
- Alternatives considered: Isar/Hive (weaker relations/migrations), raw sqflite (no type safety).

### D-16 · freezed + json_serializable for models and export
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build design
- Why: immutable models, equality, and the versioned export/import JSON from the same definitions.

### D-17 · go_router for navigation
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build design
- Why: deep links from notifications and share intents; tab branches keep their own stacks (`StatefulShellRoute`).

### D-18 · Kotlin + Pigeon for Android-specific work; background runs without Flutter
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build design
- Decision: gallery queries, background detection, VN copies and editor sharing are Kotlin, exposed to Dart through Pigeon. The background job (JobScheduler content trigger, WorkManager reminders) runs natively without a Flutter engine and never writes the database; the scan when the app opens does.
- Why: existing plugins don't expose MediaStore generations, `IS_PENDING` or content triggers; Pigeon makes the bridge type-safe; native background work is lighter and more reliable.

### D-19 · Design system as its own package, with an enforced style rule
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build design
- Decision: `packages/taketray_ui` holds tokens, theme and every component, each with golden tests and a Widgetbook entry. Feature code may not use raw colours, text styles, sizes, radii or animation durations; `tool/check_styles` fails the build otherwise.
- Why: one source of visual truth; no repeated or drifting UI code.

### D-20 · Quality tooling
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build design
- Decision: very_good_analysis (zero warnings), alchemist golden tests, Patrol on-device tests, ≥ 80% coverage for domain, data and view models, one-command `tool/check`.
- Why: enterprise-level quality bar agreed with the owner.

### D-21 · Pinned Flutter SDK via wrapper scripts
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: 0
- Decision: all commands use `tool/flutter` / `tool/dart` (Flutter 3.47.7 at `~/development/flutter`).
- Why: the `flutter` on PATH is an old 2.8.1 install with local changes that must not be touched.

### D-22 · Git local until after phase 2
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: build brainstorming
- Decision: local git now; GitHub and CI after phase 2. `tool/check` runs the same checks locally until then.
- Why: the owner's choice.

### D-23 · Pub workspace with `app` and `tool` members
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: 0
- Decision: the repo root is a Dart pub workspace (`taketray_workspace`) whose members are `app` (the Flutter app) and `tool` (`taketray_tool`, pure-Dart tooling such as `check_styles`). Further members (e.g. `packages/taketray_ui`) join the same list.
- Why: one lockfile and one dependency resolution for everything; one SDK constraint set; the tooling stays a separate package, so app code never depends on it and the boundaries stay clean.
- Alternatives: independent projects each with their own lockfile (versions drift, repeated setup); melos (an extra tool for what pub workspaces now do natively).
- Consequences: `pub get` runs once at the root and writes the single `pubspec.lock` there (members have none). Workspaces share dependency resolution and the lockfile, not lint configuration: each member has its own `analysis_options.yaml` including `very_good_analysis`. Every member needs `resolution: workspace` and an SDK constraint compatible with the root's.

### D-24 · very_good_analysis 11.0.0, `public_member_api_docs` off in `app` only
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: 0
- Decision: `app` and `tool` each include `package:very_good_analysis/analysis_options.yaml` (dev dependency `^11.0.0`, the version pub resolved for the pinned Dart 3.13 SDK). Generated code (`*.g.dart`, `*.freezed.dart`, `lib/l10n/gen/**`) and `build/**` are excluded. `public_member_api_docs` is disabled in `app` only, since it is application code rather than a library; it stays on in `tool`.
- Why: zero-warning quality bar (D-20) without forcing doc comments on every app widget.
- Alternatives: keep `flutter_lints` (weaker); disable more rules (erodes the bar).
- Consequences: `flutter_lints` is removed from `app`. Individual rule opt-outs need their own justification here or an inline `// ignore` with a reason.

### D-25 · Style rule enforced by a regex check over the whole source, not an analyzer plugin
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: 0 · Revised: 2026-10-25
- Decision: `tool/check_styles` (`tool/lib/src/style_rules.dart`, CLI `tool/bin/check_styles.dart`) scans `app/lib/features/**/*.dart` (skipping `*.g.dart`, `*.freezed.dart`; a missing directory is clean) with one regex per rule: `raw-color`, `raw-text-style`, `raw-insets`, `raw-size`, `raw-radius`, `raw-duration`. Comments (`//` and `/* */`, string-aware) are blanked from the whole text keeping offsets, the regexes run over the full text so calls split across lines (the shape `dart format` produces with trailing commas) are caught, and each match is reported on the line where it starts. A numeric literal counts only when it is a whole argument (`8`, `horizontal: 16.0`), so `spacing.m * 2` or `2 * tokens.y` are clean. A match is exempt when its start line, or a comment-only line directly above it, contains `// style-check: allow <reason>`; the reason is required (a bare marker does not exempt) and is reviewed like any other code. A marker on a line that also holds code exempts that line only.
- Why: the rule only needs to catch raw literals in feature code (D-19), which patterns do reliably; it runs in milliseconds, is plain Dart in the existing `tool` package, and is trivially unit-tested. The spec requires the rule to be actually enforced, so split calls must not slip through.
- Alternatives: `custom_lint` or an analyzer plugin (precise AST rules, but heavy, tied to analyzer internals that break across SDK upgrades, and slower in `tool/check`); grep in a shell script (untested, awkward allow-list).
- Consequences: argument matching tolerates one level of nested parentheses, so deeply nested expressions and literals produced by a conditional (`x ? 8 : 4`) are not seen. If misses prove common, move the rules to an analyzer plugin; the rule ids and the allow comment stay the same.

### D-26 · `tool/check` format-checks only paths that exist; app tests always run
- Date: 2026-10-24 · Phase: 0 · Revised: 2026-10-25
- Decision: `tool/check` format-checks only those of `app/lib`, `app/test`, `app/patrol_test`, `tool` that exist. `app` tests run unconditionally (`tool/flutter test` in `app/`). It still stops at the first failure and ends with `ALL CHECKS PASSED`. The on-device tests in `app/patrol_test` are format-checked and analyzed here but run only through `tool/patrol` (D-32).
- Why: the formatter fails on missing paths, which broke the check before every directory existed. Early in phase 0 the app-test step was also skipped while `app/test` held no `*_test.dart` (printing `app: no tests yet (skipped)`); now that app tests exist, a skip would only hide a deleted or emptied test directory, so it was removed.
- Alternatives: create empty placeholder test files (noise, fake coverage); keep the conditional test step (silently passes if tests disappear).
- Consequences: deleting or emptying `app/test` now fails `tool/check`.

### D-27 · gen-l10n output is generated, not committed
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 0
- Decision: copy lives in `app/lib/l10n/app_en.arb` (English only, template ARB) and `app/l10n.yaml` points gen-l10n at `lib/l10n/gen`; `flutter: generate: true` is set in `app/pubspec.yaml`. `app/lib/l10n/gen/` is git-ignored. `flutter pub get` (which `tool/check` runs first, and which every fresh checkout needs anyway) regenerates it, so `flutter analyze` and `flutter test` work straight after `pub get`; `tool/flutter gen-l10n` regenerates on demand. Plurals use ICU syntax in the ARB (`toTagBadgeSemantics`). `intl` is constrained `^0.20.2`, the range `flutter_localizations` pins.
- Why: generated code in the diff is noise and can drift from the ARB; the only extra requirement is `pub get`, which is already mandatory.
- Alternatives: commit the generated files (works with no step at all, but invites stale or hand-edited output and merge noise).
- Consequences: after editing the ARB, run `pub get` or `gen-l10n` before analyzing; the analyzer excludes `lib/l10n/gen/**` (D-24) so generated code is not linted.

### D-28 · Local error log: size-rotated file, start-up never blocked, paths redacted
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 0 · Revised: 2026-10-25, 2026-10-27 (database errors, phase 2 final review)
- Decision: `LogFileSink` (`app/lib/core/logging/log_file_sink.dart`) writes to `taketray-0.log` and rotates to `taketray-1.log` when a write would pass `maxBytes` (512 KiB), so the log is capped at about 1 MiB; writes are serialized on one future chain. `runAppWithLogging` (`logging_setup.dart`) works in this order: (1) set level INFO, subscribe to `Logger.root.onRecord`, install `FlutterError.onError` and `PlatformDispatcher.onError`; (2) enter `runZonedGuarded`, call `WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized()` inside it (so `runApp` in `body` shares the zone) and start resolving `logDir()` concurrently with a 5 s timeout; (3) run `body` immediately. Records and errors produced before the sink exists, including start-up errors thrown by `body`, are formatted and buffered in memory (max 200) and flushed in order once the sink is ready. If `logDir()` throws or times out, logging is disabled: the buffer is dropped, nothing more is buffered (`debugPrint` once, error type only) and the app carries on. A throwing `onSinkReady` callback is reported and ignored. `onSinkReady` hands out the live sink (phase 10's Settings share action uses it for `exportFile`). Console output in debug builds (`kDebugMode`): the `onRecord` listener also `debugPrint`s every formatted record (with its `error:` and `stack:` lines), so uncaught zone and `PlatformDispatcher` errors and ordinary warnings reach the console as well as the file, even when the file log is disabled; framework errors are printed once, by `FlutterError.presentError` (their record carries a private tag object and is not echoed). Release builds print nothing to the console.
- Redaction (D-10): `formatRecord` renders a `FileSystemException` as its type, message and OS error without `path`, and replaces path-like tokens in message and error text with `<path>`: quoted paths or URIs, any `scheme://...` (`content://`, `file://`), `C:\...`, and absolute `/a/b...` paths with at least two slashes. Database errors are rebuilt from their fields, never from `toString()`: `SqliteException.toString()` appends the failing statement and every value bound to it (a reel name, a label, a link, a file name), and drift's `InvalidDataException` prints the whole row. So a `SqliteException` is written as `SqliteException(<extended result code>): <SQLite's message>` (the message names only tables and columns), a `DriftRemoteException` (how errors arrive from the background database isolate on the phone) as its cause, an `InvalidDataException` as `InvalidDataException(<failing columns>)`, and drift's `CouldNotRollBackException` and `DriftWrappedException` by their causes. Any other message or error text that still contains `Causing statement` (a `SqliteException` interpolated into a string, say) is cut there and ends in `<statement removed>`. Stack traces are kept verbatim. Each record is cut at 8 KiB (`…[truncated]`) so one record cannot defeat the size cap.
- Why: crashes during start-up are exactly the ones worth keeping, but the log directory is only available asynchronously and a hung lookup must not stop the app starting. A small fixed cap keeps storage bounded without a cleanup job. Video names and locations must never be written to the log. Database errors are the one place the app's own text reaches an exception (a full phone during an insert, an I/O error), and they can escape to the log through the uncaught-error handlers or a later `_log.warning('…', e)`, so the formatter guards them in one place instead of every caller (the final review of phase 2 found a reel name in such a line).
- Alternatives: resolve the directory before running `body` (loses early errors, can hang start); a third-party crash reporter (violates fully offline); one unbounded file (grows forever).
- Consequences: the console echo uses the redacted line, so a developer sees `<path>` where a path was; debug the raw value with a breakpoint, not by logging it. The redaction is a conservative pattern scrub, not a guarantee: a bare relative file name or a path with spaces that is not quoted may still show (callers must not log them either). Sink failures are swallowed so logging can never crash the app.

### D-29 · Placeholder stock `NavigationBar` for the tab shell until phase 1
- Status: **Superseded by D-50** (phase 1 replaced the bar and the theme)
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 0
- Decision: `AppShell` (`app/lib/features/shell/views/app_shell.dart`) uses Material's stock `NavigationBar` with `Icons.*` and `AppLocalizations` labels, and a default `ThemeData(useMaterial3: true)` light/dark theme in `TakeTrayApp`. Phase 1 replaces both with the TakeTray floating nav pill and design tokens.
- Why: the router, back handling and per-tab stacks need a real shell to test now; the design system does not exist yet, and the style rule (D-19) forbids hand-picked colours, sizes and styles in feature code, which the stock widget avoids by using none.
- Alternatives considered: build the floating pill now (needs tokens that phase 1 defines); no visible navigation (cannot demo or test tab switching).
- Consequences: `AppShell`'s public shape (`navigationShell`) and the `PopScope` back behaviour stay; only the bar widget and theme change in phase 1.

### D-30 · Riverpod 3 with code generation, keep-alive router provider
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 0 · Revised: 2026-10-25
- Decision: state management is `flutter_riverpod` with `riverpod_annotation` and `riverpod_generator` (`@riverpod` functions), run through `build_runner`; generated `*.g.dart` files are committed (the analyzer already excludes them, D-24). `appRouterProvider` is `@Riverpod(keepAlive: true)` and disposes its `GoRouter` with the provider. Navigation uses `go_router` with `StatefulShellRoute.indexedStack`. Any location no route matches (an unknown top-level path such as `/nope`, or an unknown path below a tab root such as `/reels/stale` or `/to-tag/player/123`) is handled by `GoRouter.onException`, which logs a `router` warning without the location and calls `router.go('/reels')`; there is no top-level `redirect`. `riverpod_lint` is not used.
- Why: generated providers give typed, uniform declarations as features grow; keeping the router alive stops an auto-disposed provider from rebuilding the router (and losing every tab's stack) if it briefly has no listener. Committing `*.g.dart` means a fresh checkout analyzes and runs without a build step.
- Alternatives considered: hand-written providers (no codegen step, but inconsistent as the app grows); auto-dispose router (risk of lost navigation state); `riverpod_lint` (extra analyzer plugin weight); a `redirect` that allows any path under a tab root prefix (the first version: `/reels/stale` passed the prefix test, matched no route and showed go_router's "Page Not Found" page); `errorBuilder` (shows a page instead of recovering).
- Consequences: after changing an `@riverpod` declaration run `tool/dart run build_runner build --delete-conflicting-outputs` in `app/` and commit the output. A stale deep link loses its target silently (only the warning records it); when later phases add sub-routes (player, reel detail), they become reachable simply by declaring them, with no allow-list to keep in sync.

### D-31 · INTERNET stripped from release with `tools:node="remove"`, verified by a script
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 0 · Revised: 2026-10-25
- Decision: `app/android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` declares `xmlns:tools` and `<uses-permission android:name="android.permission.INTERNET" tools:node="remove"/>`, so the manifest merger removes INTERNET coming from `main` and from lower-priority manifests (plugins and libraries), which makes the release build INTERNET-free even when a plugin's manifest adds it. Debug and profile keep INTERNET: `src/debug` and `src/profile` declare their own `INTERNET` line (hot reload and the debugger need it), and build-type manifests rank above `main`, so the marker does not remove their entry (confirmed with `aapt2` on the debug APK). `tool/check_release_permissions` runs `tool/flutter build apk --release` in `app/`, runs `aapt2 dump permissions` (newest stable `build-tools/x.y.z` that has `aapt2`, found at run time under `$ANDROID_HOME` or `~/Library/Android/sdk`) on `app-release.apk`, and exits 1 with `INTERNET permission found in release build` if `android.permission.INTERNET` is listed; otherwise it prints the list and `RELEASE PERMISSIONS OK`. It is not part of `tool/check` (a release build takes minutes); run it at the end of every phase.
- Why: "fully offline" is a product promise (spec); the strongest enforceable form is that the shipped APK cannot open a socket. Checking the built APK, not the source manifests, also catches permissions merged in by a future plugin.
- Alternatives: rely on the template (INTERNET only in debug/profile), which passes today but silently breaks when a plugin merges it; inspect the merged manifest instead of the APK (does not prove what is packaged).
- Consequences: before this change the release build already had no INTERNET (recorded), so the marker is a guard rather than a fix. A new plugin that really needs the network cannot be added without removing this line, which is a deliberate, visible decision. Real release signing is still phase 11.

### D-32 · Patrol for on-device tests, run on the S24 only for now
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 0 · Revised: 2026-10-25
- Decision: on-device UI tests use Patrol (`patrol` 4.10.0 as a dev dependency, `patrol_cli` 4.8.0 installed globally), with the Android test orchestrator set up as in Patrol's guide (`PatrolJUnitRunner`, `MainActivityTest`, `androidTestUtil` orchestrator). `tool/patrol` runs the CLI with the pinned Flutter first on `PATH` and `--flutter-command`, sets `PATROL_ANALYTICS_ENABLED=false`, and adds `--no-uninstall` to `test` and `develop` unless the caller chose `--uninstall`/`--no-uninstall`. The environment variable turns analytics off only for runs through `tool/patrol`; any other run reads `~/.config/patrol_cli/analytics.json`, which each developer sets to `"enabled":false` once (`docs/dev-setup.md`). `patrol_cli` is pinned to 4.8.0 at install. Tests live in `app/patrol_test/` and run only on the owner's Samsung Galaxy S24 Ultra; the API 30, 33 and 34+ emulators are not created.
- Why: Patrol drives native UI (permission dialogs, system settings, notifications), which later phases need and `flutter test` / `integration_test` cannot do. Emulators were skipped because only about 14 GB of disk is free and each system image plus AVD costs several GB; the existing `Pixel_3a_API_32` AVD's system image is missing.
- Alternatives considered: plain `integration_test` (no native automation); free disk and create AVDs now (the owner chose not to); Firebase Test Lab or similar (sends the app off the machine, against the offline rule).
- Consequences: the app under test stays installed on the owner's phone after a run (patrol_cli's default uninstall removed it), but the orchestrator's `clearPackageData` still wipes its data, and a differently signed build must be uninstalled by hand. Analytics can still be sent by a bare `patrol` run if the home config file was never set; the repo cannot enforce that file. OS-version coverage is one device (Android 16 on One UI) until emulators exist; minSdk 30 behaviour is untested on old APIs. `patrol` is a dev dependency only, so its `http` and related transitive packages never ship in the app, and the release permission check (D-31) still guards INTERNET. Revisit when disk space allows (or before the first release, phase 11): create API 30 and 34+ AVDs and add them to the run list in `docs/dev-setup.md`.

### D-33 · Prototype app moved to `reference/`, not compiled or analysed
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 0
- Decision: the earlier prototype app (`app/lib` screens, state, widgets, sample models and its preview tests) was moved unchanged to `reference/prototype-app/` before the rebuild started. It is not part of the pub workspace, is excluded by the repo-root `analysis_options.yaml` (together with `design/**`), and is never built, analysed or tested. Its screens are layout references for phases 1–10; its widgets are re-implemented in the design system to the style rule (D-19); its sample data becomes a debug-only seed and Widgetbook fixtures. `app/assets/sample/` stays in the app for now and moves with that work in phase 1. Delete `reference/` after phase 10.
- Why: the spec ports the prototype rather than discarding it, but its code predates the architecture (D-13), the style rule and the strict lints, so compiling it alongside the new app would mean either fixing throwaway code or weakening the checks. Keeping it in the repo keeps it one click away while porting.
- Alternatives considered: delete it and rely on git history (harder to consult side by side); keep it in `app/lib` and fix it to the lints (wasted work on code that is being rewritten); a separate branch (invisible while working on the main line).
- Consequences: `reference/` code may rot against newer SDKs; that is accepted, because it is only read. An IDE opened at the repo root shows no errors from it (root `analysis_options.yaml`). Nothing in `app` or `tool` may import from `reference/`.

### D-34 · `tool/check` covers every workspace member
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 1
- Decision: `tool/check` names its members in two arrays, `FLUTTER_MEMBERS=(app packages/taketray_ui widgetbook)` and `DART_MEMBERS=(tool)`, and runs format, analyze, style rules and tests over each one (while the phase 1 tasks were still creating folders a missing member was skipped with a note; since the final review a listed member without a `pubspec.yaml`, or a missing style directory, fails the check). A guard at the start reads the root `pubspec.yaml` `workspace:` list and fails with `workspace member <x> is not covered by tool/check` when an entry is in neither array. Format covers `lib`, `test` and `patrol_test` of Flutter members and the whole Dart member; style rules run over `app/lib/features` and `packages/taketray_ui/lib/src/components`; every member must have a `test/` folder. The style rules also now flag `BorderRadiusDirectional.circular(<n>)`, `SizedBox.square(dimension: <n>)` and `Duration` in microseconds, minutes, hours and days.
- Why: in phase 0 `tool/check` hard-coded `app` and `tool`, so a new package could be added to the workspace and never be formatted, analysed or tested. Failing loudly when a member is unlisted keeps the one-command check honest. The style-rule gaps let a raw radius, square size or long duration bypass the rule.
- Alternatives considered: derive members from the workspace list automatically (cannot tell Flutter from Dart members without parsing each pubspec, and a new member would be checked without anyone deciding how); a YAML parser dependency (heavy for one list); only warn on uncovered members (easy to miss).
- Consequences: adding a workspace member means editing the arrays in `tool/check` in the same change, or `tool/check` fails. The `workspace:` parse expects the plain block list (`  - member`) used today; blank and comment lines inside the list are skipped and the list ends at the next top-level key, but a different YAML style (for example a flow list `workspace: [app, tool]`) yields no members and the guard fails with "no workspace members found" rather than passing silently. The style rules remain regex-based and catch literals in argument position only (D-19).

### D-35 · `taketray_ui` package with generated tokens for the shipped brand only, checked for freshness
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 2
- Decision: the design system lives in the workspace member `packages/taketray_ui` (dependencies `flutter` and `material_symbols_icons` 4.2960.0; dev dependencies `flutter_test`, `alchemist` 0.14.1 and `very_good_analysis` 11.0.0, the newest that resolve with Flutter 3.47.7 / Dart 3.13.5). `design/tools/build_tokens.py › write_dart` now writes only `packages/taketray_ui/lib/src/tokens/tokens.g.dart`, for the shipped brand (`SHIPPED`, Graphite & marigold): `ttLightScheme`, `ttDarkScheme` and the `abstract final class` groups `TtColorTokens` (REC red and success green, light and dark, plus the video surface), `TtTypeTokens`, `TtSpaceTokens`, `TtRadiusTokens`, `TtMotionTokens` and `TtSizeTokens`. `BrandOption` and `brandSchemes` are gone from the Dart; the JSON, CSS and JS still carry all five options for the hub. The video surface values moved from an inline literal in `write_json` into a `VIDEO_SURFACE` dict so the JSON and the Dart read one source. Every writer hands its text to `emit()`, and `main` either writes the files (unchanged behaviour) or, with `--check`, compares them with disk, prints `stale: <path>` for each difference and `orphan: <path>` for each file in a folder the script owns outright (`GENERATED_DIRS`: `design/tokens/colour-options/` and `design/handoff/compose/`) that it would not write, and exits 1 without writing when there is either. An unknown flag (or an abbreviation such as `--che`; `allow_abbrev=False`) exits 2. Contrast failures still exit 1 in both modes. (Orphans and the exit code 2 were added in Phase 2, Task 3.) `tool/check` runs `--check` right after `pub get`. `design/handoff/flutter/` is deleted; the package is the Flutter hand-off now.
- Why: one generated source for every token, consumed by one package, means the app and Widgetbook cannot drift from the design. The app never switches brand, so the other four options in Dart were dead code. A hand edit to a generated file, or a token change without a regeneration, now fails the one-command check instead of surviving until someone notices a colour.
- Alternatives considered: keep all brand options in Dart for a future switcher (no product need; easy to restore from the script); generate at build time with `build_runner` (needs Python in the Dart build, or a port of the colour maths); commit the tokens without a check (the old state: `app/lib/theme/tokens.g.dart` was silently recreated by the script after the app moved).
- Consequences: changing a token means editing `build_tokens.py`, running it, and committing the regenerated outputs together. The generator must keep producing `dart format`-clean, lint-clean Dart (the file is analysed, not excluded). `tool/check` needs `python3` on the PATH.

### D-36 · Theme architecture: one `TtThemeData` extension read through `context.tt`
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 2
- Decision: `TakeTrayTheme.light()`, `.dark()` and `.video()` return cached `ThemeData` built from the generated schemes. Each carries a single `ThemeExtension`, `TtThemeData`, which groups `colors` (`TtColors`: "clips waiting" = the scheme's tertiary roles, REC red, success green, glass and hairline, colours on media and on video), `text` (`TtTextStyles`: the Material type scale with colours applied, plus the TakeTray display styles from `tt-style.css`, which carry no colour and inherit the ambient one), `spacing`, `radii`, `motion` and `sizes`. Widgets read it with `context.tt` and the Material roles with `context.scheme`. `context.tt` returns the extension with `motion` swapped for the `const TtMotion.reduced` (all durations zero, same curves) when `MediaQuery.maybeDisableAnimationsOf` is true, so every widget that animates with theme durations honours "Remove animations" without its own check; widgets can also compare `motion == TtMotion.reduced` (value equality, and the same instance). `context.tt` throws a `FlutterError` when the theme has no `TtThemeData`, so a widget shown under a stock theme fails loudly. The dark theme's OLED override (`tt-style.css` §1: surface = surfaceContainerLowest, surfaceContainerLowest = black, containers one step down) is applied in `TakeTrayTheme`, not in the generator, so the token JSON keeps the stock Material 3 roles that pass the contrast report. `video()` is the dark theme rebuilt with `surface` = `TtColorTokens.videoBackground` (black), so its component themes follow too. The themes also set Material component themes (pill buttons with `labelLarge`, floating snackbar on `inverseSurface` at 94 % with radius 18, transparent bottom sheets without a drag handle, dialogs with radius 32, `scrolledUnderElevation: 0`, `InkSparkle`), so stock buttons, snack bars, sheets, dialogs and app bars look TakeTray. Type styles use `TextLeadingDistribution.even`, which matches how CSS centres a line box.
- Why: one extension keeps `lerp` and `copyWith` in one place and gives components one import and one accessor; resolving reduced motion in the accessor makes Review Focus 5 the default rather than something each component must remember.
- Alternatives considered: several extensions (colours, text, motion) read separately (more boilerplate per widget, easy to miss one in a theme); `InheritedWidget` for TakeTray values (duplicates what `Theme` already animates); OLED surfaces in the generated dark scheme (the JSON and CSS would disagree with stock Material 3 and the hub applies the override itself in `tt-style.css`); falling back to the light values when the extension is missing (hides a wiring mistake).
- Consequences: spacing, radii, sizes and motion are identical in every theme and switch at the midpoint in `lerp`; only colours and text interpolate. Anything that renders TakeTray widgets (app, Widgetbook, tests) must use a `TakeTrayTheme` theme. The app adopted the theme in Task 11 (D-50). Only the buttons, snack bar, bottom sheet, dialog and app bar get Material component themes; a stock `Switch`, `Checkbox`, `TextField`, progress indicator or `AlertDialog` in feature code gets Material defaults in the TakeTray colours, so feature code uses the `Tt*` components.

### D-37 · No user-facing copy in `taketray_ui`
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 2
- Decision: the package holds no strings a user sees. Components take every visible string and semantics label as a parameter; the app passes `AppLocalizations` strings, Widgetbook and tests pass English literals. The package has no l10n setup and never imports `app/`, Riverpod or go_router.
- Why: keeps the design system independent of the app's localisation and state, so it can be built, tested and catalogued on its own, and translations stay in one place (`app/lib/l10n`).
- Alternatives considered: give the package its own ARB files (two translation sources); hard-code English defaults (silently untranslated text in the app).
- Consequences: component APIs have more required `String` parameters; a missing label is a compile error rather than a fallback.

### D-38 · `TtMetrics` holds component numbers; the style rule covers components
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 2
- Decision: `packages/taketray_ui/lib/src/theme/tt_metrics.dart` defines one `abstract final class TtMetrics` with every non-token number a component or component theme needs, as `static const double`, named with the component as prefix (`glassBlur`, `snackbarRadius`, `dialogRadius`, `buttonMinHeight`…), each with a `// tt-style.css: <selector>` (or `proto.css`) comment naming its source. `tool/check_styles` already runs over `packages/taketray_ui/lib/src/components` (D-34), so components may use only `context.tt`, `context.scheme` and `TtMetrics`. The theme files themselves (`lib/src/theme`) are outside the rule: they are where tokens become values.
- Why: the TakeTray style adds many one-off numbers on top of the tokens; one named, sourced home keeps them reviewable against the CSS and stops raw literals from spreading across widgets.
- Alternatives considered: per-component private constants (not checked, harder to compare with the CSS); promoting each number to a design token (the hub would need them in the generator too, for values used once).
- Consequences: adding a component number means editing `tt_metrics.dart` with its CSS source. Opacities used to build `TtColors` (glass 78 %, hairline 9 %, on-media 82 %, scrim 80 %) live there too.

### D-39 · Goldens with alchemist: CI and macOS images, light and dark at 1.0× and 2.0×
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 2
- Decision: `packages/taketray_ui/test/flutter_test_config.dart` configures alchemist for every test: CI goldens (text drawn as blocks, `goldens/ci/`) and platform goldens (real text, `goldens/macos/`, macOS only). **Superseded by D-61 for which set runs where:** the CI goldens run only when the `CI` environment variable is set, the macOS goldens only when it is not (this sentence first said the CI goldens were always on). The same config loads the package's own fonts under `packages/taketray_ui/<family>`, because the test FontManifest lists them under the bare family while the theme names them with `package: 'taketray_ui'`; alchemist loads the bare and dependency-package names (including Material Symbols). The test helper `ttGoldenTest(description, fileName:, scenarios:, width: 360)` registers two golden tests and writes `<fileName>_light.png` and `_dark.png`, each one `GoldenTestGroup` with every `TtScenario` at text scale 1.0 and 2.0 side by side, on the theme's surface with labels in its colours. Before the shot it decodes every `Image` for real (`runAsync` + `precacheImage`, decode errors left to the widget's error builder), then settles; an optional `pumpBeforeTest` replaces that for a widget that never settles. Other helpers: `ttTestApp` (theme, text scale, "remove animations"), `expectTtAccessible` (`androidTapTargetGuideline`, `labeledTapTargetGuideline`, `textContrastGuideline`; **superseded by D-62:** text contrast is `ttTextContrastGuideline` everywhere, plus `textContrastGuideline` on macOS only), `fixtureThumb(1..6)` and `brokenImage`.
- Why: macOS images with real text are what a reviewer compares with the prototype; CI images avoid real text, so they need no font rendering of the Mac (D-61 found that they still differ between a Mac and Linux). One image per theme with both text scales makes the 2.0× check (spec §7 rule 2) part of every review without doubling the files.
- Alternatives considered: `golden_toolkit` (discontinued); plain `matchesGoldenFile` (no scenario grid, no CI/platform split); one image per scale (four files per component).
- Consequences: goldens are regenerated with `tool/flutter test --update-goldens` in the package; macOS images differ by OS version and must be regenerated on the owner's Mac. `test/failures/` stays ignored.

### D-40 · Font and icons are packaged in `taketray_ui`
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 2
- Decision: Plus Jakarta Sans (variable, with its OFL licence) moved from `app/assets/fonts/` to `packages/taketray_ui/fonts/` and is declared in the package pubspec as family `PlusJakartaSans`; styles reference it with `package: 'taketray_ui'` (resolved family `packages/taketray_ui/PlusJakartaSans`) and set weight on both `fontWeight` and `FontVariation('wght', w)`. Icons come from `material_symbols_icons` (Rounded), bundled fonts. The app pubspec no longer declares `fonts:`, `assets:` or `cupertino_icons`; it depends on `taketray_ui` by path.
- Why: the font belongs with the type tokens that name it, and Widgetbook and the package's own goldens need it without the app. Everything stays offline (no `google_fonts`).
- Alternatives considered: keep the font in the app (the package's goldens and Widgetbook could not render it); `google_fonts` (fetches at runtime, against the offline rule).
- Consequences: any text style written outside the generated tokens must also pass `package: 'taketray_ui'` (use `TtTypeTokens.fontFamily` and `fontPackage`).

### D-41 · Sample assets leave the app
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 2
- Decision: `app/assets/sample/` (sample JSON and 132 thumbs) moved to `reference/prototype-app/assets/sample/`, next to the prototype code that reads it; six thumbs (`c1`–`c6.jpg`) are copied to `packages/taketray_ui/test/fixtures/thumbs/` for tests and Widgetbook. This completes the move D-33 deferred to phase 1.
- Why: the shipped app must not bundle demo media; tests need a few real images without the asset bundle.
- Alternatives considered: keep them in the app behind a debug flag (still bundled in release); delete them (the prototype and fixtures need them).
- Consequences: the app has no assets of its own. A debug seed, if one is built later, reads its data from somewhere other than `app/assets/sample/`.

### D-42 · Widgetbook catalogue: hand-written, coverage-tested, its own app ID
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 3
- Decision: `widgetbook/` is a third pub-workspace member and an Android-only Flutter app (`app.taketray.widgetbook`, label "TakeTray UI", `minSdk` 30 like the app), on `widgetbook` 3.25.0 (newest that resolves with Flutter 3.47.7 / Dart 3.13.5). The catalogue is written by hand, with no `widgetbook_generator`, `build_runner` or annotations: `lib/catalog.dart` holds `ttCatalog` (a `List<WidgetbookNode>` folder tree) and imports one file per component from `lib/components/<name>.dart`, each exposing a `WidgetbookComponent`. `lib/main.dart` uses `Widgetbook.material` with add-ons: `MaterialThemeAddon` ("TakeTray light", "TakeTray dark", "Video"), `TextScaleAddon` (slider with stops 1.0, 1.5, 2.0) and `ViewportAddon` (one 360 x 780 dp phone). `wbThumb(1..12)` (in `lib/fixtures.dart`, re-exported by `catalog.dart`) gives `AssetImage`s from `widgetbook/assets/thumbs/` (copies of `c1`-`c12.jpg`). `widgetbook/test/catalog_coverage_test.dart` fails when a `packages/taketray_ui/lib/src/components/<name>.dart` has no `widgetbook/lib/components/<name>.dart` or `catalog.dart` does not import it; it passes while the package folder is missing or empty. `lib/components/theme.dart` (colour roles and text styles) has no package counterpart, which is allowed. `tool/check` formats, analyses and tests the member. The package also gets `dart_test.yaml` declaring the `golden` tag (Task 2 review ruling R3), which removes the "tag was used" warning.
- Why: the generator needs `build_runner` and annotations on every use case for little gain at this size; a plain list is readable, and the coverage test supplies the one guarantee the generator would (nothing is forgotten). A separate application ID lets the catalogue install next to the app on the phone.
- Alternatives considered: `widgetbook_generator` (extra codegen step and a second `build_runner` target); catalogue inside `app/` (ships catalogue code and assets in the product); no coverage test (components silently missing from the catalogue).
- Consequences: a component task adds its catalogue file and registers it in `catalog.dart` in the same commit, or `tool/check` fails. The Android folder is a plain `flutter create` result minus platform folders other than `android`; the Gradle wrapper jar is not committed (ignored like in `app/`).

### D-43 · Core controls: Material buttons restyled, `filled` means the glyph, the circle icon button is solid, the record dot owns its ticker
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 4
- Decision: `TtButton` wraps `FilledButton` (filled, tonal, danger, attention), `OutlinedButton` (outlined, ghost) and `TextButton` (text) with a `ButtonStyle` built from the scheme and `TtMetrics`, so ripple, focus and semantics stay Material's; every size sets `MaterialTapTargetSize.padded`, which keeps a 48 x 48 dp target around the 36 dp small button. The kinds follow `tt-style.css`: tonal is `surfaceContainerHighest` on `onSurface`, outlined is `onSurface` with an `outlineVariant` border, attention is `waitingContainer`, and ghost (from `.qrow .btn.ghost`) is white on a 35 % white border for use on video. Disabled buttons use `onSurface` at 12 % (fill, border) and 38 % (content), white in place of `onSurface` for ghost. `TtIconButton.filled` draws the glyph with `fill: 1` (the `fill` option of `h.ib`, the selected look), not a filled container; `pressed` non-null adds `toggled` semantics and the primary colour. The `circle` icon button is a solid `surfaceContainerHigh` circle inset 5 dp in the 48 dp target, with no blur, exactly the prototype's `.appbar:not(.sel) .ib::before` (changed from a blurred glass circle in Task 11, D-50). `TtGlass` ignores the prototype's `saturate(1.4)`, which Flutter's backdrop blur has no equivalent for. `TtRecDot` creates its `AnimationController` once and calls `repeat()` only while `live` and motion is not reduced, and `stop()` otherwise, so under "remove animations" no ticker runs; it then shows the static 3 dp ring. `TtCountBadge` keeps the style's 2 dp ring in `surfaceContainerHigh`, and hides itself at 0 or below. The barrel re-exports `material_symbols_icons/symbols.dart`; the Rounded style is `Symbols.<name>_rounded`.
- Why: Material buttons already provide the tap target, focus and ripple behaviour that accessibility checks expect, and only their look differs. Reading `filled` as the glyph matches both the prototype helper and the global rule that selected icons use `fill: 1`.
- Alternatives considered: hand-built `InkWell` buttons (re-implementing the tap target and semantics); `filled` as a primary container behind the icon (`.ib.filled`, which the FAB and filled buttons already cover); a blurred glass circle for the circle icon button (made real glass like the nav pill; replaced by the prototype's solid circle in Task 11, D-50).
- Consequences: new numbers for these controls are in `TtMetrics` with their CSS sources. The catalogue test `widgetbook/test/catalog_use_cases_test.dart` opens every use case, so later component tasks get a render check from registering their use cases.

### D-44 · Selection and input: a custom switch, notched text field, 48 dp chips, no switch role
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 5
- Decision: `TtSwitch` is the prototype's own switch (52 x 32 track, 16 dp thumb that grows to 24 dp with a check), not Material's `Switch`. It is an `InkResponse` inside a 52 x 48 dp target and moves its thumb with `context.tt.motion.short4`, so it jumps under "remove animations". It is announced with `toggled` semantics and its label (this Flutter has no `SemanticsRole.switchRole`). `inverted` is `.sw.inv`: white outline on a 20 % white track, a white thumb, and when on a white track with a black thumb (`videoBackground`) and a white check. Disabled follows the buttons: 12 % `onSurface` for the track and edge, 38 % for the off thumb. `TtCheckMark` and `TtRadioMark` are `ExcludeSemantics` visuals; the row that holds them owns the tap and semantics. `TtPillTabs` is a horizontal `SingleChildScrollView` of 36 dp (more at large text) stadium tabs, each in a 48 x 48 dp minimum tap target and announced as a button with `selected` set only on the active tab. `TtSuggestionChip` has the prototype's plus icon and a dashed 1.5 dp outline drawn by a painter (Flutter has no dashed border), with the dash 4.5 and gap 3 chosen to look like the browser's. `TtTextField` is a Material `TextField` with `floatingLabelBehavior: always`, which gives the prototype's label notched into the top border, a 12 dp outline (1 dp at rest, 2 dp primary when focused, 2 dp error in error), and a custom help row under it. `maxLength` uses `TextField.maxLength` with Material's counter hidden (`counterText: ''`) and the help row's own `n/max` counter; an `error` replaces the helper with an error icon and the text, announced as a live region. Material's own `errorText` is not used because it would add its own helper row and spacing. `TtSuggestionChip` and the pill tabs live in `pill_tabs.dart`, sharing the 48 dp tap-target wrapper.
- Why: the switch has to look like the prototype's and sit on the black card in the Shooting mode; Material's `Switch` has neither the check thumb nor the inverted colours. Reusing `TextField` keeps the keyboard, selection, autofill and IME behaviour, and only the decoration is ours.
- Alternatives considered: a label drawn above the box rather than on its edge (the prototype's `.tf label` is notched into the border); Material `errorText` and `counter` (extra rows, different spacing); a dashed outline from a package (a dependency for one border).
- Consequences: a row using the marks must give the label, the `selected` or `checked` flag and the tap itself. A very short tab ("All") is padded to 48 dp wide so its target is large enough, which widens the gap by about 1 dp. Tabs with the same value would all show as selected.

### D-45 · Clips and status: one pill for surface and media, a tile that owns its semantics, a shimmer that stops
- Date: 2026-10-25 · Phase: 1 · Task: 6
- Decision: `TtStatusPill` draws both looks itself (surface container colours, or dark glass with the `*OnMedia` icon colour) rather than reusing `TtGlassPill`, because the status pill also needs the large size, the caret and a button role; `onPressed` adds the caret and a 48 dp tap target (the pill stays 24 or 32 dp tall inside it, like the chips in D-44). The labels are the new "Collecting" / "Ready to edit" / "Published" passed in by the caller. `TtClipTile` is announced as one button named by `semanticLabel` (the badges, time and mark are excluded from the tree, so the caller's label must mention them); `selected` non-null gives `toggled` semantics (the `aria-pressed` of the prototype, as in `TtIconButton.pressed`) and the check circle, which is its own widget (not `TtCheckMark`: it is round, white-ringed and on media). The picture scales to 86 % with `motion.short4`, so it jumps under "remove animations". The time and length share the row in proportion to their length and shrink (`FittedBox`) at large text, rather than being cut off; the VN mark sits in the same column above them, so it never overlaps them. A disabled tile has no visual change (the CSS has none; it is announced disabled and ignores taps), and the broken-image placeholder is `surfaceContainerHighest` with `movie`. `TtMissingClipTile` draws its dashed 1 dp border with a painter and shows its label on two lines, one at text scale above 1.15, so the 3:4 tile does not overflow; its Remove button is the shared small tonal `TtButton` (36 dp, 48 dp target) rather than the prototype's 32 dp one. `TtClipTileSkeleton` repeats the CSS shimmer (100 degree band on a 220 % layer sliding every 1.4 s) with a `CustomPainter` driven by a controller that is stopped under reduced motion, leaving the plain tile; `TtSkeletonLine` is still. Goldens of the skeleton pump a fixed 0.4 s instead of settling. Nothing in these components uses `LayoutBuilder` or `WidgetSpan`, which break the intrinsic-size queries of the golden harness (and of any `IntrinsicHeight` parent).
- Why: the clip grid is the core of the app, so the tile has to be one clear control for a screen reader, readable at 2x text and cheap to scroll.
- Alternatives considered: wrapping `TtGlassPill` (no large size or caret); scaling the whole missing-tile content to fit (needs `LayoutBuilder`); a dimmed disabled tile (not in the prototype); `selected` semantics instead of `toggled` (the other toggles use `toggled`).
- Consequences: Task 7's reel card reuses `TtStatusKind` and `TtStatusPill(onMedia: true)`. `TtDayHeader`'s trailing text should be short (it does not shrink).

### D-46 · Cards: the covers own their pills, one row widget with two looks, a recoloured camera button
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 1 · Task: 7
- Decision: `TtReelCard` is a stateful widget that listens to its cover's image stream, so a cover that fails to load switches the whole card (picture, gradient and text colours) to the empty art, not only the picture. The status pill and the "Editing in VN" pill are laid out in one column at the top start (8 dp apart), and the caption is in flow under the empty art rather than absolutely placed, so at large text the pills never overlap each other and the empty art shrinks (`FittedBox`) instead of running into the name. At 1x the geometry matches the prototype's (the 48 dp bottom padding of `.ph-empty` is the height of a one-line caption); at larger text it differs on purpose, since the prototype has no text scaling. The references badge is a separate sibling button on top of the card, with a 48 dp tall target that reaches 8 dp sideways past the pill (`.refbadge::before`), so tapping it never reaches the card's `onTap`; the card's name and meta keep 50 dp free at the end while it shows. On the empty art the "N missing" mark uses the scheme's error colour, not the light red `#FFB4AB` that `tt-style.css` pins (which has too little contrast on a light card). `TtCardRow` is one widget with two looks chosen by `trailingText`: without it a references row (frosted black band, white text, chevron), with it a to-tag strip (`waitingContainer` band, arrow, filled icon); the brief's signature has no variant parameter. The camera button on the "on" card is a `TtButton` wrapped in a `Theme` whose scheme has `primary` white and `onPrimary` black (`videoBackground`, where the CSS has `#111`), so `TtButton` needs no extra kind. `TtRecDot` gains `compact` (8 dp, the stat strip) and `outline` (a 2 dp ring in a given colour, painted over the start of the pulse, as `pulseOn` does). The reference card's more button is the shared 48 dp `TtIconButton` (the CSS has 44 dp wide), and its "Open" mark is capped at 120 dp so a long label cannot squeeze the title away. The "on" card's tint is a painter that reproduces the CSS 160 degree gradient line (its length depends on the box), brand colour `primary` in light and `primaryContainer` in dark.
- Why: the Home screen is the first thing seen, so the cards must stay legible at 2x text and never throw on a bad image, and a badge inside a button must not fire both actions.
- Text scale (fix round 1): a reel card clamps its own text scaling to 1.6 (`TtMetrics.reelCardMaxTextScale`, `MediaQuery.withClampedTextScaling` around the pills, caption, badge and empty art), because the 4:5 tile has a fixed size and unbounded text put the pills over the name (the prototype has no text scaling, so this is not CSS behaviour; the clamp does not apply at or below 1.6x, so 1x is not affected by it). 1.6 is the largest 0.1 step at which the worst case in the 158 dp tile (two-line name, Editing in VN, a missing mark, a two-digit badge) keeps the name below both pills; at 1.7 the name starts under the second pill. The caption's end inset is the larger of 50 dp and the badge's real width plus its 10 dp inset, measured with the clamped scale, so the growing count never meets the meta (at 1x the badge is 40 dp wide and the inset stays 50). Text above 1.6x on these tiles does not grow further; the other cards still follow the system scale.
- Alternatives considered: an extra `variant` parameter on `TtCardRow` (breaks the binding signature); a new `TtButtonKind` for the white button (touches every `switch` over the kinds for one use); absolute positioning of the caption as in the CSS (overlaps the empty art at large text).
- Consequences: the Home screen composes `TtShootingModeCard` with `TtCardRow` children, and a stat strip's leading dot is `TtRecDot(compact: true)`.

### D-47 · Tag player chrome: chips grow with the text, a 48 dp scrub row, a drawn panel highlight
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 1 · Task: 8
- Amended by D-93: `TtTagPanel` is a semantics group with slots for an empty message, the Other reels toggle and folded chips, and takes a required `semanticLabel`; the chip's 164 dp 2x scenarios are replaced by 336 dp ones.
- Amended by D-92: the scrub bar is still 48 dp to touch but draws a 22 dp row (the CSS `.scrub`) with the thumb inside the gesture area; it is a slider with a name, a value ("1:12 of 4:32") and values for a step, all from the caller; the video scrims are the prototype's (top 0.6, bottom 0.78 with a solid lower part), not the first tokens'.
- Decision: `TtVideoSurface` wraps its subtree in `TakeTrayTheme.video()` on a black `Material`, so every piece of the player is dark whatever the app theme. `TtReelChip` is at least `size.reelChip` (56 dp) high and 58 dp at 1x (44 dp cover + 6 dp padding + 1 dp border, as in the CSS, whose `min-height: 56px` is border-box); it does not clamp its text scale. The cover stays 34 x 44 and centred while the chip grows with the text, name and count are one line each with an ellipsis at normal text (the name may take two lines from 1.5x), and a geometry test at 2x asserts that nothing overlaps the cover or the check mark. `TtScrubBar` is a fixed 48 dp row (the CSS bar is 22 dp) whose gesture area is the whole track row, so a tap 20 dp above the line still seeks; it is a slider with its two times as label and steps of 5 % for screen readers. `TtSegmentBar` leaves out segments of width zero (first and last clip above 40) and clamps `current`. `TtTagPanel` lays out its chips two to a row with `Expanded` (one per row from 1.5x text, see "Large text" below), scrolls them under a 340 dp cap (larger at large text), and draws the CSS `inset 0 1px 0` highlight as a painter (the panel's shape minus itself moved down 1 dp), because a Flutter border cannot be 1 dp on the top only with a radius. `TtSameAsPreviousButton` dims its fill and content when disabled (the prototype's `.btn.dis` rule loses to `.qrow .btn.same`, so the disabled button looks enabled there).
- Why: the tag player is the app's core loop, so a chip must stay easy to hit and readable at large text; a clamp at 1.2x (the most that two lines fit in 44 dp) would defeat large text where nothing needs to be fixed, because the text sits beside the cover and not over it. A 22 dp bar is too hard to scrub with a thumb.
- Alternatives considered: clamping the chip text like the reel card (D-46: it works there because the text sits over the cover); a fixed 56 dp chip with a 1.2x clamp; the 22 dp scrub bar with a larger hit slop (Flutter does not hit-test outside a box's bounds); `SliverGrid` for the panel (needs a known row height, which grows with the text); a `Border` for the highlight.
- Large text (fix round 1): from a text scale of 1.5 (`TtMetrics.tagPanelSingleColumnTextScale`, read from `MediaQuery.textScalerOf(context).scale(1)` by both the panel and the chip) `TtTagPanel` lays the chips out one per row, full width (336 dp at 360 dp), and a chip name may take two lines; the panel's cap grows from 340 dp by the text scale up to `tagPanelMaxHeightLarge` (520 dp, which shows three chips with two-line names under the header on a 780 dp screen). At 2x a 164 dp cell showed about 5 letters of a name (3 in multi-select), so names that share a first word ("Lisbon trip" and "Lisbon food") could not be told apart; the full row shows about 12 letters, or two lines. Not in the prototype (it has no text scaling); 1x is unchanged (two columns, one line, 340 dp).
- Consequences: the player screen has 26 dp more height in the scrub row than the prototype and can trim the quick row padding to match. The panel has no full-width cell yet. The prototype's "Other reels" label, the empty-state text and the dashed "New reel" chip are composed in phase 5 (tagging) from existing primitives, with goldens there (R8); they are not part of phase 1. Below 1.5x text a 164 dp chip shows a name on one line with an ellipsis, as in the CSS.

### D-48 · Overlays: drawn panels on transparent routes, a button row that stacks, a menu panel inside `MenuAnchor`
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 1 · Task: 9
- Amended by D-92: the snack casts the CSS `.snack` shadow, `--elev-3` (the sentence below about what the snack leaves out concerns the blur only).
- Decision: `TtSheet`, `TtDialog` and `TtSnack` draw their own panels (fill, 32/32/18 dp corners, hairline, the CSS `0 20px 50px -10px` shadow on the sheet and dialog) and their helpers put them on transparent routes. `showTtSheet` is a `ModalBottomSheetRoute` and `showTtDialog` a `DialogRoute` laid out as `.dlg-wrap` (centred, 24 dp in, lifted above the keyboard), both with a scrim of `scheme.scrim` at 50 % blurred by 3 dp (`.scrim`, through an overridden `buildModalBarrier`). A sheet can be dragged away exactly when it is dismissible, full screen or not. `TtSheet` adds the keyboard height under itself, so a sheet with a field sits 8 dp above the keyboard. `showTtSnack` hides the current snack bar first, lasts 4 s, or 6 s when it has an action (`app.js` `snack()`: `opts.undo ? 6000 : 4000`, time to undo a bulk action; `TtMetrics.snackDurationMillis` and `snackDurationWithActionMillis`, because `SnackBarThemeData` has no duration), closes with reason `action` when the action is tapped, ignores taps after the first, and keeps an action snack on screen while a screen reader is on (`persist`), because the action lives in `TtSnack` and not in `SnackBar.action`. Its 56 dp height with an action matches the CSS: the snack's 6 dp padding gives back the 2 dp the action's 48 dp tap target adds above and under its 44 dp pill. The snack leaves out the prototype's 12 px backdrop blur (6 % of the page shows through the 94 % fill, so it is barely visible) and moves the action colour 15 % from `inversePrimary` towards `onInverseSurface`, because `inversePrimary` measured 4.41:1 in the dark theme, under the 4.5:1 guideline. `TtButtonRow` (new, in `buttons.dart`) lays equal-width buttons side by side 8 dp apart and stacks them at full width when one label does not fit its share on one line; the dialog uses it for its actions and sheets use it for their footers. It is a small render object that measures each button's real intrinsic width, rather than a `LayoutBuilder`, so it works under intrinsic size queries (the golden table makes them). Dialog actions use a new `TtButtonSize.dialog`: 48 dp high with 12 dp sides (`.dlg .acts .btn`), and a label that wraps to a second line instead of ending in an ellipsis. `TtMenuButton` gives `MenuAnchor` one child, an internal `TtMenuPanel` that draws the surface, hairline, 6 dp padding, the 200 to 300 dp width and the items. `MenuAnchor`'s own surface is transparent and only casts the shadow (elevation 6 in the 20 dp shape), because its scroll view clips whatever the panel draws outside itself.
- Why: the prototype's overlays float with a hairline and blur (tt-style.css), which Material's sheet, dialog and snack bar do not draw. With the 22 dp sides of a standard button, prototype pairs such as "Keep copying" / "Stop" and "Not yet" / "Delete copies" would stack at 1x where the prototype keeps them side by side. At 2x a side-by-side footer cut "Cancel" to "Canc…" (the large-text rule from Task 8). The menu golden draws `TtMenuPanel` directly because alchemist's blocked-text (CI) variant cannot paint a golden that contains an open overlay: it repaints the tree itself and hits stale compositing bits.
- Alternatives considered: Material's `Dialog` with transparent colours (needs `Colors.transparent`, which the style rule forbids, and its 280 dp minimum width); a `LayoutBuilder` with a `TextPainter` estimate for the actions (throws under intrinsic queries and ignores icons and minimum sizes); stacking above a fixed text scale (stacks labels that still fit, or keeps labels side by side that do not); `SnackBar.action` (draws its own button, not `TtButton`); a `BoxShadow` on the menu panel (clipped by `MenuAnchor`'s scroll view).
- Consequences: a sheet's body must scroll itself (`ListView(shrinkWrap: true)`). The prototype's dialog icon (`.dico`, centred above a centred title) and sheet subtitle (`.sh-head p`) are not in the API yet; phases that need them add them. The menu shadow is Material elevation 6, close to but not exactly the CSS `0 12px 30px -6px`. A full-screen sheet starts below the status bar, so the dimmed page shows above it.

### D-49 · Lists and feedback: one Material per row, outer margins on banners, a sliding indeterminate bar, drawn illustrations
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 1 · Task: 10
- Decision: `TtGroupedRows` puts each child on its own `Material` (fill `surfaceContainerLow`, 22 dp outer and 6 dp inner corners, 2 dp apart, inset by the 16 dp gutter), so a row's ripple follows its corners and a group of one row is a 22 dp card. `TtListRow` is at least 56 dp and grows with its text (title and subtitle wrap); a tappable row is one button, and `semanticLabel` replaces the spoken text only on a tappable row (on a plain row it would hide a trailing switch or button). With `stackTrailingAtLargeText: true` the trailing widget moves under the subtitle from a text scale of 1.5 (`TtMetrics.listRowStackTrailingTextScale`), because beside a value "Notifications" broke inside the word at 2x; the call site sets it for a value, a value with a chevron or a button and never for a switch (an explicit flag, not a type check, so wrapped trailing widgets work). `TtRowIcon` draws a boxed icon filled and a bare one plain, following how the prototype uses them. `TtBanner` and `TtInfoBar` include the prototype's outer margin (16 dp sides, 4 dp above and 8 dp below a banner; 8 dp below an info bar) while `TtCallout` has none (the prototype's `.callout` has none), so screens compose them as the prototype does. A text-style `TtButton` given as the action of a warning, info or attention banner (and of a warning callout) takes the banner's text colour and an underline in that colour through a `Theme` copy (`primary` replaced, and a `TtThemeData` whose `labelLarge` is underlined), as the CSS's `.banner .btn.text { color: inherit; text-decoration: underline }`, so `TtButton` keeps its API; other button kinds are not underlined and the VN banner is not (`.banner.vn .btn.text`). Flutter has no `text-underline-offset: 3px`, so the underline sits at its default distance. The CSS has no rule for a callout's action (it stays primary, not underlined); the warning callout's action takes the container text colour for contrast and is underlined so that it is not told apart by colour alone. The VN banner always puts its action under the text, indented 36 dp; the other kinds do so from a text scale of 1.5 (`bannerActionBelowTextScale`), where a beside-the-text action squeezed the text. The `warning` banner is a live region (`role=alert`); a tappable banner shows a chevron and is one button. Banner and callout icons are drawn filled (the majority in the prototype). `TtProgressBar` fills with a linear `medium2` tween (jumps under "remove animations") and exposes its percentage as the semantics value; its label sits above the bar in title-medium (the copy screen's `.t-tm` line). The prototype's `.prog` has no indeterminate look, so the indeterminate bar is a 40 % bar that slides across the track in 1.4 s (the shimmer's pace), clipped to the track, and stands still in the middle under "remove animations". `TtIllustration` paints the `h.illus` geometry 1:1 with a `CustomPainter` (viewBox 168 x 140), the SVG path arcs translated to `Path.arcTo`, round caps and joins where the SVG has them, colours from scheme roles and `TtColors` (`--x-success-color` to `success`, `--x-record-color` to `record`, `--md-outline` to `outline`, and so on); it is hidden from screen readers. `TtEmptyState` keeps the CSS spacing (40/32/24 padding, 8 dp gap, 12 dp under the illustration and the body, body at most 280 dp), so an empty actions column still adds its 8 dp gap, as in the CSS.
- Why: these are the screens' scaffolding, so they must keep the prototype's look at 1x and stay readable at 2x text.
- Alternatives considered: an `InkWell` per row inside one clipped column (the ripple would cross the 2 dp gaps); `LayoutBuilder` for the indeterminate bar (breaks the golden harness's intrinsic queries; `Align` with fractional widths does not); a `CircularProgress`-style spinner; an extra underline parameter on `TtButton`.
- Consequences: a bold span in `TtCallout.richBody` must set both `fontWeight` and `FontVariation('wght', 700)` (the font is variable). Button labels in an empty state still end in an ellipsis at 2x when one does not fit in 296 dp (`TtButton` labels are one line); a later task can let `TtButton` labels wrap at large text.

### D-50 · The app adopts the theme: a floating nav pill replaces the stock bar, `reservedHeight` is the bottom-padding contract, one large-text threshold
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 1 · Task: 11
- Decision: `TakeTrayApp` uses `TakeTrayTheme.light()` and `.dark()` and leaves `themeMode` at its default (`ThemeMode.system`). `AppShell` is `Scaffold(extendBody: true, bottomNavigationBar: TtNavPill(...))` with the same `PopScope` and `goBranch(target, initialLocation: target == index)` as before; the placeholder screens show a large `TtAppBar`. `TtNavPill` is a `TtGlass` pill (64 dp, radius 32, 6 dp padding, 4 dp gaps, 20 dp from the sides, 10 dp above the safe area) with one equal-width item per `TtNavItem`; the selected item has a `primaryContainer` fill (radius 26, `motion.short4`) and a `fill: 1` icon, a `TtCountBadge` hangs off the icon's top right (`left: 50% + 4`, `top: -4`), and each item is a button announced with its label (or `badgeSemanticLabel` while the count is above 0) and `selected`. `TtAppBar` is 64 dp (80 dp large) with `screenTitle` (`pageTitle` large), an optional subtitle, `leading` and `actions`; `selection` gives the `secondaryContainer` bar with a one-line `titleMedium` title. Icon buttons in an app bar use the `circle` style; in selection mode an internal `TtBarScope` makes them circle-less and `onSecondaryContainer` (`.appbar:not(.sel) .ib::before`, `.appbar.sel .ib`). Both draw their text at no more than `TtMetrics.appBarMaxTextScale` / `navPillMaxTextScale` (1.5), because they are fixed-height chrome. `TtNavPill.reservedHeight(context)` is `64 + 10 + bottom padding`; because `extendBody` adds the pill's height to the body's `MediaQuery` padding, `AppShell` wraps the body in a `MediaQuery` that restores the device's plain bottom inset, so tab screens add `reservedHeight` once. Three carried rulings land here: R5 the circle icon button (formerly `glass`) is the prototype's solid circle (D-43 updated); R6 `TtCountBadge` clamps its own text scale at `TtMetrics.badgeMaxTextScale` (1.3); R10 `TtButton` wraps its label to two lines from `TtMetrics.largeTextScale` (1.5), the one shared large-text threshold, which `tagPanelSingleColumnTextScale`, `listRowStackTrailingTextScale` and `bannerActionBelowTextScale` now equal (the first stays as an alias). A stacked `TtButtonRow` button wraps the same way; below 1.5 nothing changes.
- Why: the nav pill and app bar are the shell of every screen, so the app has to use the design system's theme and chrome before feature phases build on it; the prototype is the visual source of truth for the glass circle; a fixed-height bar cannot grow with the text, so clamping its text is the one way to keep it inside its height, and the full title is still announced.
- Alternatives considered: leaving `extendBody` off (the screen would stop at the pill and the glass would blur only a flat surface); letting the pill and bar grow with text (their heights are part of the layout contract and of `reservedHeight`); a separate threshold per component (they would switch at different scales); an app bar that decides circles from a parameter on each button (the caller would have to remember it in selection mode).
- Consequences: a long title in a bar ends in an ellipsis at large text (it is clamped, not wrapped). A phase-5 tab screen scrolls with `bottom: TtNavPill.reservedHeight(context)` of padding. The To tag count is a constant 0 in `AppShell` until phase 5. D-29 is superseded. The `TtButton` change replaces the "labels end in an ellipsis at 2x" consequence of D-49.

### D-51 · System bar icons follow the bar's background; Android back closes an open menu first
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 1 · Final review (I-1, Minor 1)
- Decision: `TtAppBar` and `TtVideoSurface` wrap themselves in an `AnnotatedRegion<SystemUiOverlayStyle>` built by the internal `ttSystemUiStyle(background)`. It picks `SystemUiOverlayStyle.dark` (dark icons) for a light background and `.light` for a dark one with `ThemeData.estimateBrightnessForColor`, so the app bar uses its own background (`surface`, `secondaryContainer` in selection mode) and the video surface, which is black, always gets light icons. It also sets `systemNavigationBarIconBrightness` the same way (the stock constants assume a black navigation bar) and makes the status bar, the navigation bar and its divider transparent with `background.withValues(alpha: 0)`, which keeps the style rule's ban on raw colours. `TtMenuButton` is now stateful: while its menu is open a `PopScope(canPop: false)` turns Android back into `MenuController.close`, so back closes the menu and the page underneath stays.
- Why: the app is edge to edge and its theme leaves `windowLightStatusBar` false, so Android draws white status bar icons; Material's `AppBar` fixes that from its background, but `TtAppBar` replaces it and did not, which would leave the clock and battery white on the near-white light surface on every screen. The tag player and clip viewer need light icons on black whatever the app theme. For the menu, `MenuAnchor` closes only on Escape, and users expect back to dismiss a popup before leaving the screen.
- Alternatives considered: one `SystemChrome.setSystemUIOverlayStyle` call in the app (does not follow the screen: the video surface needs the opposite of the app bar, and it must change when the theme does); `BackButtonListener` for the menu (asserts without an ancestor `Router`, so it would break plain-`MaterialApp` hosts such as Widgetbook and the package tests); `NavigatorPopHandler` (made for nested navigators).
- Consequences: a screen that has neither a `TtAppBar` nor a `TtVideoSurface` sets no style and keeps Android's default icons; it should wrap its body in `AnnotatedRegion` with `ttSystemUiStyle`-equivalent values, or use one of the two. Flutter reads the region at the middle of the status bar and the navigation bar; if a second region sits at the bottom (not today), it supplies the navigation bar properties. Unit tests read the region's value in light, dark, selection mode and on the video surface; whether the icons are really readable on the S24 Ultra is part of the owner check (Task 12 step 4).

### D-52 · Debug builds and Patrol are a separate app: `app.taketray.dev`, "TakeTray Dev"
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 2 · Task: 1
- Decision: the `debug` build type in `app/android/app/build.gradle.kts` has `applicationIdSuffix = ".dev"` and `manifestPlaceholders["appLabel"] = "TakeTray Dev"`; the manifest's `android:label` is `${appLabel}`. The default placeholder is "TakeTray" and is set in `defaultConfig`, not on `release`, because Flutter's `profile` build type does not inherit release-only placeholders (the profile build fails with an unresolved `${appLabel}`), so release and profile builds keep the id `app.taketray` and the label "TakeTray". The `namespace` stays `app.taketray`, so Kotlin packages and `R` do not change. The `patrol:` block in `app/pubspec.yaml` has `android.package_name: app.taketray.dev`, so patrol_cli installs, launches and clears that app.
- Why: Patrol's `clearPackageData` (needed so each test starts fresh) wipes the data of the app it tests. When Patrol tested `app.taketray`, a test run on the owner's S24 would delete the real app's data once it holds reels. With a separate id, `flutter run` and Patrol can never touch `app.taketray`'s data.
- Alternatives considered: a `dev` product flavor (every Flutter and Patrol command would need `--flavor`, and the plan has no other use for flavors); dropping `clearPackageData` (tests would depend on each other's leftover data); testing only on an emulator (the S24 is where the real behaviour is checked).
- Consequences: a debug install appears as a second app, "TakeTray Dev", next to "TakeTray" on the phone. Anything that depends on the package name in debug (the Patrol test APK `app.taketray.dev.test`, `adb` commands) uses `app.taketray.dev`. The real app is installed from release or profile builds. The on-device run of this is Task 12.

### D-53 · Generated files are committed, and `tool/check` fails when they are stale
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 2 · Task: 2
- Decision: `*.g.dart`, `*.freezed.dart` and `drift_schemas/**` stay in git. `tool/check_generated` (run by `tool/check` right after `pub get`) hashes them, deletes every `*.g.dart` and `*.freezed.dart` (not `drift_schemas/` or `test/drift/`, whose old dumps cannot be rebuilt), runs `dart run build_runner build` in every member whose pubspec lists `build_runner` under `dev_dependencies` (any other section does not count) (plus `dart run drift_dev make-migrations` when its `build.yaml` configures drift `databases:`), hashes again and fails with `stale: <path>` for each file that changed, appeared or disappeared.
