It also hashes everything under `test/drift/`, where `make-migrations` writes its schema helpers. `app/lib/l10n/gen/` is not part of this: it is git-ignored and rebuilt by `pub get` (D-27). The script does not pass `--delete-conflicting-outputs`, because this build_runner ignores it and warns.
- Why: a generated file that is out of date compiles fine and fails later, or worse, behaves like the old code. Committing them keeps a fresh clone buildable without a code-generation step and makes generator changes show up in review. Comparing hashes before and after, not `git status`, means uncommitted work in the tree cannot cause a false failure. The files stay regenerated on disk after a failure, so a second run passes and the diff to commit is visible in `git status`.
- Alternatives considered: not committing generated files (every clone, CI job and IDE needs `build_runner` first, and drift's schema files must be committed anyway for migration tests); a `git diff --exit-code` after regenerating (fails for anyone with uncommitted changes, and passes when a stale file is itself uncommitted); checking only on CI (the owner would find out after pushing).
- Consequences: `tool/check` takes longer: about 30 s on a cold build_runner cache, about 10 s once the compiled builders are cached in `.dart_tool` (every generated file is rebuilt on each run). The check validates the working tree, not the commit: a correct generated file that was never `git add`ed passes locally, and only a CI run on a clean checkout catches it. Deleting before regenerating is what makes an orphan (a generated file no generator writes any more) show up as stale; build_runner alone only removes outputs its build cache knows about, so on a cold cache (CI) it would leave the orphan. A failed check leaves the regenerated files on disk; commit them. A new generator is covered automatically if its output is named `*.g.dart` or `*.freezed.dart`; any other output name needs adding to `hash_generated` in `tool/check_generated`.

### D-54 · Database schema v1: UTC-millisecond times, enums by name, foreign keys on, a refusing `onUpgrade`
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 2 · Task: 4
- Decision: `app/lib/data/database/` holds the Drift schema, version 1 (spec §8.1): `reels`, `clips`, `clip_reels`, `inbox`, `reel_references` (not `references`, an SQL keyword), `vn_sessions`, `vn_copies`, `settings` (JSON-encoded values) and `zone_timeline`, the Dart mirror of the native zone timeline (§8.3a) that native code fills from phase 3. Every instant is an int column of UTC milliseconds since the epoch, mapped to a UTC `DateTime` by the one `UtcMillisConverter`; no table uses Drift's `DateTime` columns, and `build.yaml` sets `store_date_time_values_as_text: false`. Enums (`ReelStatus`, `ClipSource`, `InboxState`, `ReferenceSource`, plain Dart in `domain/models/enums.dart`) are stored by `name` with Drift's `textEnum`, with no SQL `CHECK` on the values. A clip's identity is unique (`volumeName`, `mediaStoreId`) or unique `pickerUri`, and two table `CHECK`s keep it from being half empty: `source = 'mediaStore'` needs `volume_name` and `media_store_id`, `source = 'picker'` needs `picker_uri`. Links to reels, clips and VN sessions are foreign keys with `ON DELETE CASCADE`, except `vn_copies.clip_id`, which is a plain indexed integer (`vn_copies_clip_id`) with no foreign key: deleting a clip row keeps its copy rows, while deleting a reel still removes them through `vn_sessions`. Also, `beforeOpen` runs `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` on every open. Row classes end in `Row` (`ReelRow`, `ClipRow`, …), so the domain models keep the plain names; repositories live in `data/repositories/` and return domain models. `onUpgrade` throws `UnsupportedError` at version 1: the only way to reach it is a database written by a newer TakeTray (a downgrade), and Drift only writes the new `user_version` after `onUpgrade` succeeds, so the file is left untouched. `AppDatabase` takes its `QueryExecutor`: `appDatabaseProvider` (kept alive, closed on dispose) opens `driftDatabase(name: 'taketray')`, tests use `testDatabase()` (`NativeDatabase.memory()`), and "now" comes from `clockProvider` (`Clock.now()` in UTC; tests use `FixedClock`). `drift_dev make-migrations` dumps the schema to `app/drift_schemas/taketray/drift_schema_v1.json`. At one version it writes nothing else (no `app_database.steps.dart`, no generated migration test), so the v1 test helpers in `app/test/drift/taketray/generated/` come from `dart run drift_dev schema generate --data-classes --companions` (the same generator make-migrations uses from version 2 on), and the hand-written `test/drift/taketray/schema_test.dart` opens a database built from every committed dump with today's `AppDatabase` and compares it with the tables in code (`validateDatabaseSchema`, `validateDropped: true`).
- Why: milliseconds keep the order of clips shot in the same second, which whole seconds would lose, and one converter means no column can store local time by accident. Storing names means adding or reordering enum values never shifts what is already stored, and leaving out `CHECK` constraints on enum values lets a later version add a value without rebuilding the table (SQLite cannot alter a `CHECK`). The identity `CHECK`s are different: they only name the two sources that exist, and without them a `mediaStore` clip with a NULL `volume_name` would slip past the unique key (NULLs are distinct), so the same video could be stored twice. A `vn_copies` row is the app's only record of a file it created in `Movies/TakeTray/`; a clip removed from its last reel is deleted (orphan rule), and a cascade would then lose track of its copy, which only the orphaned-copies screen (spec §8.6, with a system consent dialog) could find again. Clip ids are `AUTOINCREMENT`, so a copy's dangling `clip_id` can never point at a different clip. Fixing either later would need a table rebuild in a migration. SQLite turns foreign keys off for each new connection, so without the pragma the cascades would silently do nothing. A schema on users' phones is hard to change, so the committed dump and a test against it make an unplanned change fail twice: `make-migrations` (run by `tool/check_generated`) exits with "A schema for version 1 … differs from the current schema", and `schema_test.dart` reports the differing column.
- Alternatives considered: Drift `DateTime` columns (seconds only, or ISO text that sorts by string); storing enums by index (reordering would corrupt data); `CHECK` constraints on enum columns; `vn_copies.clip_id` as a cascading foreign key (the plan's first draft; it loses copies as above) or a no-action one (deleting an orphan clip would then fail); no identity `CHECK` (the repository would be the only guard); an empty `onUpgrade` (a downgrade would stamp a newer schema as version 1 and the next upgrade would run on the wrong tables); Drift's default row class names (they clash with the domain models `Reel`, `Clip`, `ReelReference`, `VnSession` and `VnCopy`); text-length limits in SQL (`withLength` counts UTF-16 units, so 40 emoji would fail; the domain rules count characters).
- Consequences: changing a table means bumping `schemaVersion`, replacing the throwing `onUpgrade` with the generated `stepByStep`, running `make-migrations` and committing its output (docs/flow.md, "Database open and migrations"). Renaming or removing an enum value is a schema change that needs a migration of the stored text. Times are stored to the millisecond; microseconds are dropped. `tool/check_generated` regenerates only what `make-migrations` writes, so at version 1 it does not rebuild `test/drift/taketray/generated/` (the frozen v1 dump means it cannot go stale); from version 2 on `make-migrations` rewrites those files and also writes `lib/data/database/app_database.steps.dart`, which `hash_generated` does not cover yet. A `vn_copies.clip_id` may point at a clip row that no longer exists; code that joins copies to clips must allow for that. Adding a new `ClipSource` value means adding its identity `CHECK` in a migration.

### D-55 · Domain models are freezed values; text limits count characters; reference links are cleaned so one page is one reference
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 2 · Task: 5
- Decision: `app/lib/domain/models/` holds the immutable models (`Reel`, `Clip`, `NewClip`, `ClipFingerprint`, `ReelReference`, `VnSession`, `VnCopy`, `ZoneEntry`, `AppSettings`), written with `freezed` and `json_serializable` (`explicit_to_json: true` in `build.yaml`, so a nested model such as `Clip.fingerprint` becomes a plain map). They import neither Flutter nor Drift; times are UTC `DateTime`s and enums are the plain ones from `enums.dart`. `NewClip` is a `Clip` that does not exist yet (no `id`, `firstSeenAt`, `missing`, `lastVerifiedAt`): its main constructor asserts that a `mediaStore` clip has `mediaStoreId` and `volumeName` and a `picker` clip has `pickerUri` (the same rule as the database `CHECK`s, D-54), and `NewClip.mediaStore(...)` / `NewClip.picker(...)` take exactly the identity their source needs. The pure rules are in `app/lib/domain/rules/`. Text limits count characters, not UTF-16 units: `String.characters.length` from the `characters` package, after `trim()`. Reel name 1 to 40, reference label 0 to 60 (empty or null means no label); "trimming" removes whitespace and the invisible characters U+200B, U+200C, U+200D, U+2060 and U+FEFF at either end, so a name made only of those is empty; so 40 emoji, 40 flags or 40 `e` + combining accent all pass and the 41st fails. `normalizeReferenceUrl` returns null unless the trimmed text has no whitespace and parses as an `http`/`https` URL with a host. It lowercases scheme and host (Dart's `Uri` does), drops the fragment, and for the exact hosts `instagram.com`, `www.instagram.com`, `m.instagram.com` and `instagr.am` it rewrites the link to `https://www.instagram.com`, drops the query, and ends the path with exactly one `/`. A post path (`/p/<code>`, `/reel/<code>`, `/reels/<code>`, `/tv/<code>`, each optionally behind a `/<user>` segment; code = letters, digits, `_`, `-`) is folded to `/p/<code>/`, because Instagram redirects `/p/<reel code>` to the reel. So `https://www.instagram.com/reel/ABC/?igsh=x`, `http://instagram.com/reel/ABC?utm_source=ig_web_copy_link`, `https://m.instagram.com/someuser/reel/ABC/`, `https://instagr.am/tv/ABC//` and `https://www.instagram.com/p/ABC` are all `https://www.instagram.com/p/ABC/`. The code keeps its case (codes are case-sensitive, so different codes stay different). A bare `/reels/`, `/<user>/reels/` or any other path (a profile, say) is only given its single trailing slash. Other hosts keep their query. `referenceSourceOf` matches a domain or any subdomain of it (`instagram.com`, `instagr.am`, `youtube.com`, `youtu.be`, `tiktok.com`), never a look-alike such as `notinstagram.com`. `zoneAt` binary-searches the sorted timeline for the last entry at or before the instant (the later one if two share an instant). `uniqueFolderName` returns the base, then `base (2)`, `base (3)`, comparing names without regard to case. `InvalidInputException(problem)` carries one of the problem enums (`ReelNameProblem`, `ReferenceLabelProblem`, `ReferenceUrlProblem`) for the screen to turn into a message.
- Why: a model is what the app talks about; a database row is how it is stored. Keeping them apart lets the app's code read `reel.status` and `clip.fingerprint.durationMs` without knowing about tables, and `freezed` makes them values: they cannot change after they are built, two with the same fields are equal, and `copyWith` makes a changed copy. Characters, because a person counts 40 emoji as 40, while SQL `length` and Dart's `String.length` count UTF-16 units (a family emoji is 8). The Instagram query is only tracking (`igsh`, `utm_source`), and the same reel shared twice carries different values, so keeping it would store one reel twice (Review Focus 1); other sites keep theirs because it names the video (`youtube.com/watch?v=`). The Instagram rule is limited to the four exact hosts because `l.instagram.com/?u=<target>` is a redirector whose query is its whole meaning. Forced `https` and the `www.` host, because creators copy the same reel as `instagram.com` and `www.instagram.com`, and `http` and `https` links are the same page; one trailing slash, always, so the link copied with and without the slash is one reference. Case-insensitive folder names, because Android's shared storage treats `Goa` and `goa` as one folder, so a case-sensitive check would pick a name that then collides on disk.
- Alternatives considered: plain classes with hand-written `==`, `copyWith` and JSON (more code to get wrong, and every new field must be added in four places); using the Drift row classes as the models (the UI would depend on table shape and the `domain/` layer would import Drift); `String.length` for the limits (would reject a name of six family emoji); dropping the query for every host or for every `*.instagram.com` host; keeping the path of an Instagram link as it was typed (the trailing-slash variant would save twice); a stricter host list for `referenceSourceOf` that leaves out `www.`, `m.` and `vm.` hosts.
- Consequences: Model JSON writes every time as UTC with a trailing `Z`, but `DateTime.parse` of a timestamp without `Z` or an offset gives a local time; Task 9's importer must not assume such a timestamp is UTC (reject it, or state a zone). `freezed` 4 still needs `const factory Name(...)`, which the new `unnecessary_type_name_in_constructor` lint flags, so that lint is switched off in `app/analysis_options.yaml` until `freezed` supports `factory new(...)`. A host that is neither in the Instagram list nor a known site keeps its tracking query, so two shares of one TikTok or YouTube video with different `?si=` values are two references (accepted for v1; only Instagram is cleaned). Changing `normalizeReferenceUrl` later (cleaning `?si=`, say) makes two stored references of one reel the same page: that change needs a database migration that merges them (keeping the lowest id, as the import does) before the unique key on (reel, url) is relied on again; the backup importer already merges them instead of refusing the file (D-59). The Instagram rule drops the query on every Instagram path, including one where it matters (such as `/explore/search/?q=`); references are posts and reels, where it does not. The JSON of these models is for the models only: the backup file format is Task 9's. Case-insensitive folder names mean that `Goa` and `goa` get `Goa` and `Goa (2)`.

### D-56 · Reel names are not unique (amended by D-80 and D-84: duplicates are no longer allowed); one orphan rule shared by every repository; reel delete is one transaction
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 2 · Task: 6
- Amended by D-84: `create` and `rename` reject a name another reel has, ignoring case (backup import and Undo of a delete still restore one); a description is at most 1,000 characters at the repository; `delete` returns a `DeleteReelUndo`, and the orphan sweep's rows are read first (`selectOrphanClips`) so Undo can put the clips back.
- Decision: `ReelsRepository` (`app/lib/data/repositories/reels_repository.dart`) accepts two reels with the same name. A name is trimmed and must be 1 to 40 characters (`checkReelName`); an empty or blank description is stored as null, and a description has no length limit at the repository (the phase 4 form caps it; parked in `docs/later.md`); every edit (`rename`, `setDescription`, `setStatus`) sets `updatedAt` to now, and an unknown id throws `StateError`. `updatedAt` also moves on clip-link changes (Task 7 touches it when a clip is added to or removed from a reel) and on reference changes (Task 8, D-58), so Home's "most recently updated first" order follows what the user last did. `delete(id)` removes the reel, which takes its `clip_reels`, `reel_references` and `vn_sessions`/`vn_copies` rows with it (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), then calls `AppDatabase.deleteOrphanClips()`, all in one transaction. `deleteOrphanClips` (`data/database/housekeeping.dart`) deletes every clip with no `clip_reels` row and no `inbox` row, and returns how many clips it deleted (the delete's log line uses that count); it is the one place that rule is written, and Task 7 reuses it when a clip leaves its last reel. `delete` with an unknown id deletes no reel but still sweeps orphans, and its comment and log line say so.
- Why: two trips can both be "Goa", and rejecting the second would make the user invent a name for no technical reason; the only place a name must be unique is the VN folder, and `uniqueFolderName` already handles that (D-55). A clip that no reel and no list points to cannot be reached from any screen, so keeping its row would only grow the database. One transaction means a failure half way cannot leave a reel gone but its clips still there, and the clean-up reads the state after the cascade.
- Alternatives considered: unique names with a "Goa (2)" suffix (the user would see a name they did not type, and the VN folder rule already covers the one real clash); deleting clips one by one in Dart (more statements, and the rule would be copied into Task 7); a database trigger for orphans (invisible in the code, and a schema change); having `delete` also remove `vn_copies` rows (the copy files in `Movies/TakeTray/` could then no longer be found, D-54).
- Consequences: the caller (phase 8) must delete the reel's VN copy files before `delete`, because afterwards nothing records which files were copied; `vn_copies` rows of an orphaned clip are deliberately left behind. Screens that show reels must not assume names identify a reel: use the id. Logs name only ids and counts (D-10).

### D-57 · Clips: identity decides "the same clip"; detection is idempotent; every inserted clip gets a place in the same transaction
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 2 · Task: 7
- Amended by D-84: `addClipsToReel` and `removeClipsFromReel` return undo records (`AddUndo`, `RemoveUndo`), `moveClips` is new, and `watchReelClips` gives `ReelClip` (the clip, its reel count and its `addedAt`).
- Amended by the Phase 3 final review (Ruling AL): `addDetectedClips` and `recordVerification` were removed, because nothing in the app called them after D-72. `addCheckedClips(clips, sameVideo:)` is the one way to save detected clips; with a `sameVideo` that accepts every stored clip it is the idempotent lookup-or-insert described below, and with the scanner's check it also detaches a clip that is another video (D-72). `applyVerification` sets `missing` and `lastVerifiedAt`. Read `addDetectedClips` below as `addCheckedClips`.
- Decision: `ClipsRepository` (`app/lib/data/repositories/clips_repository.dart`) treats two clips as the same clip when `volumeName` + `mediaStoreId` match (a detected clip, `ClipSource.mediaStore`) or `pickerUri` matches (a picked one, `ClipSource.picker`); the fingerprint (size, duration, date taken, name) is never used to decide, only to recognise a video after Android renumbers it (phase 3). `addDetectedClips` looks each clip up by identity inside one transaction: a known clip returns its existing id and changes nothing (no second `inbox` row, `firstSeenAt` kept, a clip already in a reel does not come back to To tag, a skipped clip stays skipped); a new clip is inserted with `firstSeenAt` = now together with an `inbox` row `pending`. `addNewClipsToReel(reelId, clips)` (this replaces the brief's `upsertClips`, ruling R6b) does the same lookup-or-insert but links the clip to the reel instead of creating an `inbox` row, deletes the `inbox` row (pending or skipped) of a known clip, and sets the reel's `updatedAt` when a link was added; it throws `StateError` for an unknown reel and saves nothing. `tagClip(clipId, reelIds)` replaces the clip's links with exactly `reelIds` (`ArgumentError` when empty), keeps `addedAt` for links that stay, deletes the `inbox` row, and sets `updatedAt` of every reel that gained or lost the clip; `untagToInbox` (Undo) deletes all the clip's links, writes `inbox` = `pending` and sets `updatedAt` of the reels that lost it. `skip` and `restore` change only existing `inbox` rows. `addClipsToReel` and `removeClipsFromReel` set the reel's `updatedAt` only when a link was really added or removed (a repeat changes nothing, so the reel does not jump to the top of Home); adding deletes the clips' `inbox` rows in the same transaction, whatever their state (ruling from the Task 7 review: adding a clip to a reel by hand takes it out of To tag and Skipped, like tagging); removing then calls `deleteOrphanClips()` in the same transaction (the orphan rule, D-56), so a clip removed from its last reel with no `inbox` row is deleted (Review Focus 3) and its `vn_copies` row stays (D-54). `recordVerification(found:, missing:)` sets `missing` and `lastVerifiedAt` = now (`ArgumentError` if a clip is in both sets). Unknown ids: an unknown reel throws `StateError` (and changes nothing) in `addNewClipsToReel`, `tagClip`, `addClipsToReel` and `removeClipsFromReel`; an unknown clip throws `StateError` in `tagClip`, `untagToInbox` and `addClipsToReel`; `skip`, `restore`, `removeClipsFromReel` and `recordVerification` ignore clip ids that do not exist. `untagToInbox` removes every link of the clip, also ones from before the last `tagClip`, and rewrites the `inbox` row of an already pending clip (its `addedAt` becomes now). `watchInbox(state)` is ordered by `dateTakenUtc` ascending, ties by id; `watchReelClips` the same (spec §8.4); `watchPendingCount` and `watchReelIdsOf(clipId)` complete the streams. `dateTakenUtc`, `width` and `height` are `NOT NULL` in the database (R4g): the repository stores them as given, with no fallback logic. Phase 3's bridge supplies them: DATE_ADDED, then DATE_MODIFIED, when MediaStore has no date taken, and `0` for a width or height it does not know. This is written in the doc comments of `NewClip` and `ClipFingerprint`.
- Why: the media library's id is what Android promises is stable for one video on one volume, and the unique key in the database already enforces it, so the code agrees with the schema. Idempotency matters because the detector can see a clip twice: a rescan, or a crash after the clips were saved and before the baseline moved forward, would otherwise make a duplicate clip and a second To tag entry (Review Focus 5). The orphan sweep is global (any `deleteOrphanClips` call deletes every clip with no link and no `inbox` row), so a clip inserted first and linked later could be deleted by any concurrent delete in between; inserting the clip and its place (`inbox` row or link) in one transaction makes that impossible. Tests start a sweep from outside the transaction while it is open (a clock that starts it when the repository reads the time) and run each insert together with a reel delete; both fail when the insert and the place are split into two transactions. `tagClip` keeps `addedAt` because it records when the clip joined that reel, and tagging again into a reel the clip is already in did not add it again; it bumps only the reels that actually changed so Home's "most recently updated" order reflects what the user did, not what was merely re-saved. Times the platform may not know are filled by the bridge, not here, so the repository stays free of platform guesses and its tests stay exact.
- Alternatives considered: `upsertClips` returning ids with no place (the orphan race above); a database trigger that creates the `inbox` row (invisible in the code, and a schema change); `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` then selecting (it works for the unique key, but the lookup is needed anyway to know whether to add the `inbox` row); using the fingerprint as a second identity (two takes of one shot could look alike, and the rename case belongs to the phase 3 baseline logic); making `dateTakenUtc`, `width` and `height` nullable (every sort and display would then handle null); leaving a hand-added clip in To tag (it would show in the reel and in To tag, and `tagClip`, which means "exactly these reels", would silently drop the hand-added link when the user ticked other reels in the tag panel).
- Consequences: a clip is never in a reel and in To tag or Skipped at once through this repository; it has exactly one kind of home. `removeClipsFromReel` also deletes any other orphan that happens to exist, because the sweep is global. The caller (phase 8) must delete a clip's VN copy file before removing the clip from its last reel during a VN session. A clip whose `volumeName` is not null but whose `source` is `picker` is looked up by `pickerUri` only. Passing the same id twice in an `Iterable<int>` is the same as passing it once.

### D-58 · References dedupe on the cleaned link and report the outcome as a sealed type; VN folders are unique across sessions; settings are three key/value rows with an exported set; the zone timeline appends only on a change
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 2 · Task: 8
- Decision: four repositories, each built from `(AppDatabase, Clock)` and exposed by a keep-alive provider. **`ReferencesRepository`** (`references_repository.dart`): `add(reelId, rawUrl, {label})` cleans the link with `normalizeReferenceUrl` (D-55) before it looks for a duplicate or stores anything, so the stored `url` is the cleaned one and a reel's references are unique on it (the database unique key on (reel, url) is the safety net). A link that is not an http(s) link throws `InvalidInputException(ReferenceUrlProblem.invalid)`; a label over 60 characters throws `InvalidInputException(ReferenceLabelProblem.tooLong)`; a label is trimmed and a blank one is stored as null; an unknown reel throws `StateError`; nothing is saved in any of these cases. The result is `sealed AddReferenceResult`: `ReferenceAdded(reference)` or `ReferenceAlreadySaved(existing)`, and the second one changes nothing (the existing label and time are kept, the new label is ignored). The same link in two different reels is two references. `setLabel(id, label)` checks the label the same way and throws `StateError` for an unknown id; `delete(id)` ignores an unknown id; `watchForReel(reelId)` is newest first (`createdAt`, ties by id, both descending); `watchCounts()` maps reel id to count and leaves out reels with none. A reference change sets the reel's `updatedAt` to now, in the same transaction, so the reel moves to the top of Home like a clip change does (D-56): `add` when it returns `ReferenceAdded` (not for `ReferenceAlreadySaved`, nor when it throws), `setLabel` (unknown id: `StateError`, nothing changes) and `delete` when it removed a row (an unknown id changes nothing). **`VnRepository`** (`vn_repository.dart`): `startSession(reelId, baseFolderName, {alsoTaken})` trims the name (a blank one throws `ArgumentError`), then picks `uniqueFolderName(base, taken)` where `taken` is the folder name of every existing session (any reel) plus `alsoTaken`, which phase 8 fills with the folders already in `Movies/TakeTray/`; it throws `StateError` for an unknown reel or a reel that already has a session. `addCopy` throws `StateError` when the reel has no session or the session already has a copy of that clip; `copiesOf(reelId)` is ordered by file name, then clip id; `endSession(reelId)` deletes the session, and the database cascade deletes its copy rows. **`SettingsRepository`** (`settings_repository.dart`): one `settings` row per key, the value written as JSON: `watchedFolders` (a list of strings), `eveningReminderEnabled` (a bool) and `eveningReminderMinutes` (an int, 0 to 1439). `load()` gives each missing key its default from `AppSettings`; a row that is not valid JSON, has the wrong type or a minute outside the day is treated as missing, and the log line names the key only. `save` writes all three in one transaction, leaves every other key alone, and throws `ArgumentError` for minutes outside 0 to 1439, so it never writes what `load` would discard. `watch()` emits the settings now and after each change, but not for writes to other keys or for a save that changes nothing. `SettingsRepository.exportedKeys` is exactly those three keys and is the only set Task 9's backup exports. **`ZoneRepository`** (`zone_repository.dart`): `record(at, zoneId)` looks up the entry in effect at `at` (the last one at or before it) and does nothing when its zone is the same; otherwise it keeps the timeline free of neighbours with the same zone: an entry at exactly `at` with another zone is replaced (or removed, when the entry before it already has `zoneId`), and the entry after `at` is deleted when it has `zoneId`, since it no longer marks a change (so recording London on the 8th between Kolkata on the 5th and London on the 12th leaves Kolkata 5th, London 8th); `all()` is oldest first; `zoneAt(instant)` loads the timeline and uses the domain `zoneAt`.
- Why: the page, not the text, is what must be unique, and Instagram's `?igsh=` differs on every share (Review Focus 1), so comparing the cleaned link is the only way "saved twice" can be noticed; doing it in the repository means every caller (the share-in sheet, the add form) gets the same answer. A sealed result beats the alternatives: a boolean cannot carry the existing reference (the screen wants to say "already saved" and show it), an exception is for something going wrong, not for a normal outcome the user causes by sharing twice, and a sealed class makes the compiler list the cases a `switch` forgot. The folder name is what must be unique on disk and Android treats `Goa` and `goa` as one folder, so the check covers every session and what is already on disk, not only the same reel's; two reels may share a name (D-56). Settings are three small independent values, so a row each means a corrupt value costs only that setting, a new setting is a new key (no migration), and a missing key is simply "the default"; a bad stored value must never stop the app opening or lose the other settings. The exported set is a list of names on purpose: the `settings` table will also hold this phone's own state (Shooting mode, baselines), and restoring that onto another phone would be wrong, so a key is exported only when it is listed. The zone timeline only needs a new row when the zone changes: repeating the current zone on every app start and background run would grow it forever, and `zoneAt` stays a cheap lookup.
- Alternatives considered: returning `bool` or `ReelReference?` from `add` (loses the outcome, or the existing reference); throwing a `DuplicateReferenceException`; deduping in the screen; storing the settings as one JSON blob (one corrupt byte resets every setting; a new setting rewrites the blob); typed columns on a one-row settings table (a schema change per setting); exporting every key in the table except a block list (a new phone-state key would be exported until someone remembered to block it); `startSession` returning the existing session for a reel that has one (a caller that forgot to check would silently reuse a folder); a database `CHECK` or trigger for the zone rule.
- Consequences: `startSession` does not know the folders on disk by itself, so phase 8 must pass them in `alsoTaken` or two reels could still clash with an old folder left on the phone. `endSession` deletes the copy rows, as deleting the reel does (D-56): the caller deletes the copy files first. A phone-state key added to the `settings` table later (phase 3) must not be added to `exportedKeys`, and `load` and `watch` read only the three keys, so those rows never reach `AppSettings`. A stored `eveningReminderMinutes` outside the day shows as the default 21:00 and a corrupt `watchedFolders` as `DCIM/Camera`, with a warning in the log (the key name only). The native side also writes to `zone_timeline` from phase 3; it must follow the same rule, or the timeline gets redundant rows (harmless, but longer). Adding, relabelling or deleting a reference moves its reel to the top of Home. Different `?si=` tracking on a YouTube or TikTok link still makes two references (D-55). Pruning is exact for in-order records; two out-of-order records can drop a later observation (A@2, B@8; record B@4 drops B@8; then A@7 leaves zoneAt(8) = A). Phase 3 added `ZoneRepository.recordAll` for this (D-71): it reads the whole timeline, adds the entries, sorts by time and drops repeated neighbours in one transaction, so the native timeline goes in through `recordAll`, never through `record` one entry at a time. The phone-state keys this entry reserved are `shootingMode` and `mediaAccessWasFull`, held by `PhoneStateRepository` (D-71).

### D-59 · Backup format v1: a hand-written versioned JSON file, checked in full before one transaction replaces everything, ids kept
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 2 · Task: 9
- Decision: `data/backup/backup_format.dart` writes and reads the file by hand (not through the models' `toJson`), and `data/backup/backup_service.dart › BackupService(AppDatabase, Clock)` (`backupServiceProvider`, keep-alive) has `exportJson()` and `importJson(json)`. **Format v1:** an object with exactly `app` ("TakeTray"), `schemaVersion` (1, `backupSchemaVersion`), `exportedAt`, `reels`, `references`, `clips`, `clipReels`, `inbox`, `settings` and `zoneTimeline`, in that order, 2-space indent; row keys in the order of spec §8.6; ids are the database ids; enums by name; times are UTC ISO-8601 with exactly three fraction digits and `Z` (microseconds dropped), the year as `DateTime.toIso8601String` writes it (four digits for 0 to 9999, `-0001` down to -9999, a sign and six digits beyond, such as `+010001`); rows sorted by id (links by clip then reel, inbox by clip, zone entries by time), so one database always gives one text. `settings` holds the three `SettingsRepository.exportedKeys`, written as `load()` reads them (a missing or corrupt one as its default). **Left out:** `vn_sessions` and `vn_copies`, and every other `settings` key (phone state such as Shooting mode and the baselines, phase 3). **Import:** `decodeBackup` reads the whole file first. Not JSON → `BackupProblem.notJson`; not an object, `app` not "TakeTray", or `schemaVersion` missing, not a whole number or below 1 → `notTakeTray`; above 1 → `newerVersion`, before anything else is read; anything else wrong → `invalidData`. Checked: every format key present with its type (`null` only for the nullable fields, and an absent nullable key is invalid too); ids whole numbers from 1 to 2^53 and unique per table (SQLite's last id is 2^63 − 1, and a table whose `AUTOINCREMENT` counter reaches it can never add another row, so a hand-edited id near it is refused); `sizeBytes`, `durationMs`, `width` and `height` whole numbers ≥ 0; enums known; every `references.reelId`, `clipReels.reelId` in `reels` and every `clipReels.clipId`, `inbox.clipId` in `clips`; no duplicate link, no second inbox row for a clip; reel names by `checkReelName`, labels by `checkReferenceLabel`, links by `normalizeReferenceUrl`; the `clips` identity rule (a `mediaStore` clip has `volumeName` and `mediaStoreId`, a `picker` clip has `pickerUri`) and no two clips with the same (`volumeName`, `mediaStoreId`) or `pickerUri`; reminder minutes 0 to 1439; watched folders all text; no two zone entries at the same instant; every time a real date and time with seconds and a `Z` or an explicit offset, its year in one of those three forms and inside what a `DateTime` can hold (converted to UTC, then cut to whole milliseconds, as the database stores it, so two times that would be stored the same count as a duplicate). A time with no `Z` or offset is refused, never guessed to be UTC. Unknown keys are ignored. Text is stored the way the repositories store it: names, descriptions and labels trimmed, a blank description or label stored as none, links stored cleaned. Three states the app never makes are brought back to its rules rather than refused or imported as they are: of the references of one reel whose cleaned links are the same, only the one with the lowest id (the first saved) is kept, as `ReferencesRepository.add` would have kept it (ruling of the final review, 2026-10-27); a clip with a reel link loses its `inbox` row (the link wins, as in `tagClip` and `addClipsToReel`, D-57); and then a clip with neither a link nor an `inbox` row is left out (the orphan rule, D-56). The counts of all three are logged (`referencesDropped`, `inboxRowsDropped`, `orphanClipsDropped`). Only then, in **one transaction**, every row of all nine tables is deleted (VN rows and phone-state settings too) and the backup's rows are inserted with their own ids. `importJson` returns `ImportSummary(reels, clips, references, inboxPending, inboxSkipped)`, counting what was stored. If the replace still hits a database constraint (`SQLITE_CONSTRAINT`, directly or wrapped in drift's `DriftRemoteException` from the background isolate), the transaction rolls back and `importJson` throws `BackupException(invalidData)`, never the raw `SqliteException` (whose text holds the statement's values); only the result code is logged. Any other database error (a full disk, say) is rethrown as it is, and if it reaches the log, `formatRecord` writes it without the statement or values (D-28). `BackupException.where` names the bad spot as a path of format keys and positions (`clips[3].source`), which is logged; values never are (D-10).
- Why: import replaces all of a user's data, so a bad file must be refused before anything is touched (Review Focus 2), and checking the whole file in Dart first gives one clear `BackupProblem` instead of a SQLite error halfway through; the one transaction is the second guard, so even a failure the checks missed rolls back to the old data. A separate format version (not the database's `schemaVersion`, which migrations handle on the phone) lets an older TakeTray say "this file is from a newer version" instead of misreading it; ignoring unknown keys lets a later version add fields without a version bump. Writing the format by hand means changing a model can't silently change the file; the committed fixture `test/data/backup/fixtures/backup_v1.json` must import with a known summary and export back to the same text, which pins it. Keeping ids keeps every link between sections without renumbering, and makes a round trip exact. VN sessions describe files in this phone's `Movies/TakeTray/`, and Shooting mode and its baselines describe this phone's media library; restoring them elsewhere would be wrong (spec §8.6). Trimming and cleaning on import keep a hand-edited file from storing a reel name with spaces or a second copy of one Instagram reel behind a tracking code.
- Alternatives considered: the models' `toJson` (a refactor would change the file); validating while inserting and relying on rollback (still safe, but the error is a database error, and limits SQL cannot check, like 40 characters, would pass); renumbering ids on import (no gain, and the round trip would no longer be exact); accepting a time without `Z` as UTC (D-55: never guess); being lenient about absent nullable keys (a strict reader is simpler to explain, and our export always writes them); for two references of one reel with the same cleaned link: refusing the file (the first version did; but D-55 expects a later version to clean YouTube and TikTok `?si=` links, and from then on every backup holding such a pair, written by that same version, would be refused) or keeping the first one in the file (the lowest id is the one the user saved first, whatever order a hand-edited file uses); for a clip in no reel and no list, or a clip both in To tag and in a reel (states the database allows but the app never makes): refusing the file (one such slip would make a user's backup unrestorable) or importing them as they are (an orphan is invisible, and `addCheckedClips` (then `addDetectedClips`) treats its identity as known, so the video would never reach To tag again; a clip in both places shows in the reel and in To tag, and `tagClip` would silently drop the hand-added link, D-57).
- Consequences: Imported ids may be ids the database used before the import for other rows: clip 10 can be one video before the import and another after. D-54's "a dangling clip id never points at another clip" survives only because the same transaction deletes every `vn_copies` row, the one place a clip id outlives its clip. So anything that ever keeps clip ids outside these tables (a later table, native state, a cache) must be wiped by the import too, or ids will clash. SQLite's `AUTOINCREMENT` counter (`sqlite_sequence`) is moved up by an inserted id and never down by a delete, so rows added after an import get ids above every imported one and above any handed out before; tests check both. VN copy files on the phone are no longer recorded after an import: the storage screen lists them as not owned (spec §8.6, phase 8). After an import Shooting mode is off (its key is gone), and phase 3 must reset the baselines to "now" then. Choosing the file (Storage Access Framework) and the warning before import are phase 10; "X of Y clips found" needs the media bridge (phase 3) and the screen (phase 10). Changing what a v1 file means needs `backupSchemaVersion` 2 and a reader that still reads v1; adding an exported setting means adding it to `exportedKeys` and to the format. The reference `source` is kept from the file, not recomputed from the link. The zone timeline is imported as written, not pruned by `ZoneRepository.record`'s rule.

### D-60 · CI runs `tool/check` on Ubuntu with the CI goldens only; Patrol and the release check stay local
- Date: 2026-10-26 · Phase: 2 · Task: 11
- Decision: `.github/workflows/check.yml` runs on every push and pull request (and by hand), on `ubuntu-latest`, with a 30-minute timeout, `permissions: contents: read`, no secrets and no deploy step. It installs Flutter 3.47.7 with `git clone --depth 1 --branch 3.47.7` into `$HOME/development/flutter`, the path `tool/flutter` and `tool/dart` already use, so no wrapper changed, then runs `tool/check` with `CI=true`. `CI=true` turns the macOS goldens off, so only `goldens/ci/` is compared (D-61 now also makes `CI` the switch that turns the CI goldens on, and says they are Linux images). The Flutter folder is cached by version and `~/.pub-cache` by the hash of `pubspec.lock`. A newer run on the same branch or pull request cancels the older one, except on `main`, where every run finishes. When a run fails, any `failures/` folder written by a golden test is uploaded as an artifact. Not run on CI: Patrol and everything that needs the phone (they stay local, on the S24) and `tool/check_release_permissions` (it builds the release APK, which needs the Android SDK; the owner runs it at the end of each phase, D-31).
- Why: one script, `tool/check`, defines "the code is healthy", so a push is judged by exactly what the owner runs by hand, and the workflow has no logic of its own to drift out of date. Ubuntu runners are the cheapest, and the CI goldens (text drawn as Ahem blocks) were expected to match across machines (they did not, see D-61).
- Alternatives considered: a third-party Flutter setup action (one more thing to trust and pin, and the wrapper path would need to change); one workflow step per check (the list would duplicate `tool/check` and could disagree with it); running the macOS goldens on a macOS runner (ten times the price, and the images would still move with every runner image update); running Patrol on a CI emulator (slow and flaky, and D-32 already puts device tests on the S24).
- Consequences: a change to the steps in `tool/check` needs no workflow edit, but a program that `tool/check` newly calls must exist on the runner (the workflow checks for the current ones). Bumping Flutter means changing `FLUTTER_VERSION` in the workflow as well as `EXPECTED_FLUTTER` in `tool/check`; the cache key follows the version. The drift tests download a prebuilt SQLite when they are built, so the runner needs the network; the app itself stays offline. **Superseded by D-61:** the open risk about arm64-Mac CI goldens on x64 Linux came true on the first run, and D-61 records the fix (the CI goldens are made on Linux). On-device and release-manifest regressions are still caught only by hand. The first run on GitHub is described in D-61.

### D-61 · CI goldens are made and checked on Linux only; macOS goldens on the Mac only
- Date: 2026-10-27 · Phase: 2 · Task: 11 (follow-up)
- Decision: the `CI` environment variable picks the set of goldens (`packages/taketray_ui/test/flutter_test_config.dart`). With `CI` set, only `goldens/ci/` runs (alchemist's `CiGoldensConfig(enabled: onCi)`); without it, only `goldens/macos/` runs (`PlatformGoldensConfig(enabled: !onCi)`, macOS only). So `tool/check` on the Mac compares `goldens/macos/` and GitHub compares `goldens/ci/`. Both are compared with zero tolerance (no `diffThreshold`). The `goldens/ci/` images are made on GitHub's Linux runner by the manual workflow `.github/workflows/update-goldens.yml` (`workflow_dispatch` only, `contents: read`, same Flutter install and caches as `check.yml`): it runs `CI=true tool/flutter test --update-goldens --tags golden` in `packages/taketray_ui` and uploads every `goldens/ci/` image, under its repository path, as the artifact `ci-goldens`. It never pushes or commits. `tool/pull_ci_goldens <run-id>` (needs `gh`, logged in) downloads that artifact and copies the images into the tree, replacing each `goldens/ci/` folder (images the run no longer makes are deleted), then prints `git status --short` for them; it refuses to run if `goldens/ci/` has uncommitted changes. To update: run the "update goldens" workflow on the branch (Actions tab, or `gh workflow run update-goldens.yml --ref <branch>`), run `tool/pull_ci_goldens <run-id>`, look at the changed images, commit them.
- Why: the first GitHub run (run 40511210358) passed every logic test but 58 CI goldens failed by 0.00 to 3 % of their pixels, because the images had been made on an arm64 Mac and Linux x64 anti-aliases and scales images slightly differently (D-60 had named this as the open risk). The owner chose exact Linux images on GitHub and exact macOS images on the Mac over a tolerance, so a visual change shows up as a real difference and never hides inside a threshold.
- Alternatives considered: a `diffThreshold` in the comparator (a real one-pixel regression could pass); making the CI goldens on the Mac in Docker (slow, needs Docker, still not the real runner); one set only (the Mac set has real text and is the one that is compared with the prototype, the Linux set is what GitHub can check); having the workflow commit the images itself (needs write access to the repository; D-60 keeps the token read-only).
- Consequences: a change that alters a widget's look needs both sets updated: `--update-goldens` on the Mac for `goldens/macos/`, the workflow plus `tool/pull_ci_goldens` for `goldens/ci/`. Until the Linux images are updated, `tool/check` on the Mac still passes (it does not read `goldens/ci/`) but GitHub fails, so the update has to be part of the same branch. `CI=true tool/check` on the Mac now compares the Linux images and is expected to fail on pixels; do not use it to check goldens. On Linux without `CI` no golden runs at all. A Linux runner image update could in principle move the images; the same workflow regenerates them. A new Flutter member with CI goldens needs its own step in `update-goldens.yml`; today only `packages/taketray_ui` has any. Supersedes the golden sentences of D-39 and D-60.

### D-62 · Text contrast in tests is measured from resolved colours on every platform, worst case, for every shown text; Flutter's pixel guess runs on macOS only
- Date: 2026-10-27 · Phase: 2 · Task: 11 (follow-up)
- Decision: `expectTtAccessible` (`packages/taketray_ui/test/support/tt_accessibility.dart`) checks text contrast with `ttTextContrastGuideline` (`test/support/tt_text_contrast.dart`) on every platform, and runs Flutter's `textContrastGuideline` as well only when `Platform.isMacOS`.
  - **Thresholds:** the same as Flutter's (`MinimumTextContrastGuideline.targetContrastRatio`: 4.5:1, or 3:1 at 18 dp or bold at 14 dp), with the same 0.01 tolerance and the same `fontWeight == bold` rule.
  - **Which texts:** the texts Flutter's semantics walk finds (a node whose label is the text), plus every other shown text: a hit-testable, non-empty `Text` or `EditableText`, such as one merged into a parent label (tiles, chips, pills) or under `ExcludeSemantics`. Exempt, as in Flutter's walk: text whose semantics node (after merging) is disabled, hidden or invisible, and text more than 50 px off screen. So a test cannot pass because nothing was measured: every visible text is a sample. `measureTtTextContrast` returns the samples, one per text.
  - **How:** three screenshots in which no glyph is drawn. Each `RenderParagraph`/`RenderEditable` in the view gets a copy of its spans with the text colour transparent and no shadows; that image is exactly what is behind the text. Then each run also gets `backgroundColor` = its own text colour: each run is a solid box, composited exactly as a fully covered glyph pixel would be (opacity layers, translucent fills, Stack siblings, backdrop blur). Then the same boxes in the inverted colour, so a box shows up even where the text colour equals the background. The original spans are put back and the frame flushed in a `finally`.
  - **Which pixels:** inside each text's paint bounds, the box pixels are the ones the second or third image changed. Pixels with a 4-neighbour outside the boxes are dropped: partly covered rows and columns, and clip edges. Each remaining pixel has a (text, background) pair.
  - **The ratio:** the worst pair, after ignoring the worst 2 % of the text's pixels (`TtTextContrastGuideline.ignoredShare`), so a few stray pixels cannot decide it. So at least 98 % of each text's fully covered pixels meet the reported ratio. A band under the threshold fails: text half over a light area, the light end of a gradient, a faint span in `Text.rich`.
  - **Fail loud:** a span with a `foreground`/`background` paint or a `backgroundColor`, or a text run with no colour, throws `StateError`. Text with no visible box measures 1:1 and fails.
- Why: after D-61 made the goldens green, four contrast tests still failed only on GitHub's Linux runner (runs 40511210358 and 40516635767): TtSnack 1.24:1, the TtSceneHeader subtitle 1.77:1, TtGlass 1.42:1 (dark) and 2.44:1 (light). All passed on the Mac.
  - **The evidence that it is a measuring artefact, not a colour bug** (debug run 40518575196): on Linux the backgrounds are pixel-identical to the Mac's (`#d3d6d9`, `#f7fafd`, `#0c0e11`, `#1a1c1e`, same counts within 1 %), but there are far fewer pixels in the exact text colour: 87 on the Mac vs 12 on Linux for "Clip deleted", 64 vs 15 for "8 clips · 2:14", 39 vs under 6 for "Hello". Flutter's guideline takes the most frequent pixel on each side of the average lightness as the text colour, so on Linux an anti-aliased edge pixel wins (`#bec1c4` for text that is `#2e3133`). Measured from their real colours, the texts pass AA: 8.98, 8.94, 13.27 and 14.41:1.
  - **Why Linux glyphs are lighter is inferred, not proven.** The likely cause is the rasteriser: CoreText on macOS, which thickens small glyphs, vs FreeType on Linux. Another candidate is how each engine applies the weight axis of the variable font `PlusJakartaSans-Variable.ttf`; its default instance is 400, which makes that less likely. The conclusion does not depend on which one it is.
  - **Worst case instead of the most frequent pair:** the owner wants the contrast tests tight. Flutter's mode-based guess passes white text half over white, and white text over a black-to-white gradient (6.39:1, from a middle row, in review). The box method makes a worst-case rule cheap.
  - **Every shown text:** Flutter's walk only finds text whose semantics label is exactly that text. Text merged into a parent label was checked by neither guideline. Measuring it directly reached the texts of the app bar, day header, player header, scrub bar, reel chip, reference cards, status pill and the "same as previous" button in their accessibility tests, 21 tests that measured no text before; all of them pass.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Skipping or loosening the contrast check on Linux: rejected by the owner, the check must stay tight.
  - A tolerance or a lower threshold: would hide real failures.
  - Computing both colours from the widget tree alone: cannot see text over images, gradients, blurred glass or Stack siblings, which the screenshot sees.
  - Sampling Flutter's guideline at physical resolution: still a guess from glyph pixels, only less often wrong.
  - The strict minimum over all pixels: a single edge or rounding pixel could fail a good text.
  - Failing only when no text at all was measured: simpler, but a component with one measured text and one merged text would still pass silently.
- Consequences:
  - Linux CI and the Mac judge contrast with the same deterministic numbers. The Mac also keeps Flutter's pixel check, so a component must pass both there.
  - Text shadows are ignored, which can only make the result stricter.
  - When text overlaps another text's run box, both boxes are in the same screenshot; no component does that so far.
  - A new text style feature (a gradient `foreground` paint, highlighted text) needs the guideline extended before its component can call `expectTtAccessible`.
  - Decorative text that is shown is now held to AA too. Exempt it only through its semantics (disabled), never by skipping the check.
  - Supersedes the `expectTtAccessible` sentence of D-39.

### D-63 · Each phase works on its own branch and lands on `main` through a green pull request
- Date: 2026-10-28 · Phase: 3 · Task: 1
- Decision: each phase works on a branch named `phase-N-…` (this one: `phase-3-media-bridge`), created from an up-to-date `main`. The branch is pushed after every task commit, so CI (D-60) runs on it each time, and a red run is fixed in the task that caused it. The phase ends with a pull request into `main`, which must be green. The owner approves before it is merged, and the branch is deleted after the merge.
- Why: the owner asked for it on 2026-10-27, so `main` only ever gets code that CI has passed and the owner has reviewed.
- Alternatives considered: committing straight to `main` as phases 0 to 2 did (a red or half-finished commit can sit on `main`, and CI only judges it after the fact).
- Consequences: CI runs more often (once per task push). A phase's work is only visible on `main` after the owner merges the pull request. The pull request description ends with the generated-with line, and commits end with a `Co-Authored-By` line.

### D-64 · No Google backup or device transfer of app data; a new phone starts empty; Export / Import is a same-phone backup
- Date: 2026-10-28 · Phase: 3 · Task: 1
- Decision: the owner chose to switch Android's automatic backup off for TakeTray. The manifest's `<application>` sets `android:allowBackup="false"`, `android:dataExtractionRules="@xml/data_extraction_rules"` (Android 12+) and `android:fullBackupContent="@xml/backup_rules"` (Android 11). Both XML files exclude every domain (`root`, `file`, `database`, `sharedpref`, `external`, path `.`); `data_extraction_rules.xml` does so in both `<cloud-backup>` and `<device-transfer>`. `tool/check_release_permissions` now dumps the merged release manifest (`aapt2 dump xmltree`) and fails unless `allowBackup` is `false` and both rule files are set. It also fails if the release build has `INTERNET`, `READ_MEDIA_IMAGES`, `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` or `ACCESS_MEDIA_LOCATION`. The check that `READ_MEDIA_VIDEO`, `READ_MEDIA_VISUAL_USER_SELECTED` and `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (max SDK 32) are present is written but off until Task 4 adds them (`ENFORCE_MEDIA_PERMISSIONS`).
- Why: drift_flutter keeps `taketray.sqlite`, with its `-wal` and `-shm` sidecar files, in `app_flutter/` (path_provider's documents directory, `context.getDir("flutter")`). Android's backup counts that folder under the `root` domain (the `file` domain is only `getFilesDir()`, `files/`), so `root` is the exclusion that covers the database; the other domains are excluded so nothing else (shared preferences with the native zone list, cache) leaves the phone either. The `device_*` domains are not listed because the app uses no device-protected storage. The clip rows hold MediaStore ids. Restored on another phone, those ids would point at other videos, which is the worst bug TakeTray can have (spec §4: a wrong video is worse than a missing one).
- Alternatives considered: let Android restore the database and re-match every clip by its fingerprint (size, duration, date) after the restore. Rejected: a wrong match is still possible when two videos look alike, and it only helps when the videos themselves moved to the new phone, which is not guaranteed.
- Consequences: a new phone starts with an empty TakeTray. Export / Import (Settings, phase 10; D-59) is a same-phone backup, for example before reinstalling, and an import leaves Shooting mode off. Moving to a new phone is not supported in v1 (spec §8.6; the clips would show as missing there). Nothing of the app is copied by "Back up to Google One" or by the phone-to-phone setup. A reinstall also starts empty. The release check proves the manifest, not Android's behaviour; no test on the phone restores a backup.

### D-65 · The coverage gate counts every file under its prefixes, through a generated import test; a prefix with no files yet passes
- Date: 2026-10-28 · Phase: 3 · Task: 2
- Decision: before the `app` test run, `tool/check` writes `app/test/_coverage_imports_test.dart`, which imports every non-generated `.dart` file under `lib/domain/`, `lib/data/`, `lib/platform/` and `lib/features/*/view_models/` and holds one empty test. It is deleted when the run ends (an `EXIT` trap, plus a removal at the start of every run), is git-ignored and is never committed. `check_coverage` accepts a `*` that stands for exactly one folder name, and a prefix that matches no file with executable lines prints `no files` and passes, unless no prefix matches anything (then it fails, so a typo cannot pass).
- Why: `flutter test --coverage` lists only the files some test loads. A new file that no test touches was therefore invisible and could not lower the total. The Phase 3 and 4 code (`lib/platform/`, the view models) arrives in several tasks, so the gate must count each file from the day it exists. A temporary generated test is the smallest way to load them all with the real tool; checking the lcov list against the files on disk would need an allowlist for files without executable lines (enums).
- Alternatives considered: compare the lcov file list with the files on disk and fail on a missing one (needs an allowlist, and a missing file is a failure rather than a number that falls); write the imports test as a committed file (it would go stale as files are added).
- Consequences: the gate now fails a change that adds an untested file with enough lines to pull a prefix under 80 %. A prefix that is mistyped in `tool/check` while another prefix is right prints `no files` and passes (only the all-empty case fails), so a new prefix should be checked once by reading the printed line. A file that cannot be imported on its own (a part file) is skipped.

### D-66 · The native bridge: one Pigeon host API, a thin Kotlin pipe, every decision in Dart, `MediaBridge` as the test seam, generated files committed and checked
- Date: 2026-10-28 · Phase: 3 · Task: 3
- Decision: Dart and Kotlin talk through one Pigeon host API, `MediaHostApi`, declared in `app/pigeons/media_api.dart` (`@HostApi`, no Flutter-side API). Pigeon is pinned exactly in `app/pubspec.yaml` (`pigeon: 29.0.11`, no caret). It writes `app/lib/platform/media_api.g.dart` and `app/android/app/src/main/kotlin/app/taketray/media/MediaApi.g.kt` (package `app.taketray.media`); both are committed like other generated code. The contract has plain message classes (`VideoRowMsg` and others, ending in `Msg`), keeps MediaStore's units (DATE_TAKEN in milliseconds, DATE_ADDED and DATE_MODIFIED in seconds) and names the tests' rules in its doc comments (what each query includes, pending rows, folder matching). Kotlin is a thin pipe: it reads MediaStore and reports, and decides nothing about which video is which. Every such decision is Dart code in `lib/domain/rules/` and `lib/platform/`. Dart reaches the contract only through `MediaBridge` (`lib/platform/media_bridge.dart`), an interface in domain types (`MediaVideo`, `MediaAccess`, `ZoneEntry`, ...; `lib/domain/models/media.dart`). `PigeonMediaBridge` implements it over the generated class and only converts types: a null `mimeType` becomes `video/*`, a `ZoneChangeMsg.atMs` becomes a UTC `ZoneEntry`, and a `PlatformException` passes through unchanged. `mediaBridgeProvider` is `@Riverpod(keepAlive: true)`. Tests use `FakeMediaBridge` (`test/support/`), which filters the way the contract says, with `failNext`, gates and a call log. `tool/check_generated` now also runs Pigeon for every `pigeons/*.dart` of a member that has `pigeon` in `dev_dependencies` (before build_runner, then `dart format` on the Dart output, because Pigeon does not format it and `tool/check` does), hashes `*.g.kt` under `android/app/src/` as well, and fails with `stale: <path>` when a committed Pigeon output differs. Until Task 4, `MediaHostApiImpl.kt` is a stub whose methods throw `NotImplementedError`; it is not registered in `MainActivity`.
- Why: the research for D-18 found no plugin that exposes MediaStore generations, `IS_PENDING` or the row fields the matching needs; Pigeon gives a typed contract on both sides from one file, so a field cannot be renamed on one side only. Keeping Kotlin free of decisions means the rules that protect against the worst bug (spec §4, a wrong video) run on a laptop in fast tests, and the Kotlin tests only have to prove that a query selects the right rows. The interface plus a fake is what lets Tasks 5 to 10 test the scanner, the verifier and the screens without a phone. Exact pinning and a freshness check mean a Pigeon update is a deliberate change with a visible diff, never a surprise in `pub get`.
- Alternatives considered: a third-party media plugin (none exposes generations or pending rows, and it would put its decisions in someone else's code); a hand-written `MethodChannel` (untyped, the two sides can drift apart without any failing check); Pigeon's JNI/FFI mode (experimental; the plain channel is proven and testable with a mock messenger); a caret range for Pigeon (generated code changes with every minor version, and the freshness check would fail on another machine); putting the matching rules in Kotlin (needs the phone or Robolectric to test, and a second copy of the rules to keep in step).
- Consequences: the contract is the one place a new native question is added: edit `pigeons/media_api.dart`, run `cd app && ../tool/dart run pigeon --input pigeons/media_api.dart` (or `tool/check_generated`), change `MediaBridge`, `PigeonMediaBridge` and `FakeMediaBridge`, and implement it in Kotlin. A stale generated file fails `tool/check` and CI. Pigeon 29 generates `suspend fun` for `@async` methods, so the Kotlin implementation uses coroutines (the build already has them). The fake's `volumeList` is named so because `volumes` is the bridge method. `videosByIds` is not specified for pending rows in the contract; the fake returns any row that exists and Task 4 must do the same. `videosShotSince` compares DATE_ADDED / DATE_MODIFIED with `sinceMs ~/ 1000`, in the fake and in Kotlin, so the answer can only be a bigger superset, never a smaller one. The plan called this entry D-65; D-65 was already taken by the coverage gate (Task 2).

### D-67 · Android unit tests: Robolectric on the JVM, in `tool/check` and CI, for the Kotlin that depends on the Android version
- Date: 2026-10-28 · Phase: 3 · Task: 4
- Decision: the Kotlin media bridge has unit tests in `app/android/app/src/test/kotlin/app/taketray/media/`, run with JUnit 4 and Robolectric 4.17 (`testImplementation` of `junit:junit:4.13.2`, `org.robolectric:robolectric:4.17`, `androidx.test:core:1.7.0`). `src/test/resources/robolectric.properties` runs every Robolectric test on SDK 30, 33, 34 and 35 (Android 11, 13, 14 and 15; 31 and 32 ask for the same permission as 30). Pure rules (`MediaAccessChecker`) are plain JVM tests that take the SDK level as an argument and cover 30, 32, 33, 34 and 35. `unitTests.isIncludeAndroidResources = true`, with a `dependsOn` from `package<Variant>UnitTestForUnitTest` to Flutter's `copyFlutterAssets<Variant>` (both write the merged assets folder; Gradle 9 refuses the undeclared overlap). `tool/test_android` runs `./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest --console=plain` in `app/android`, after `tool/flutter build apk --debug --config-only` when `local.properties` or the git-ignored `gradlew` is missing or `flutter.sdk` is not `~/development/flutter`. `tool/check` runs it as the step `android unit tests`, after the Flutter tests. CI adds `actions/setup-java@v4` (Temurin 17), an `actions/cache` of `~/.gradle/caches`, `~/.gradle/wrapper` and `~/.m2/repository/org/robolectric` (the framework jars Robolectric downloads, about 650 MB for the four SDKs), keyed on the Gradle files and `robolectric.properties`, a step that fails early when `ANDROID_HOME` is not the image's Android SDK, and the NDK that Flutter names (`28.2.13676358`, `env.ANDROID_NDK_VERSION`, checked against Flutter's `FlutterExtension.kt`) installed with `sdkmanager` (three attempts) and cached by version: Gradle needs it to configure the project because the `jni` plugin behind `path_provider_android` has a native build, and the image does not ship that version (Gradle's own download of it took 6.5 minutes and failed in run 40701015612). A query's semantics are tested on real SQLite through an `SQLiteQueryBuilder` set up as MediaProvider sets one up for other apps (strict, strict columns, strict grammar), so a selection Android's grammar check would refuse fails on the laptop. MediaStore itself, `getVersion` and `getGeneration` have no Robolectric shadows: `Volumes` is an interface and `MediaStoreVolumes` takes its version read as a function.
- Why: emulators are still deferred (D-32), and the phone run (Task 11) proves only the S24's Android version. The permission model and the query selections differ per Android version and are exactly where a mistake shows a wrong video or hides a real one; JVM tests run them for four versions in seconds on every push. The SDK 35 framework jars are Java 17 bytecode (class version 61), so JDK 17 covers all four levels; SDK 36 would need JDK 21.
- Alternatives considered: no Kotlin tests until the phone run (the per-version branches would be proven for one version only); instrumented tests on CI emulators (slow and flaky, D-32); mocking `ContentResolver` with Mockito (proves only what the mock is told; Robolectric runs the real framework classes, including the real query builder's grammar check); every SDK from 30 to 35 (31, 32 add time and no new branch).
- Consequences: a CI run takes about 10.5 minutes with warm caches (run 40705948149: 10 min 27 s, of which the Android unit tests 2 min 43 s and the Gradle, Robolectric and NDK cache restores about 45 s), against 5 min 46 s to 7 min 15 s before (runs 40691639864, 40693658464): about 3 to 4.5 minutes more, under the 6-minute line that would have parked it for phase 11. The first run after a cache key changes is longer (run 40703957612: 11 min 49 s, Android tests 4 min 38 s, NDK install 41 s). `tool/check` needs a JDK and the Android SDK on the Mac (both already there for builds). The first run downloads the framework jars into `~/.m2`; later runs take about 30 s when Gradle is warm. A Kotlin change that breaks a per-version branch fails `tool/check` and CI. What Robolectric cannot prove (MediaProvider's own behaviour, the real permission dialog, One UI) is still Task 11's job on the S24.

### D-68 · The permission model: three levels per Android version, both media permissions asked together on 14+, and when TakeTray may ask again
- Date: 2026-10-28 · Phase: 3 · Task: 4
- Decision: the release manifest declares exactly `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (`maxSdkVersion="32"`), `READ_MEDIA_VIDEO` and `READ_MEDIA_VISUAL_USER_SELECTED`, and `tool/check_release_permissions` now enforces that list by default (`ENFORCE_MEDIA_PERMISSIONS` defaults to 1). `MediaAccessChecker.level`: Android 11 and 12 (API 30 to 32) are full with `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE`, else denied; Android 13 (33) is full with `READ_MEDIA_VIDEO`, else denied; Android 14 and later (34+) are full with `READ_MEDIA_VIDEO`, limited with only `READ_MEDIA_VISUAL_USER_SELECTED` ("Select photos and videos"), else denied. `permissionsToRequest` asks for `READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (30 to 32), `READ_MEDIA_VIDEO` (33), or `READ_MEDIA_VIDEO` and `READ_MEDIA_VISUAL_USER_SELECTED` in one request (34+), so the system dialog offers "Allow all" and "Select photos and videos". `requestAccess` uses `Activity.requestPermissions` with request code `0x7A71` and one pending `CompletableDeferred` (FlutterActivity is not a ComponentActivity, so the Activity Result API is not available); a second request while one is waiting fails with `FlutterError("busy")`; `MainActivity.onRequestPermissionsResult` completes it, and the level is read again with `checkSelfPermission` rather than from the result arrays (empty when Android cancels a request). When the activity is destroyed (`onDestroy`, `cleanUpFlutterEngine`) a waiting request fails with `host-gone`, because the engine and its Dart side go with the activity; it never hangs. The rationale is `shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale(first requested permission)`, read before the request and again after the answer (Ruling F, which replaced the plan's "canAskAgain = rationale after the request"). A request ends **blocked** when `!full && !rationaleAfter && (rationaleBefore || wasBlocked)`: Android gives a rationale only between the first and the second denial, so a first dismissal or a first denial stays askable, a second denial (rationale true → false) is blocked, and a dismissal while blocked stays blocked. The result is remembered as `media_request_blocked` in the `taketray_native` preferences; full access clears it, after a request and in every `accessReport` (access granted in Settings ends a block). `canAskAgain` (after a request and in a report) is true when access is full, or limited (Android shows the picker again for partial access, Ruling G; to be confirmed on the S24), or Android would show a rationale, or not blocked. A failure of the `requestPermissions` call itself goes through the same sanitising guard as every other method and frees the request slot.
- Why: video is all TakeTray reads, so it asks for nothing else (no images, no all-files access, no location). Limited access must be its own level, because under it MediaStore returns only the picked rows, and the verifier would otherwise mark every other clip missing (Review Focus 1). Asking for both permissions together on 14+ is how Android offers the partial choice at all. "Can ask again" decides between showing the dialog and sending the user to Settings: Android's rationale flag alone cannot tell "never asked" from "said no twice", and a wrong `false` on first launch would send a new user to Settings instead of the dialog.
- Alternatives considered: request only `READ_MEDIA_VIDEO` on 14+ (Android's compatibility mode then grants `READ_MEDIA_VIDEO` for the session even when the user picked only some videos, so a partial choice would look like full access and the verifier would mark the other clips missing); an Activity Result launcher (needs a ComponentActivity, which FlutterActivity is not); a permission plugin (adds code TakeTray does not need, and its levels do not map to MediaStore's limited reads); remembering "asked once" instead of "blocked" (after Android resets unused permissions, the dialog would be possible again but TakeTray would send the user to Settings); the plan's rule, rationale after the request only (a first dismissal, with no rationale before or after, would have sent a new user to Settings for good).
- Consequences: `MediaAccessView.canAskAgain` (D-75) can be trusted on first launch and after a dismissed dialog. If Android's real rationale sequence differs on One UI, the worst case is one extra trip to Settings or one dialog that does not appear (Task 11 checks it on the S24). Every unexpected native exception reaches Dart as `FlutterError("native-error", "<method> failed: <ExceptionClass>")`, without its message, which can hold a URI or a path (D-10). The real dialog and One UI's buttons are checked on the S24 in Task 11.

### D-69 · The shooting date is the first plausible MediaStore date; "the same video" is size, duration and date, never the name; when unsure, a clip is missing
- Date: 2026-10-28 · Phase: 3 · Task: 5
- Amended by D-78: the "Add earlier videos" windows below (`EarlierRange { today, lastTwoDays, lastSevenDays }` and `earlierWindowStart`) are gone. `EarlierRange` is now `EarlierToday` or `EarlierCustom(from, to)`, `earlierWindowStart` is `earlierWindowBounds` and `shotInWindow` also takes the end (start inclusive, end exclusive, both built with `DateTime(year, month, day + n)` as described here). The local-midnight rule and the London test run are unchanged.
- Decision: pure rules in `app/lib/domain/rules/`, with no platform code:
  - **Shooting date** (`clip_date.dart › shotAt(v, now)`): DATE_TAKEN (milliseconds) if plausible, else DATE_ADDED × 1000, else DATE_MODIFIED × 1000, else `now`. A date is plausible when it is ≥ 0 (1970-01-01, the epoch itself included) and ≤ `now` + `Duration(days: 1)`, exactly 24 hours after the UTC `now` given, both ends included. The two seconds fields are compared in seconds (against that bound divided by 1000, rounded down) before they are multiplied, so a huge value cannot overflow 64 bits and wrap around into a plausible date. The result is always UTC, also for a local `now`.
  - **New clip** (`media_clip.dart › newClipFrom(v, now, zoneId)`): `NewClip.mediaStore` with the video's volume and MediaStore id; fingerprint (size, duration or `0`, `shotAt`, display name); width and height or `0`; the mime type (the model already makes a missing one `video/*`); `generationAdded`; `timeZoneId` passed through verbatim, null included. `isUsable(v)` is `!isPending && (durationMs ?? 0) > 0`: a video still being recorded or without a length is never offered, matched or counted as shot.
  - **Same video** (`fingerprint_match.dart › sameVideo(f, v, now)`): the row's size is above 0 and equal to the fingerprint's, its duration (unknown counts as `0`) is equal, and `shotAt(v)` is the same instant as `dateTakenUtc` (`isAtSameMomentAs`, so a local `DateTime` of the same instant counts). The display name is not compared.
  - **After Android rebuilds its media library** (`matchAfterRebuild(stored, current, volumeName:, now:)`, returns `RebuildMatch(remap: clip id → new MediaStore id, lost: clip ids)`), one rebuilt volume at a time: (1) a clip's candidates are the usable rows *on `volumeName`* that are `sameVideo` (rows on other volumes are ignored); (2) with more than one, only those with the clip's display name stay; (3) a clip left with exactly one candidate claims it; (4) a row claimed by two or more clips goes to none of them; (5) every other clip is lost. Every clip passed in ends in exactly one of the two sets. Every stored clip must be on `volumeName`: a clip on another volume, or with no volume (a picked clip), throws an `ArgumentError` before anything is matched (Ruling L).
  - **Add earlier videos** (`earlier_window.dart`): `EarlierRange { today, lastTwoDays, lastSevenDays }`; `earlierWindowStart(r, nowLocal)` is local midnight of today, of yesterday or of six days ago, built as `DateTime(year, month, day - n)` in the phone's local time and returned in UTC; `shotInWindow(v, startUtc, now)` is `shotAt` not before the start (the start itself included) and `isUsable`.
  - **The daylight-saving tests run on every check:** the 2026-10-25 (25-hour) and 2027-03-28 (23-hour) cases in `earlier_window_test.dart` hold only in Europe/London, so they skip themselves, with a reason, in any other zone, and a plain `flutter test` passes anywhere. `tool/check` runs that file again as the step `daylight-saving tests (TZ=Europe/London)`, with `TZ=Europe/London` and `--run-skipped`. There they cannot skip: a first case asserts the zone really is London, so if `TZ` did not take effect, the step fails instead of passing silently. CI runs `tool/check`, so CI proves them too.
- Why:
  - Container metadata holds garbage: QuickTime's 1904 epoch, seconds read as milliseconds, camera clocks set years ahead. Such a date would sort a clip to the far end of To tag and of its reels (the Phase 3 item parked from D-57). DATE_TAKEN is when the video was shot, so it comes first; DATE_ADDED is when the row was created (the start or the end of the take, depending on the camera app); DATE_MODIFIED is the last resort. One day of slack above `now` allows a phone or camera clock that is a few hours ahead (a zone change, a wrong manual time) without accepting dates far in the future. `now` as the last resort keeps `dateTakenUtc` `NOT NULL` (D-57) with a value that sorts the clip where the user expects a new one.
  - A gallery app can rename a file; the size, duration and date stay. So the name is only a tie-break between rows that are otherwise identical, never a reason to match.
  - The worst bug TakeTray can have is showing the wrong video (spec §4). A fingerprint is not an identity: two takes can share size, duration and date. So whenever the rule cannot pick one row for certain (no candidate, two equally good candidates, or two clips pointing at one row) the clip is marked missing. A missing clip can be found again by hand; a wrong one silently replaces the user's take.
  - A size of 0 is how the Kotlin bridge reports a size Android did not know (`rowFrom`, null SIZE → 0; see "Kotlin side" in `docs/flow.md`), so two unknown sizes say nothing about the video.
  - The match runs for one named volume and only looks at that volume's rows, because `remap` carries only the new id: a clip on the phone's storage matched to the same id on an SD card would point at a different video. A stored clip from another volume is a caller's mistake, and it throws rather than ending in `lost`: a silent "lost" would mark a good clip missing.
  - Creators think "yesterday and today", not "the last 48 hours". Subtracting `Duration(days: n)` is wrong on a day when the clocks change: in London, 24 hours before midnight on 2026-10-26 is 01:00 on the 25th, not midnight (a mutation test proves the test catches it, and that only the London run does).
- Alternatives considered: no plausibility check (the 1904 and far-future dates above); a bound of exactly `now` (a clock a few minutes ahead would fall back to DATE_ADDED for every take); DATE_MODIFIED before DATE_ADDED (an edit or a metadata write changes it later); matching on the name too (every renamed clip would be lost); picking the "closest" of several candidates, or giving a doubly claimed row to the first clip (a guess, and the answer would depend on the order of the rows); comparing `DateTime`s with `==` (also compares the UTC flag, so a local `DateTime` of the same instant would not match); a 48-hour window; checking the daylight-saving cases only on a Mac set to London (CI runs in UTC, so they would only ever skip there).
- Consequences:
