  - A video whose dates are all implausible gets the detection time as its shooting date, and after a rebuild it can never be found again by fingerprint (it is lost).
  - A date that becomes plausible later (a camera clock more than a day ahead) changes `shotAt` once real time catches up, so after a rebuild such a clip can be lost. That is the safe direction.
  - Two identical takes (same size, duration and date) with the same name are both lost after a rebuild; with different names each is found.
  - The rules are only called from Tasks 6 to 9 (database writes, scanner, verifier, view models), which must keep their inputs as described: `matchAfterRebuild` gets only the clips of the rebuilt volume, named in `volumeName` (anything else throws), and every `now` comes from `clockProvider`.
  - The London step adds about three seconds to `tool/check` (3.4 s on the Mac). A new test with zone-specific cases must be added to `DST_TESTS` in `tool/check`; a listed file that does not exist fails the check at the start.
  - Zones whose clocks change at midnight itself (where local midnight does not exist on that day) are not tested; London changes at 01:00 UTC.

### D-70 · Each volume gets a plan: skip it, start from now, resync, or scan after its baseline; a pending video or a cut page holds the baseline
- Date: 2026-10-28 · Phase: 3 · Task: 5
- Amended by D-72: what holds the baseline is `holdsBaseline` (a pending row, or a finished row with no length or no size added at most three hours ago; it was ten minutes and length only until the final review, Ruling AL), and `advanceBaseline` takes `now`.
- Decision: `app/lib/domain/rules/baseline.dart`.
  - `sealed class VolumePlan` with `SkipVolume`, `StartFromNow`, `Resync` and `ScanAfter(generation)` (`ScanAfter` compares by value; the base is `@immutable`). `planVolume(baseline, current)` checks, in this order, and the first that applies wins: (1) the volume is not mounted (its version is null) → `SkipVolume`; (2) there is no baseline for it → `StartFromNow`; (3) the version differs from the baseline's (compared for equality only) → `Resync`; (4) the current generation is below the baseline's → `Resync`; (5) otherwise → `ScanAfter(baseline.generation)`.
  - `advanceBaseline(from, captured, seen, truncated = false)` gives the generation to store after a scan. `from` is the baseline the scan started from, `captured` the volume's generation read before the query, `seen` every row the scan returned (all pages, pending ones included), `truncated` whether the scan stopped before the end (Ruling K). Not truncated: with a pending row in `seen`, `max(from, oldest pending generationAdded − 1)`; otherwise `max(from, captured, every generationAdded in seen)`. Truncated: `captured` is ignored; with no rows, `from`; otherwise `max(from, highest generationAdded − 1)`, and with a pending row `max(from, min(oldest pending − 1, highest − 1))`, so the pending hold still applies. It never returns less than `from`.
  - `nextPageCursor(cursor, page)` gives the cursor for the next page of a scan that asked for rows added after `cursor` and got a full page back: `highest generationAdded − 1` when that is above `cursor`. It is null for an empty page, for a page whose rows all share one generation, and when the answer would not move past `cursor`. Null means the scan has stalled; the caller asks again with a bigger limit. (Ruling K's text returns `highest − 1` whenever it is above `cursor`; the one-generation check is added because such a page, from a cursor more than one below it, would otherwise come back unchanged on the next query and only stall one round later.)
- Why:
  - **Pending hold** (Review Focus 2): a camera app inserts its row, with IS_PENDING set, when a recording starts. GENERATION_ADDED is fixed at that insert and does not change when the recording finishes. If the baseline moved past it, the "added after the baseline" query would never return that video again, and it would never reach To tag. Holding just before the oldest pending row makes the next scan return it again; rows after it that were already saved come back too, and `addCheckedClips` finds them again and ignores them (it is idempotent, D-57).
  - **No baseline → from now, never "everything is new"**: after a backup import Shooting mode is off and the baselines are gone (D-59, D-64), and a volume can be mounted for the first time during a session. Treating every video on it as new would flood To tag with the user's whole library.
  - **Unmounted → skip, first**: an unmounted volume has no version, which would otherwise read as "changed" and start a resync, marking its clips missing while the SD card is only out of the phone (Review Focus 5).
  - **Truncated pages hold below the last generation** (Ruling K): MediaProvider gives every row written in one transaction the same generation, so many rows can share one. When a page with a row limit ends inside generation G, the rows at G that did not fit are unseen; a baseline at G would skip them forever. Holding at G − 1 brings them back. For the same reason the next page starts after G − 1, and a page that is all one generation cannot move the cursor at all. A pending row that never finishes holds the baseline, but the scan cursor is separate and still moves forward, so the rows after it are not stuck behind the same first page.
  - **Version for equality only**: since Android 16 the version string differs per app, so it means nothing but "changed" or "same" (the platform facts in the Phase 3 constraints). A generation that went backwards under the same version should not happen; if it does, nothing about the old numbers can be trusted, so it resyncs too.
- Alternatives considered: comparing generations across a version change (the numbering restarts, so new videos could be skipped or old ones re-added); moving the baseline to `captured` even with a pending row (the Review Focus 2 bug); holding at the pending row's generation instead of one before it (the query is `> baseline`, so that row would not come back); moving the baseline to `captured` or to the last generation of a full page (the rows of that generation that did not fit are skipped forever); paging with the baseline itself as the cursor (a stuck pending row would return the same first page on every scan); resyncing an unmounted volume (marks clips missing that are only on a removed card); an unordered set of checks (an unmounted volume with no baseline must still be skipped, not started).
- Consequences:
  - While a recording is pending, every scan sees the rows after it again; that is a few rows, and saving them again changes nothing.
  - Videos shot onto a volume before the first scan that saw it (with no baseline) are not detected; "Add earlier videos" (D-69) is the way to bring them in.
  - The scanner (Task 7) pages through a volume with a **scan cursor kept separate from the baseline**: it starts at the baseline, asks `videosAddedAfter(cursor, limit)`, and after each full page sets the cursor to `nextPageCursor`. Because the next page starts one generation back, rows can come back on two pages, so it de-duplicates rows by (volume, MediaStore id) across pages. When `nextPageCursor` is null it asks again from the same cursor with a bigger limit. A page shorter than the limit is the end. It calls `advanceBaseline` with all rows seen, and with `truncated: true` only if it stops before the end (a page or time budget); otherwise `truncated: false` with the `captured` generation.
  - Moving the stored baseline is compare-and-set in the repository (Task 6), so a stale scan cannot overwrite a newer session's baseline.

### D-71 · Phone state is one JSON value per key and never exported; the baseline moves by compare-and-set; a lost clip keeps its row with a negative id; an inbox-only missing clip leaves To tag; the zone timeline merges in time order
- Date: 2026-10-29 · Phase: 3 · Task: 6
- Amended by D-72 (rebuilt flag): `advanceBaseline` takes `rebuilt` (default false); with it, a lower generation under the same version may be written, for a resync after the generation went backwards. D-72 also adds a third phone-state key, `mediaVolumeVersions`, and makes the identity lookup of `addCheckedClips` find only MediaStore clips.
- Amended by the Phase 3 final review (Ruling AL): `ClipsRepository.detach` and `removeMissingFromLists`, below, were removed: nothing in `lib/` called them (the app uses `applyRebuildMatch`, `addCheckedClips` and `applyVerification`, which do what they did inside their own transactions). Their tests moved onto `applyVerification`. `recordVerification` went too. What they promised still holds, as listed in D-72's "One rule for leaving the lists".
- Amended by D-72 (Ruling Y): "an inbox-only missing clip leaves To tag" applies to every lost or detached clip, not only to `removeMissingFromLists`. `applyRebuildMatch` now takes a lost clip that is only in To tag or Skipped out of it (and sweeps it) in its own transaction; a lost clip in a reel still keeps its row and place, as below.
- Decision: all in `app/lib/data/repositories/`.
  - **`PhoneStateRepository`** (`phone_state_repository.dart`, keep-alive provider) keeps what belongs to this phone in the `settings` table under two keys, `shootingMode` and `mediaAccessWasFull`. Neither is in `SettingsRepository.exportedKeys`, and an import deletes every `settings` row (D-59), so a restore leaves Shooting mode off. `shootingMode` holds the whole `ShootingState` (`on`, `since`, the baselines by volume name, `earlierAdded`) as one JSON value; a missing row is `ShootingState.off`, and so is a value that is not JSON, is the wrong shape, or says `on` without a `since`, with only the key name logged. `turnShootingOn(baselines)` writes `since` = now and `earlierAdded: false`; when Shooting mode is already on it writes nothing and returns the current state. `turnShootingOff()` deletes the row. `markEarlierAdded()` sets the flag only while Shooting mode is on. `watchShooting()` emits distinct values. `accessWasFull()` and `markAccessWasFull()` keep a separate boolean that Shooting mode going off does not clear.
  - **`advanceBaseline(volume, expectedSession:, expected:, next:)` is a compare-and-set in one transaction.** It writes `next` only when Shooting mode is on with the same `since` as `expectedSession`, the stored baseline of `volume` equals `expected` (null means none stored), and, when `expected` and `next` have the same version, `next.generation >= expected.generation`. A different version is a resync, whose generations start over, so it may write a lower one. It keeps the other volumes and `earlierAdded`, and returns whether it wrote.
  - **`ClipsRepository`** gains `mediaStoreClipsOn(volumeName)` (MediaStore clips of the volume with `mediaStoreId > 0`), `applyRebuildMatch(volumeName, match)`, and, until the final review removed them (nothing called them), `detach(clipIds)` and `removeMissingFromLists(clipIds)`.
    - A clip that is **lost or detached keeps its row, its place (To tag, Skipped, its reels) and its fingerprint**, and gets `mediaStoreId = -clipId`, `missing = true` and `lastVerifiedAt` = now. The negative number never equals a real MediaStore id (those are positive, and a new id below 1 is refused), and it is unique per clip because the clip id is the primary key, so no two clips share it. The invariant is "a negative `mediaStoreId` is always minus its own clip id"; a backup import enforces it (below).
    - `applyRebuildMatch` runs in one transaction. Step 1 gives every clip in `remap` and `lost` the temporary id `-clipId`. Step 2 checks that no clip outside the match holds one of the new ids (`StateError`, and the transaction rolls back every change). Step 3 gives each `remap` clip its new id, `missing = false` and `lastVerifiedAt` = now. Lost clips are negated in step 1, together with the found ones, so a found clip may take the old id of a lost one. A match that puts a clip in both sets, gives two clips one new id or a new id below 1 is a programming error: `ArgumentError`, nothing changed. Inside the transaction it first selects the eligible clips: those in the match that are on this volume, with source `mediaStore` and `mediaStoreId > 0`. Steps 1 to 3 run on those only, and the clash check uses only their new ids. A clip that is already detached (a stale match), a picked clip, a clip of another volume and an unknown id are ignored, so a stale match cannot re-attach a detached clip. Detaching (also in `applyVerification` and `addCheckedClips`) touches only MediaStore clips.
    - Leaving the lists (first written as `removeMissingFromLists(ids)`, now the private step of `applyRebuildMatch`, `addCheckedClips` and `applyVerification`) deletes the `inbox` rows of those clips that are flagged `missing` (checked inside the same transaction, so a clip that is not flagged, such as a picked one or one that came back, keeps its `inbox` row), then runs `deleteOrphanClips`. A clip in a reel is never removed (its link and row stay, even if it also has an `inbox` row).
  - **`ZoneRepository.recordAll(entries)`** runs in one transaction: it reads the timeline, adds the entries (cut to milliseconds; an entry at an existing instant replaces it; for two new ones at one instant the later in iteration order wins), sorts by time, drops every entry whose zone equals the one before it, and rewrites the table, only when the result differs from what is stored. `record` is unchanged.
- Why:
  - **One key per state, so it changes atomically.** If Shooting mode, `since` and the baselines were separate rows, a crash or a second caller between two writes could leave Shooting mode on with no baselines, or baselines from an old session. One JSON value is one row, and one row is written or not.
  - **Compare-and-set** (Review Focus 4): a scan reads the state, queries MediaStore for a while, and then moves the baseline. In between, the user may turn Shooting mode off and on (a new session, new baselines), or a second scan may have moved the baseline. Writing blindly would put the old scan's number into the new session, and a clip shot while Shooting mode was off could then appear, or a baseline could move backwards. The three checks (same `since`, same stored baseline, never backwards within one version) make a stale scan write nothing; the transaction makes two scans with the same `expected` write exactly one.
  - **A negative id instead of deleting the row or keeping the old id** (Review Focus 3): the clip is in a reel or a list the user made, so its row must stay. But MediaStore numbers are reused: after a rebuild the old number can belong to another video. Keeping the number would make the lookup of `addCheckedClips` "find" the new video as the old clip (wrong video, the worst bug, spec §4). With `-clipId` the number is free, so the new video is a new clip. The `(volumeName, mediaStoreId)` unique key still holds, because `-clipId` is unique per clip.
  - **Temporary ids in `applyRebuildMatch`**: SQLite checks the unique key on every row it writes, so moving clip A to B's id while B still holds it fails, and a swap or a rotation would always fail. Moving everything to a free, negative id first makes the order of the final moves irrelevant. The clash check comes after that step, inside the transaction, so a clash found late undoes everything.
  - **An inbox-only missing clip leaves To tag** (on every path that loses or detaches a clip, D-72 Ruling Y): it is in no reel and its video is gone, so there is nothing to tag and nothing to open. A clip in a reel is part of the user's work and stays, flagged missing, so they can fix it.
  - **`recordAll` instead of `record` in a loop** (D-58): `record` prunes using only the entries around one instant, so entries that arrive out of order can drop a later observation. Merging everything in time order and pruning once cannot.
- Alternatives considered: separate rows for Shooting mode, `since` and each baseline (not atomic); moving the baseline unconditionally and checking Shooting mode before the scan only (a stale scan wins the race); deleting a lost clip's row (loses the user's tag); keeping the old MediaStore id on a missing clip (a different video could be matched to it); setting `mediaStoreId` to null for a lost clip (the `CHECK` on mediaStore clips refuses it); applying remaps one by one in the order given (a swap clashes); making the clash a plain unique-constraint failure instead of a `StateError` (a driver error with the values in its text, and no clear cause); deleting reel links of missing clips (the user's work); exporting the phone-state keys (the baselines belong to this phone's media library, D-64).
- Consequences:
  - A detached clip's old number is free, so a later `addCheckedClips` with it inserts a new clip. `mediaStoreClipsOn` leaves detached clips out, so the verifier does not look for them again; a missing clip that still holds its id (a backup row flagged missing) is still in it, so it can come back.
  - A backup holds negative `mediaStoreId`s as they are (a test round-trips one). Import checks them: a negative `mediaStoreId` must equal minus the clip's own `id`, anything else is `BackupProblem.invalidData` at `clips[n].mediaStoreId` (`backup_format.dart`), because another negative number could collide with a detached clip, and no TakeTray version writes one. After an import the clips are found again, if at all, by the verifier.
  - Leaving the lists sweeps every orphan clip, not only the ones passed in, because `deleteOrphanClips` is global (D-56); every writer already gives its clips a place in the same transaction, so there are none to lose.
  - The caller of `applyRebuildMatch` passes `matchAfterRebuild`'s result for clips from `mediaStoreClipsOn(volumeName)`; the repository does not check that the match covers every clip of the volume.
  - `recordAll` rewrites the whole `zone_timeline` table when it changes; the timeline has tens of rows.

### D-72 · Scan and verify: one serial media queue, coalesced scans, full access only, paging by a cursor bounded by the generation read first, the baseline moves only after the insert, a known number must be the same video, the verifier is rebuild-aware, an error never means "missing"
- Date: 2026-10-29 · Phase: 3 · Task: 7 (amended in its fix round, Rulings U to X)
- Amended by the final review (Ruling AL): (1) the scan and `addEarlier` run `ZoneSync.sync()` inside their queued job before they read the zone timeline (step 3 below), (2) "Add earlier videos" catches the volume's library version up like the scan (D-73), (3) `isUsable` and `holdsBaseline` also look at the size, and the late-metadata window is three hours (Ruling W below), (4) `detach`, `removeMissingFromLists`, `recordVerification` and `addDetectedClips` were removed as dead code, (5) a known gap is recorded (Consequences, I-3).
- Decision: four classes in `app/lib/data/media/` and one shared queue.
  - **`MediaWorkQueue`** (`media_work_queue.dart`, `mediaWorkQueueProvider`, keep-alive): `run(job)` starts a job only after every job queued before it has finished or failed. A job's error reaches only its own caller; the queue goes on. The scan, "Add earlier videos" (`countEarlier`, `addEarlier`, D-73) and the verifier (`verify`, `verifyAll`) all run on it, so none of them ever overlaps another.
  - **`LibraryResync`** (`library_resync.dart`, Ruling V): the one rebuild rule, used by both the scanner and the verifier from inside their own queued jobs (never enqueued itself). `resync(volume)`: `allVideos` → `matchAfterRebuild(mediaStoreClipsOn(volume), rows, volumeName: volume)` → `applyRebuildMatch` → `PhoneStateRepository.recordVolumeVersion(volume, version)`. `catchUp(volume, recorded)`: the version recorded for the volume equals the current one → nothing; none recorded → record the current one; another one → `resync`.
  - **Volume versions** (Ruling V): `PhoneStateRepository` keeps the MediaStore version the stored clips' numbers belong to, per volume, under the `settings` key `mediaVolumeVersions` (one JSON object). It is not exported, an import deletes it (like every `settings` row), and Shooting mode going off leaves it alone. Unreadable reads as empty, logging only the key.
  - **`MediaScanner.scan()`** (`media_scanner.dart`, `mediaScannerProvider`, keep-alive) returns `ScanShootingOff`, `ScanNoAccess` or `ScanDone(added, resynced, failedVolumes)`. **Coalescing** on top of the queue: the scanner keeps the scan that is queued but has not started; a call while one is queued gets that same future, a call while none is queued queues a new one. So calls during a running scan share exactly one follow-up run. One run:
    1. `loadShooting`; off → `ScanShootingOff` (the bridge is not called).
    2. `accessReport`; not `full` → `ScanNoAccess`, nothing written. Otherwise `markAccessWasFull`.
    3. Runs `ZoneSync.sync()` (a failure is logged by type and the scan goes on), so the zone changes the phone saw while the app was away are in the timeline before it is read (Ruling AL, I-2: without it a clip shot in a new zone was stored with the old one for good). `MediaLifecycle` still syncs first as well, because with Shooting mode off the scan does nothing and the timeline must still learn the zone; the second sync changes nothing that is stored. Then reads once: the watched folders, the zone timeline, `currentZoneId`, `now`, then `volumes()`. A `PlatformException` here is logged by its code only (never its message) and rethrown; no baseline or clip has been written.
    4. `loadShooting` again (M6): if Shooting mode is off, or its `since` changed while the phone answered, → `ScanShootingOff` and nothing more is written. Then the recorded volume versions are read once.
    5. Per volume, `planVolume(baseline, volume)` (D-70). `SkipVolume`: nothing (an SD card that is out keeps its baseline and version; its clips are untouched). `Resync`: `LibraryResync.resync`, then `advanceBaseline(expected: the stored one, next: (version, generation), rebuilt: true)`; no clip is added; if that write loses to an off/on, the remap stands. Otherwise, first `LibraryResync.catchUp` (a library rebuilt while Shooting mode was off, or since Shooting mode was switched on, is matched again before anything; the volume is then in `resynced`), then `StartFromNow`: `advanceBaseline(expected: null, next: (version, generation))`, or `ScanAfter(g)`: below.
    6. A `PlatformException` on one volume puts it in `failedVolumes` (logged by volume name and code). **Any other error inside one volume's work** (Ruling X: a database surprise, a bug) does the same, logged by volume name and the error's type only. Its baseline does not move, and the other volumes still run.
  - **`ScanAfter(g)`, the paging** (Ruling P, D-70): `captured` is the volume's generation from step 3. A scan cursor, separate from the baseline, starts at `g`; the limit starts at `pageSize` = 500. Each page is `videosAddedAfter(volume, cursor, folders, limit)`. Rows with `generationAdded > captured` are not kept, and a page that holds one is the last (those rows belong to the next scan). Other rows are kept once, by MediaStore id (later pages win). A page shorter than its limit is the last. After a full page, `cursor = nextPageCursor(cursor, page)` and the limit goes back to 500; when that is null (the page is stalled inside one generation) the same cursor is asked again with the limit doubled (500, 1000, …, `maxPageLimit` = 16000). Still stalled at 16000 → the scan stops there (`truncated`), logs a warning with the volume name and counts. Then the usable rows (`isUsable`: finished, with a length and a size above 0) become `newClipFrom(zoneId: zoneAt(timeline, shotAt) ?? currentZoneId)` and go to **`ClipsRepository.addCheckedClips`** (Ruling U): a stored MediaStore clip that holds a row's (volume, number) is found only if it is `sameVideo` with the row; otherwise it is detached (flagged missing, number `-clipId`; it leaves To tag and Skipped unless it is in a reel, Ruling Y) and the row is inserted as a new clip in To tag, all in the same transaction. `added` is the number inserted. **Only after that succeeds**, `advanceBaseline(from: g, captured, seen: all kept rows, now, truncated)` gives the new generation, written with compare-and-set against the stored baseline (and skipped when it equals it).
  - **Late metadata holds the baseline** (Ruling W, amended by Ruling AL): `holdsBaseline(v, now)` in `baseline.dart` is true for a pending row, and for a finished row whose duration is unknown or 0, or whose size is unknown or 0 (Kotlin maps a missing SIZE to 0, Ruling A), and whose DATE_ADDED is at most `lateMetadataWindow` (3 hours, inclusive; it was 10 minutes) before `now` and at most one day after it (D-69's plausibility bound; compared in seconds, so it cannot overflow). `advanceBaseline` holds just below the oldest such row, exactly as it did for pending rows (also when truncated). An older row without them is passed, as before. The scan cursor is separate from the baseline, so a held row never stops the paging: the rows after it are still read and saved in the same scan (a test holds a two-hour-old row below 1200 later ones).
  - **`MediaVerifier.verify(clips)` / `verifyAll()`** (`media_verifier.dart`, `mediaVerifierProvider`) return `VerifyOutcome(found, missing, skipped)` (clip ids; each clip in exactly one). Only the clip ids are taken from the caller: when the job runs, the clips are read again (`clipsByIds`), because a scan queued before it may have remapped or detached them. Steps: (1) `accessReport` not `full` → everything `skipped`, nothing written; (2) a picked clip, a detached one (`mediaStoreId <= 0`), one whose volume is not mounted or not listed, and an id that no longer exists are `skipped`; (3) per volume, `LibraryResync.catchUp` first (Ruling V): after a rebuild the clips are matched again by fingerprint before any number is looked up; a clip that match loses is `missing` (detached, and out of the lists unless it is in a reel, Ruling Y); the others are read again; (4) their numbers go to `videosByIds` in batches of 500: a returned row that is `sameVideo` → found; a returned row that is not → "other video" (counted as missing); no row → gone (missing). A `PlatformException` or any other error on a volume (Ruling X, logged by type) skips that volume's clips that were not already lost in a rebuild; (5) **one write**, `ClipsRepository.applyVerification(found, gone, otherVideo)` (M8): in one transaction it detaches the other-video clips, flags gone (missing) and found (not missing) with `lastVerifiedAt`, and takes gone and detached clips out of To tag and Skipped (the orphan sweep). A failing `accessReport` or `volumes()` throws before anything is written.
  - **`ZoneSync.sync()`** (`zone_sync.dart`, `zoneSyncProvider`): `pendingZoneChanges`, `currentZoneId`, then `recordAll(native entries + (now, current zone))`, and only after that `clearZoneChangesUpTo(the newest native atMs)`. With no native entries it clears nothing. Any failure throws, and the native list stays.
  - **One rule for leaving the lists** (Ruling Y, the D-71 rule "an inbox-only missing clip leaves To tag", stated once here): whenever a clip is detached or marked lost or gone, its `inbox` row (To tag or Skipped) is deleted, only if the clip is flagged `missing` (Ruling N's guard), and the orphan sweep runs, in the same transaction as the detach or the flag. A clip in a reel keeps its links and its row, flagged missing. The paths, each in one repository transaction:
    1. `applyRebuildMatch` (a rebuild's lost clips): the scanner's `Resync` plan and `LibraryResync.catchUp`, from both the scanner and the verifier;
    2. `addCheckedClips` (a stored clip that is another video, Ruling U): the scan and "Add earlier videos";
    3. `applyVerification` (the verifier's gone and other-video clips);
  - **`ClipsRepository`** gains `addCheckedClips(clips, sameVideo:)` (returns `DetectedClips(ids, inserted, detached)`), `mediaStoreClipsHolding(volume, numbers)` (the attached MediaStore clips holding them), `applyVerification`, `clipsByIds(ids)` and `allClipIds()`; reads by id or number ask in batches of 500. `addCheckedClips` finds a clip only among MediaStore clips (Ruling X). It is the only way detected clips are saved.
  - **Backup import** (`backup_format.dart`, Ruling X) refuses a picked clip with a `volumeName` or a `mediaStoreId`, and a detected clip with a `pickerUri` (`BackupProblem.invalidData` at that field).
  - **`PhoneStateRepository.advanceBaseline`** gains `rebuilt` (default false; Ruling T). With it, a lower generation under the *same* version may be written; the session and stored-baseline checks still apply. This amends D-71's "never backwards within one version" for the one case where it is wrong: `planVolume` resyncs a volume whose generation went backwards under the same version (D-70), and without it that baseline could never be written, so every later scan would resync again and, once the generation passed the old baseline, skip the videos in between forever.
  - Triggers (D-75): `MediaLifecycle` runs zone sync, the access check and the scan on the first frame and on resume; `ToTagController` scans and then verifies the waiting clips when To tag is opened and on resume; `verifyAll` is for Phase 10's "X of Y clips found" after an import.
- Why:
  - **One queue** (Ruling Q): a verify that read a clip's old number while a scan was remapping it after a rebuild would ask for the old number, find another video or none, and detach a clip that had just been found again. An "Add earlier videos" next to a scan could count a video the scan was adding. Serial jobs remove the whole class of race; scans are rare and short, so waiting costs nothing the user sees. Re-reading the clips inside the job closes the last gap (the caller's `Clip` objects are a snapshot).
  - **Coalescing**: app start, resume and opening To tag can fire together (Review Focus 4). Each must be answered by a scan that started after it, but three queued scans would do the same work three times. One queued follow-up that every call shares satisfies all of them.
  - **Full access only** (Review Focus 1): under limited access a query returns only the videos the user picked, so every other stored clip would look deleted and every new video would be invisible. The scan does nothing and moves no baseline; the verifier checks nothing and writes nothing.
  - **The baseline moves after the insert**: if it moved first and the insert failed (a full disk, a crash), the next scan would start past the videos and never see them again.
  - **The cursor is not the baseline, and a page ends inside a generation** (Ruling P, D-70): many rows can share one generation; starting the next page after the last row's generation would skip the rest of that generation forever, and a stuck pending row would hold the baseline (and the cursor, if they were one) at the same first page forever.
  - **Bounded by `captured`**: a scan that is still paging when Shooting mode goes off and on would otherwise pick up, on a later page, a video shot while it was off. The bound also ends paging on a phone that keeps recording during the scan. Rows past it are not lost: the baseline never passes `captured`, so the next scan returns them. The second `loadShooting` (M6) stops a scan whose session ended while the phone answered before it writes anything at all.
  - **A known number must be the same video** (Ruling U): a clip restored from a backup of another phone, or a missing clip, can hold a positive number this phone has not reached yet (numbers are only unique per library, and a new phone's library starts from 1). When the phone later gives that number to a new video, finding "the clip" by number alone would keep the old clip, add nothing, and move the baseline past the real video: lost for good. The fingerprint proves it is another video, so the old clip is detached (its old number can never match again) and the video becomes a new clip. Both in one transaction, so a crash cannot leave the old clip detached without the new one, or two holders of one number.
  - **The verifier is rebuild-aware** (Ruling V): after a rebuild (with Shooting mode off, or a verify queued before the scan that would resync), the old numbers either miss (an inbox clip would be deleted) or hit other videos (the clip detached), and fingerprint recovery would then be impossible. Recording the version the numbers belong to, per volume and whatever Shooting mode is doing, lets both jobs notice a rebuild and match by fingerprint first. The scan catches up too, because Shooting mode switched on after a rebuild takes the new version as its baseline and would otherwise never resync.
  - **Late metadata** (Ruling W): Android can finish a row (no longer pending) and fill in its duration a moment later. A row without a length (or, Ruling AL, without a size: `sameVideo` never matches a size of 0, so a clip saved with it would be removed by the next verify while the video is still on the phone) is not usable, and if the baseline passed it, it would never be seen again once the value arrived. The hold covers that; an older row without them is a broken file and must not hold the baseline for ever. The window was ten minutes. It is three hours because a camera app may insert its row when a recording starts, finished (not pending) but without a length until the recording ends, and only a hand check on the S24 could say which cameras do; three hours covers a long recording at the price of re-reading the rows after a broken file for three hours, which is cheap (saving them again changes nothing).
  - **One volume cannot stop the others** (Ruling X): a picked clip with a volume and a number (only a hand-made backup could make one) made `applyRebuildMatch` throw `StateError`; that aborted the whole scan, the baselines were never written, and every later scan hit the same error. Import now refuses such clips, the lookups only consider MediaStore clips, and any error is contained to its volume.
  - **An error never means "missing"**: a `videosByIds` that throws says nothing about the videos; marking them missing would take inbox clips out of To tag for good. A whole volume is skipped rather than half of it, so one outcome never mixes checked and unchecked clips of a volume. The verifier's writes are one transaction (M8), so a crash cannot leave a detached clip in To tag.
  - **Clear only after recording, only up to the native entries**: clearing first would lose the changes if `recordAll` failed; clearing up to `now` could drop a native entry recorded after the read. The native side forgets its last zone after a clear and records it again; `recordAll` drops the repeat, so the timeline does not grow.
- Alternatives considered: a mutex per class (a verify could still race a scan); dropping a scan call while one runs (the trigger would see stale results); running the verifier on the caller's `Clip` objects (stale after a remap); following the cursor rule of the plan ("after the last row's generation", which skips rows); no cap on the limit (one huge generation could make the native side return a list too big for a message); trusting rows past `captured` (the off/on race above); marking a failed volume's clips missing; making `ScanDone` the result of a `volumes()` failure (it would look like a scan that found nothing); trusting a known number without the fingerprint (the lost-video bug above); keeping the version only in the Shooting-mode baselines (gone while Shooting mode is off, exactly when a verify needs it); holding the baseline for every row without a length (one broken file would hold it for ever); letting a non-platform error abort the scan (one bad volume blocks all, for ever).
- Consequences:
  - `scan()`, `verify()` and the Add earlier calls can throw: a `PlatformException` before the volume loop, a database error outside one volume's work, or `StateError` from `addEarlier` without full access. Callers catch and log them (D-75); the next trigger tries again.
  - A generation with more than 16000 rows in the watched folders is scanned up to its first 16000 rows per scan; the baseline stays below it, so every later scan reads those 16000 again (and saves them again, which changes nothing) and never gets further. No real camera writes that many videos in one transaction.
  - A video is added by the scan that follows the one in which its row appeared past `captured`; on a phone that never stops recording that is one trigger later.
  - A finished video without a length or a size holds the baseline for up to three hours after it was added; rows after it are read again by every scan meanwhile (saving them again changes nothing). A recording longer than three hours whose row has no length is still missed.
  - **Known gap (final review I-3):** a rebuild resync that runs while Android is still re-indexing detaches the clips it cannot see yet. `allVideos` returns only what is indexed so far, so `matchAfterRebuild` loses the rest and `applyRebuildMatch` detaches them (`-id`, missing; inbox-only ones are deleted). A detached clip is never looked at again, so when the videos appear a minute later they come in as new clips: reel clips stay "missing" and the same videos return to To tag as duplicates. No file is touched. It needs a library reset and TakeTray opened within minutes. Recorded in `docs/later.md` for Phase 6; the idea is to re-match detached, missing clips by a unique fingerprint on a later resync or verify, and to decide which of the two rows survives.
  - The verifier detaches a clip whose number holds another video (D-71), and its old number can become a new clip. The scan and "Add earlier videos" do the same to a stored clip that holds a row's number but is another video (Ruling U). In a reel such a clip stays, flagged missing; only in To tag or Skipped it is gone (Ruling Y). So an original edited in place (another size or length under the same number) stays in its reel as missing, and comes back to To tag as a new clip.
  - After a rebuild the verifier reads `allVideos` of the volume once (one full read per rebuild). A clip the match loses is reported missing; in a reel it stays, flagged; only in To tag or Skipped it is gone.
  - No clip that a scan or a verify has found lost or detached waits in To tag or Skipped. A missing clip can still be in To tag when it arrived that way (a backup row flagged missing); the next verify of it takes it out if its video is still gone.
  - A resync matches only finished rows (`allVideos` leaves pending ones out, M9): a video still being recorded during a resync is passed by the resync's baseline (the current generation) and is never added.
  - A generation that is only transiently low under the same version (if MediaStore ever reported one) would also resync and, with `rebuilt: true`, write that lower baseline (M7); the next scan would then see the rows above it again (saving them again changes nothing). Task 11 checks on the S24 that this does not happen.
  - `matchAfterRebuild` and `applyRebuildMatch` are only given `mediaStoreClipsOn(volume)` (every attached MediaStore clip of that volume), so every such clip ends found or lost.
  - Logs carry volume names, generations, counts, error codes and error types only: never a display name, a folder, a URI, an error message or a MediaStore version ("version changed" or "generation went back").

### D-73 · Add earlier videos (owner request): a card link and a To tag menu item, calendar days in local time, never a known clip, never a baseline, full access only
- Date: 2026-10-29 · Phase: 3 · Task: 7
- Amended by D-90: there is no one-use mark any more. `markEarlierAdded` and `ShootingState.earlierAdded` are gone, so point 5's `addEarlier` writes nothing to the Shooting state, and the card link is shown whenever access is full, with Shooting mode on or off, and stays after an add (the Placement line below read "hidden once `earlierAdded` is set").
- Amended by D-78: the fixed windows "Last 2 days" and "Last 7 days" are gone. `EarlierRange` is now `EarlierToday` or `EarlierCustom(from, to)`; `earlierWindowStart` is `earlierWindowBounds` (start inclusive, end exclusive); `countEarlier()` returns the Today count only, and `countEarlierIn(range)` and `addEarlier(range)` take a window. The query is one `videosShotSince(folders, start)` per volume, narrowed by the end bound in Dart, so points 2 and 4 below read that way. Points 1, 3 and 5 and the rules that follow them (full access only, known clips excluded, no baseline) are unchanged. Everything below is as it was written on 2026-10-29, before D-78.
- Amended by the final review (Ruling AL): the three media steps of D-72 apply here too. `_earlierVideos` runs `LibraryResync.catchUp` for each mounted volume before it compares held numbers, so the volume's library version is recorded when none is (a rebuild right after the first clips came from Add earlier is then noticed, and the stored clips are matched first after a rebuild, so nothing is added twice), and `addEarlier` runs `ZoneSync.sync()` before it reads the zone timeline. A failed `catchUp` leaves that volume out, like a failed query. Counting therefore may record a version; it adds no clip. `isUsable` also needs a size above 0.
- Decision: `MediaScanner.countEarlier()` returns the count per `EarlierRange` (`today`, `lastTwoDays`, `lastSevenDays`); `addEarlier(range)` adds that range and returns the new clips' ids (for Undo). Both run on the media queue (D-72).
  1. Full access, or nothing: `countEarlier` returns zeros and `addEarlier` throws `StateError`, and neither reads a video.
  2. On every mounted volume, one `videosShotSince(folders, earlierWindowStart(lastSevenDays, now))`; a volume whose query throws is left out (logged by name and code).
  3. The rows are narrowed with `shotInWindow` (finished, with a length, shot at or after the start), and every video that a stored MediaStore clip holding its number *is* (`mediaStoreClipsHolding`, then `sameVideo`, D-72) is dropped: a clip in To tag, Skipped or a reel never comes back or moves. A stored clip that holds the number of another video does not hide it.
  4. Each range is the same list narrowed with its own `earlierWindowStart` (local midnight of today, of yesterday, of six days ago, D-69).
  5. `addEarlier`: nothing to add → `[]`, nothing written. Otherwise `addCheckedClips` with the same check (zone from the timeline at the shooting time, or the zone now; a stored clip that is another video is detached, D-72), then `markEarlierAdded` (which does nothing while Shooting mode is off). It returns the inserted ids, which are exactly what `countEarlier` counted for that range.
  Baselines are never read or written.
- Placement (Tasks 9 and 10): a link on the Shooting mode card, hidden once `earlierAdded` is set for the session, and an item in To tag's menu.
- Why: turning Shooting mode on starts from now (D-70), so videos shot just before it, or before an SD card was first seen, are never detected. The owner wants a way to bring them in on purpose. Calendar days match how a creator thinks ("today", "yesterday and today"), and the window is computed in local time, so midnight and daylight-saving days are right (tests at 00:05 and on both 2026-10-25 and 2027-03-28 in London, run in `tool/check`'s London step). Never touching a baseline keeps the scan's guarantees (D-70, D-72) independent of this feature.
- Alternatives considered: asking at every turn-on ("add today's videos too?") (a question each time, mostly answered no); a setting (hidden, and a permanent behaviour for a one-off need); hours instead of calendar days (wrong across midnight and daylight saving); moving the baseline back (the scan would then also re-add videos the user had skipped and deleted).
- Consequences: an added video counts as a new clip in To tag; a later scan does not add it again (the identity is known). A video outside the watched folders is never offered. A video on a volume that does not answer is not counted, so the number shown can be lower than what is on the phone until the next count.

### D-74 · Thumbnails: a disk cache keyed by video identity and size, written by temp-and-rename, 64 MB, pruned at start; the image provider reports "no picture" as an image error
- Date: 2026-10-29 · Phase: 3 · Task: 8
- Decision: `ThumbnailCache(bridge, dir, {maxBytes = 64 MB})` (`lib/data/media/thumbnail_cache.dart`, `thumbnailCacheProvider`, a `Future` because the folder, `getApplicationCacheDirectory()/thumbs` in `thumbnailCacheDirProvider`, comes from `path_provider`) sits in front of `MediaBridge.thumbnail`. `ClipThumbnailImage` (`lib/features/clips/widgets/clip_thumbnail_image.dart`) is the `ImageProvider` the tiles use.
  1. **The key** is `ClipThumbnailKey`: volume name, MediaStore number, the fingerprint's `sizeBytes`, the fingerprint's `dateTakenUtc` in ms since the epoch, and the size in pixels. The file is `<volume>_<mediaStoreId>_<sizeBytes>_<dateTakenMs>_<sizePx>.jpg`. Any character of the volume name outside `A-Z a-z 0-9 _ -` becomes `_` in the file name only (the bridge gets the real name), so `../x` cannot leave the folder. The key does not hold the clip's row id: two rows of one video share a picture. Android renumbering a video, or giving a number to another video, changes `sizeBytes` or `dateTakenMs` or the number itself, so a picture is never shown for the wrong video (Review Focus 5).
  2. **`load(clip, sizePx)`** returns null, without asking the bridge, for a clip that is not a MediaStore clip, has a number of 0 or less (a detached clip), has no volume, or for `sizePx` below 1. A larger `sizePx` than 4096 counts as 4096 (the bridge's limit), before the key is built. The bridge is asked for a square, `sizePx` by `sizePx`.
  3. **Disk first**: a file that is there is returned and its modified time is set to `clock.now()` (so pruning removes what was used longest ago). A file that does not start with the JPEG marker `FF D8` (empty, one byte, or foreign) is not a picture: it is deleted and the bridge is asked. A read that fails (the folder or the file is gone) is a miss.
  4. **Then the bridge.** Each bridge call is limited to 15 seconds (`defaultBridgeTimeout`); a call that has not answered by then gives null, writes nothing, ends the load for everyone sharing it, and logs `No thumbnail from volume <name>: timeout` (an answer that arrives later is ignored). Null or empty bytes, a `PlatformException` (the video is gone, access is refused, the volume is not mounted: `native-error`) or any other `Exception` give null and nothing is written. The log line is `No thumbnail from volume <name>: <code or exception type>`: never a path, a display name or an error message (D-10).
  5. **Written by temp and rename**: the folder is created if it is missing (Android may delete a cache folder while the app runs), the bytes go to `<name>.jpg.tmp`, then the file is renamed to `<name>.jpg`. A kill between the two leaves a `.tmp` and no real name, so a half picture is never read. A write that fails with an `Exception` (disk full) is logged by volume and exception type, the temp file is removed, and the bytes are still returned: only the cache entry is lost.
  6. **Loads of one key at the same time share one bridge call** (and one disk read). The entry is removed when the load ends, so a failure is not remembered: the next call asks again.
  7. **`prune()`** keeps the total size of the `.jpg` files at or under `maxBytes` by deleting the oldest (modified time, ties by name) first. Exactly `maxBytes` deletes nothing. It also deletes every `.tmp` file, which are not counted, except the temp files this cache is writing at that moment (a set of paths, so a half-way write is never deleted). Files with other names, and sub-folders, are left alone. A missing folder is fine. `MediaLifecycle` calls it once at app start (D-75); nothing prunes during a session, so the folder can pass the cap until the next start.
  8. **`ClipThumbnailImage(clip, logicalSize, cache)`**: the key is made at `logicalSize * devicePixelRatio` (rounded; a missing ratio counts as 1), and the picture is drawn with scale `sizePx / logicalSize`, so it fills `logicalSize`. Two providers are equal for the same volume, number, size, date taken and `logicalSize`; their keys are equal when the pixel size is the same too. When there is no picture (null from `load`, or a clip with no key), the stream gets an error, which an `Image` shows through its `errorBuilder`: the tile's placeholder. The error is silent (not in the log). When bytes were found but the decoder refused them (a damaged file, or bad bytes from the bridge that were written), the provider first calls `ThumbnailCache.discard(clip, sizePx)`, which deletes that key's file (a no-op when there is no file, no key, or a load of that key is running right now: that load may have just written a fresh file, and if its bytes are bad too its own tile discards them). Without it a file that does not decode would be read, touched and shown as the placeholder by every tile for as long as the app runs, because a read refreshes the modified time and nothing prunes during a session. The provider also asks Flutter's image cache to forget a failed key (`ImageCache.evict`, as `NetworkImage` does): Flutter otherwise keeps a failed load as "pending" for that key and replays the error to every later tile, so a thumbnail that failed once would never be asked for again.
- Why: a picture of the wrong video is a form of the worst bug (spec §4), and showing the same picture again and again must not need the phone (a list of 100 tiles, scrolled). The fingerprint fields in the name are the same facts the matching rules trust (D-69, D-72). Temp and rename is the standard way to make a file appear whole or not at all. Sharing in-flight loads matters because a tile is rebuilt often and the bridge call (a native decode) is the slow step. 64 MB is room for over a thousand small JPEGs and small for a phone.
- Alternatives considered: a database table of thumbnails (blobs in SQLite; more to migrate and back up, no gain); keying by the clip's row id (a renumbered video would keep its row and picture, but a detached or re-added clip would not); keying by number alone (the wrong-video bug); pruning after every write (many directory scans while scrolling); `package:path` for names (not a dependency, and the names are ours); decoding in Dart from a content URI (needs a read permission path the bridge already owns); keeping the failed completer in Flutter's image cache (the bug above).
- Consequences: Android clears the cache folder under storage pressure; the cache then fills again from the bridge. A bad file on disk that has bytes but does not decode (disk corruption) shows the placeholder once, is discarded, and is fetched again by the next tile; a file with a JPEG marker and a damaged body is only found when the decoder fails. A discard can delete a good file in a rare race (a tile's decode fails on bad bytes while a second load, started after the file was removed, has written a good one); the cost is one more bridge call. The bridge's bytes are not checked for the marker before they are written: the decoder is the judge. A hung bridge call costs a tile its picture after 15 seconds, and the abandoned call may still finish on the Kotlin side. Two volume names that differ only in characters outside the allowed set would share file names (Android's names are `external_primary` and a hex id such as `1234-5678`, so this does not happen). `thumbnailCacheProvider` is asynchronous, so a screen waits for it before it can build a `ClipThumbnailImage`. A log line is written for every thumbnail that cannot be had (info level), which for an unmounted volume is one per visible tile each time the list is built.

### D-75 · The access banner rule and the Shooting-on flow; start and resume run zone sync, then access, then scan, one run at a time; every media call from a view model is guarded; Undo for "Add earlier videos" only takes back clips nobody touched
- Date: 2026-10-29 · Phase: 3 · Task: 9
- Decision: four view models under `lib/features/*/view_models/` and the widget `MediaLifecycle` (`lib/app/media_lifecycle.dart`, in `TakeTrayApp` above the router).
  1. **Banner rule** (`bannerFor(level, wasFull)`): full → `none`; limited → `manual` (even if access was full before: the user chose to share fewer videos); denied with `mediaAccessWasFull` → `lost`; denied without it → `manual`. Any read of the access that sees full (the build, `refresh`, `allow`) stores `mediaAccessWasFull`, so a later loss, also one that happened while the app was in the background, shows as `lost` after the next resume.
  2. **`MediaAccessController`** (keep-alive, `AsyncNotifier<MediaAccessView(level, banner, canAskAgain)>`): `allow()` reads a fresh `accessReport()` (the screen's copy may be old), then: full → show and scan; `canAskAgain` or limited (limited can always be widened, D-68) → `requestAccess()`, where a full answer stores was-full and starts one scan, and a limited or denied answer is only shown; else `openAppSettings()`. A second request while one waits fails with `busy` (D-68): logged by type, state unchanged. `refresh()` re-reads; an error keeps the last view (an `AsyncError` only if there never was one); when several calls overlap, only the one that started last writes the state, and a first read that fails late does not replace a view that a refresh or an allow already published (the first read hands that view back). A later `refresh` or `allow` turns a first-read error into a view.
  3. **`ShootingModeController`** (keep-alive, state = `watchShooting`): `setOn` calls are chained, so rapid taps end in the last requested state. On: `askIfDenied()` (a request only if denied and Android would still ask); baselines `{volumeName: VolumeBaseline(version, generation)}` for mounted volumes only (`version != null`), `{}` if `volumes()` throws (the next scan starts each volume from now, D-70); `turnShootingOn` (already on: the first session stays, D-71); an unawaited scan. It turns on whatever the permission answer was: without full access the scan does nothing and the banner says so. Off: `turnShootingOff`, To tag untouched. `openCamera()` is false when no camera app opened or the call failed. No `setOn` throws.
  4. **`ToTagController`** (auto-dispose, state = `watchInbox(pending)`): each build scans, then verifies the waiting clips, once; `onResume()` does both again. The two steps are independent and neither can fail the list.
  5. **`EarlierVideosController`** (a codegen `@riverpod` class, auto-dispose, Ruling D): the state is `countEarlier()`, read when the sheet opens; it works with Shooting mode off (D-73). A failed count is an `AsyncError`. `add(range)` and `undo(ids)` rethrow after logging the type (the sheet must catch and tell the user), and recount on success. (Amended by D-78: the state is Today's count, an `int`; `countIn(range)` also rethrows and leaves the state alone.)
  6. **Undo** (Ruling AC): `ClipsRepository.undoEarlierAdd(Set<int>)`, in one transaction, deletes the `inbox` row of each id that is still `pending` and in no reel, then runs the orphan sweep, and returns the number of clips removed (the ids are taken in batches of 500 inside the one transaction). It runs on `MediaWorkQueue`, so never beside a scan. Not the missing-clip removal (`applyVerification`): it removes only clips flagged missing, so Undo would silently do nothing.
  7. **`MediaLifecycle`**: on the first frame and on every resume, zone sync, then the access refresh, then the scan; each step runs even if the one before it failed. The first frame also awaits `thumbnailCacheProvider.future` and calls `prune()` once per app start (once per mount of the widget), never on resume. A trigger during a run (a resume during the start) sets a flag and costs exactly one more run after it, so runs do not overlap.
  8. **Errors** (Ruling AD): every call from a view model or the lifecycle to `scan`, `verify`, `countEarlier`, `addEarlier`, `undoEarlierAdd`, `prune` and `ZoneSync.sync` is wrapped by `guarded` (`lib/core/guarded.dart`) or the same catch. It catches anything thrown (an `Error` too: `ThumbnailCache.load` can let one through) and logs `<step> failed: <ErrorType>`, never the message or a stack trace, which may hold a path (D-10). `retry` is off for these providers (`noRetry`), because Riverpod 3 would otherwise rebuild a failed one with a growing delay.
- Why: the banner has to tell "you never gave access" from "you had it and lost it" (Review Focus 1), which only works if full access is remembered the first time any code sees it. The scan has to see a fresh zone timeline (the shooting zone of a new clip comes from it, D-69) and the current access (a scan without full access does nothing), hence the order. One run at a time keeps two zone syncs from racing, while the scanner and the queue already prevent parallel scans. Guarding every call keeps a bridge or database error from becoming an uncaught async error (which in a release build is a problem report at best, and would skip the steps after it), and the next trigger tries again. Chaining `setOn` makes the switch deterministic however fast it is tapped.
- Alternatives considered: awaiting the scan in `allow()` and `setOn()` (the button would wait for a scan); marking was-full only when the user taps Allow (a grant in the phone's settings would never be remembered); a new zone sync and scan for every resume (they would race); letting the UI catch errors from the triggers (it would have to know every one); an Undo that deletes by id alone (it would delete a clip the user tagged in the meantime).
- Consequences: the Shooting card's switch shows what is stored, so a turn-on that failed (a database error) simply stays off, with a log line and no message to the user. `add` and `undo` throw, so Task 10's sheet must catch. `allow()` and `setOn(true)` return before their scan ends, so a test or screen that needs the result waits on the list, not on the call. The prune can be missed if the app is killed before the first frame; it runs at the next start. `undoEarlierAdd` runs the global orphan sweep (as every sweep does, D-56), so it would also remove a clip that was already in no reel and no list, which no code path leaves behind. A LIMITED "Allow" keeps calling `requestAccess` with no fallback to the settings page (Ruling AE): if Android shows no dialog again, a dismissed dialog would otherwise send the user to Settings every time. Task 11's test on the S24 checks that One UI shows the dialog again for a limited grant.

### D-76 · The first To tag screen and the Shooting card's place before Phase 4/5; the access banner is one widget; Add earlier videos is a card row, a menu item and a sheet that reports to the screen, not to itself
- Date: 2026-10-29 · Phase: 3 · Task: 10
- Decision: the screens that show the Task 9 view models (D-75), built only from `taketray_ui` components and tokens.
  1. **Files.** `features/media_access/views/media_access_banner.dart` (`MediaAccessBanner`), `features/shooting_mode/views/shooting_mode_section.dart` (`ShootingModeSection`) and `shooting_time_text.dart` (`shootingTimeText`), `features/tagging/views/to_tag_screen.dart` (`ToTagScreen`) and `add_earlier_videos.dart` (`showAddEarlierVideos`, the sheet), `features/clips/widgets/clip_thumbnail.dart` (`clipThumbnail`), and `features/tagging/view_models/pending_clips.dart` (`pendingClipsProvider`, `inShootingOrder`, `latestPending`). `ToTagScreen` replaces `to_tag_placeholder_screen.dart` (deleted) in the router. `ReelsPlaceholderScreen` keeps its name until Phase 4 and now holds `ShootingModeSection` under the large app bar, with `TtNavPill.reservedHeight` of room below.
  2. **The banner** (`MediaAccessBanner`) is `TtBanner` with an outlined icon (`iconFilled: false`: a status, not an action): `AccessBanner.manual` is `info` with a lock, "Manual mode", "New clips aren't found automatically." and the text button "Allow full access"; `AccessBanner.lost` is `warning` with `no_photography`, "TakeTray can't see your videos", "Allow access to find new clips." and "Allow". Both buttons call `MediaAccessController.allow()`. `none`, loading and an `AsyncError` (a failed first read) show nothing: no banner is better than a wrong one. It follows the state, so it goes away when access becomes full. **Layout:** the brief said the banner goes below the card on the Reels tab; the prototype's `routes.home` puts `accessBanner(s)` before `shootingCard(s, u)`, so it is **above** the card (the design wins for layout, the brief for text). On To tag it is above the strip, as in `routes.totag`.
  3. **The card** is `TtShootingModeCard`, with 4 dp above and 8 dp below and the gutter at the sides (`.shoot { margin: 4px 16px 8px }`). It is not drawn until the stored Shooting mode has been read (so it never flashes "off"); if that read fails it shows "off", and the switch still works. Off: "Shooting mode" and "Off. Turn on before you start filming, and new videos wait in To tag." On: "Shooting mode on", "On since {time}" (or "On since {time} · Manual mode: add clips yourself" when access is known and not full; unknown access says nothing about Manual mode), the **Open camera** button, and the backdrop (`latestPending`: the last waiting clip in shooting order; when nothing waits, the first stored clip that is not missing, lowest id, as `screens.js` does with `Object.values(s.clips).find(c => !c.missing)` (Ruling AI; a clip with no picture to ask for, a picked or detached one, is not chosen, `watchFirstShowableClip`); none, so plain black, if there is no such clip). The card takes its rows only when on, so "N clips to tag · Tag now" (plural key; opens To tag with `context.go`) shows only then. **The attention banner** (Ruling AH) sits below the card, as in `routes.home` (access banner, card, attention banner): a `TtBanner` in the attention style with the filled `sell` icon and a chevron, shown only while Shooting mode is off and at least one clip waits. Title `clipsWaitingTitle` ("N clips to tag", plural), body `clipsWaitingBody` ("{duration} of video waiting"). The duration is the prototype's `fmt.mins` (`waitingDurationText`, in `waiting_duration_text.dart`, ARB keys `durationSeconds`, `durationMinutes`, `durationHoursMinutes`): under 60 s whole seconds, rounded ("45 s"; "0 s" for none); otherwise minutes rounded while under 60 ("3 min"); otherwise "1 h 5 min". The roundings are the prototype's, edges included: 59.5 s reads "60 s" (the seconds are rounded before the unit is chosen) and 59.5 min reads "1 h 0 min"; half rounds up. The total is the sum of every waiting clip's length, as `q.total` does and as the title counts every waiting clip. Tapping it opens To tag (`context.go`). It never shows with the card's own strip: Shooting mode on has the strip, off has the banner.
  4. **`{time}`** (`shootingTimeText`, Ruling AF, changed by Ruling AG): `since` and `now` (`clockProvider`) are converted to local time. The same calendar day: the time of day alone. Any other day: the date and the time (`MMMd`, a comma, the time: "Oct 27, 9:41 PM"), because a time alone is ambiguous after midnight. Local midnight today is today; 23:59 yesterday is not. **The phone's 24-hour setting is followed** (`MediaQuery.alwaysUse24HourFormatOf(context)`, which the Shooting card, To tag's strip and the grid all read, so a change redraws them): 12-hour phones get `DateFormat.jm` ("9:41 AM", "Oct 27, 9:41 PM"), 24-hour phones `DateFormat.Hm` ("09:41", "Oct 27, 21:41"). This overrides Ruling AF's literal `jm`, which would have shown "9:41 PM" in TakeTray and "21:41" everywhere else on such a phone. One helper, `timeOfDayText(time, locale:, use24Hour:)`, writes every time of day (the card, both strips, the tile's time and its spoken label "Clip, {time}, {duration}"; amended by D-83: the tile and its label now use `wallClockTimeText`, which shares the same private formatter and the same 24-hour rule but does not convert to the phone's zone); `shootingTimeText` adds the day rule on top. The locale is the app's (`Localizations.localeOf`), not the raw phone locale: `intl` has data for many locales, but Flutter's localization delegate loads the date data only for the locales the app supports, and `DateFormat` throws for one that is not loaded (the app is English only for now). The text is built from `now` at build time (amended by D-86: `localDayProvider` rebuilds the card and To tag's strip at each local midnight).
  5. **To tag** (`ToTagScreen`): a large "To tag" app bar; the banner; a `TtStatStrip` ("Shooting since {time}" with a text **Open camera** button and a live record dot while on; "Shooting mode off" with **Turn on** when off and clips wait; none when off and empty); then the clips or the empty state. Clips are a lazy `SliverGrid.builder`, three columns, 4 dp gaps, 16 dp gutters, `TtClipTile` 3:4, oldest first by `dateTakenUtc` then id (`inShootingOrder`), the time of day (`timeOfDayText`, in the phone's time zone, not the zone the clip was shot in; **amended by D-83:** the tile now shows the time where the clip was shot, `wallClockTimeText` of `toTagTimesProvider`'s `ClipLocalTime`, with the same 24-hour rule, and the phone's time only until that is known or when it cannot be read) and the length (`m:ss`, `h:mm:ss` from an hour, rounded to the second) on each tile, spoken as "Clip, {time}, {duration}". 500 clips build a screenful of tiles, not 500. The picture is asked for at the tile's height. While the list (or the Shooting state) loads, six skeleton tiles; if the list stream itself fails, they stay (there is no designed error state and no copy for one). A clip flagged missing is a `TtMissingClipTile` ("Missing from phone", no Remove button): the verifier already takes missing clips that are only in To tag out of the list (D-72), so this is a safety net. Empty: **All caught up** (Shooting mode on; **Open camera**) or **Nothing to tag** (off; **Turn on Shooting mode**). Tapping a tile does nothing yet. Not built: scenes, the summary row, Start tagging, Skipped (Phase 5), Add videos (Phase 7).
  6. **When To tag checks the phone.** Watching `toTagControllerProvider` scans and verifies when the screen is first built (D-75). The shell keeps a tab built after its first visit, so the screen also calls `onResume()` when the app comes back to the front while its tab is showing, and when its tab is selected again (`TickerMode` goes from off to on; go_router turns the ticker of a hidden tab off). The Reels tab never takes the place of this: the card reads the waiting clips through `pendingClipsProvider`, a plain `watchInbox(pending)` that does not scan or verify. (Had the card watched `ToTagController`, the controller would be built at app start and opening To tag would no longer trigger the verify.)
  7. **Add earlier videos.**
     - **Where:** a `TtCardRow` in the card's `referencesRow` slot (now the `rows` list, D-79; the history icon, "Add earlier videos", a chevron), shown only when Shooting mode is on, access is full and the session has not used it (`earlierAdded`); and the **⋮ menu item** of To tag's app bar, shown with full access whether Shooting mode is on or off (D-73) and not otherwise (the menu button is not drawn at all when it would be empty). Both appear and go without a restart as access and the session change. The References row of Phase 4/5 will need the slot too; that phase decides how the two share the card.
     - **The sheet** (amended by D-78: it has two rows, Today and "Choose dates…", not three; see D-78; the rows, skeleton and disabled look below are replaced by D-79: `TtSkeletonCount` 48 x 16 dp, `TtListRow.off` with no opacity, and `TtMetrics.listRowDisabledOpacity` is deleted) is `TtSheet` with a header drawn by the sheet's own child (the title, then the line "From your camera folders. Clips you already tagged or skipped stay where they are." 4 dp below it, as `.sh-head` has; `TtSheet.title` has no second line), then three `TtListRow`s: Today, Last 2 days, Last 7 days, each with the trailing count ("none", "1 new", "N new"). While the counts load the rows are there with a `TtSkeletonLine` for the count (`TtMetrics.skeletonCountWidth`, 56 dp, added to `taketray_ui`), announced "Today, Loading" (ARB key `loading`), and cannot be tapped. If the count fails: "Couldn't check your videos." and a **Try again** button (invalidates the controller; the sheet shows the skeletons again). A row with 0, and every row while an add runs, is disabled: dimmed to `TtMetrics.listRowDisabledOpacity` (0.45, `.li.dis`, added to `taketray_ui`) and announced disabled; a second tap while an add runs is ignored (`_adding` is set before the first await). An add that finds nothing (the videos went away since the count) leaves the sheet open; the controller counted again, so the row is now disabled.
     - **Outcome:** `showAddEarlierVideos(context)` shows the sheet and shows its snack on the screen that opened it. Added: "{count, plural, one{1 clip added to To tag} other{{count} clips added to To tag}}" with the check icon and **Undo**, where the count is the number of ids `add` returned. Undo calls `EarlierVideosController.undo(ids)`; the controller is kept alive until the snack closes. After the snack has closed there is no button, so nothing can happen. The snack is shown even when the sheet was dragged away while the add was running (the add itself is not cancelled).
     - **Errors** (Ruling AF; both new copy, both ARB keys): `add` fails → the sheet closes and the snack says "Couldn't add videos. Try again." (a snack shown over an open sheet would be behind its blurred scrim); `undo` fails → "Couldn't undo. Try again.". Both are caught, logged by type by the controller, and nothing is thrown. Other new copy: `tryAgain` ("Try again"), `earlierCountFailed`, `undo`, `moreOptions` (the ⋮ button's label), `clipMissing`, `clipTileSemantics`, `loading`, and for the Reels banner `clipsWaitingTitle`, `clipsWaitingBody`, `durationSeconds`, `durationMinutes`, `durationHoursMinutes`.
  8. **Pictures.** `clipThumbnail(clip, logicalSize, cache)` gives a `ClipThumbnailImage` once `thumbnailCacheProvider` (a `Future`) has a value, and until then a picture that fails at once, which `TtClipTile` shows as its placeholder. `MediaLifecycle` already prunes; the screens do not.
  9. **Tests.** `app/test/flutter_test_config.dart` loads the fonts the app declares (Plus Jakarta Sans), because Flutter's tests otherwise draw every letter as a square of the font size and a layout check ("nothing overflows at 2x text, 360 dp") would measure text about twice as wide as the real thing; the banner test with "Allow full access" overflowed because of it. `test/support/pump_screen.dart` pumps a screen with the theme, the localizations, a router, a text scale and "remove animations" on (the record dot and the skeleton loop for ever, and `pumpAndSettle` would never settle). `test/support/test_app.dart › testApp` ends a whole-app test by taking the tree down and moving the test clock once, because closing a drift stream schedules a zero-delay timer that fails a test that ends with it pending; the clean-up never hides the body's own failure (the body's error is rethrown and a clean-up error is only printed). `test/support/accessibility.dart` re-exports `expectTtAccessible` from `taketray_ui`'s test support.
- Why: the screens needed to show the Task 9 states (Review Focus 1 and 4) before the real Reels and tagging screens exist in Phases 4 and 5, so the card and a first To tag are built from the components that are already designed. Putting the banner, the card's rules and the sheet's rules in one place each means the two screens cannot disagree (the Manual mode text, the menu rule, the link rule). Keeping the add and its snack in `showAddEarlierVideos` means both entry points behave the same, and putting the snack on the screen (not on the sheet) keeps Undo available after the sheet is gone. Lazy grids, bounded tiles and `ready` states keep 500 clips and a slow start from freezing the first frame.
- Alternatives considered: the card watching `ToTagController` (breaks the open-triggers-verify rule, point 6); showing the add error inside the sheet (needs a place in the component and keeps the user in a state that failed); a text-only retry (no way to recount without closing); the platform locale for `{time}` (can throw); `jm` for every time, Ruling AF's literal reading (it ignores the phone's 24-hour setting); a new `TtSheet` subtitle parameter (a component change that this task was told not to make); disabling rows by only removing `onTap` (they would look usable).
- Consequences: `openCamera()` returning false (no camera app) shows nothing, because no copy exists for it (amended by D-86: it shows "Couldn't open the camera."). The disabled look of a sheet row (opacity and a disabled node) is done in feature code (superseded by D-79: `TtListRow.off`, no opacity, and the `TtSkeletonLine` of 56 dp became `TtSkeletonCount`). `TtShootingModeCard.referencesRow` holds the "Add earlier videos" row in Phase 3 (its doc comment says so); Phase 4/5 References must share or redesign it. A failed add closes the sheet, so the user reopens it to retry. A tab re-selection runs one more scan and verify (cheap, coalesced by the queue). The tile time is in the phone's time zone, so a clip shot abroad shows the phone's local time until the zone-aware time lands with the player (Amended by D-83: it landed in Phase 4, Task 4; the tile shows the time where the clip was shot). If the list stream fails (a database error), To tag shows skeletons. The history icon and the row inside the card stand in for a design the prototype does not have, and should be looked at on the phone (Task 11). Tests that run the whole app need `testApp`, "remove animations", and a real-time turn for the database.

### D-77 · Test videos on the phone: a debug-only channel, one folder of its own, an owner-only delete, removed in the tear-down, absent from the release build
- Date: 2026-10-29 · Phase: 3 · Task: 11 (the brief called it D-76; that number was taken by Task 10)
- Decision: the Patrol tests that run on the S24 need real videos in the phone's media library, and they must not touch anything else in it.
  1. **A debug-only channel.** `app.taketray/test_media` is registered by `DebugHooks.register(engine, context)` in `src/debug`. Its three calls: `insertVideo({bytes, displayName})` → `{volumeName, mediaStoreId}`, `deleteVideo({mediaStoreId})` → true (deleted) or false (no such row), and `deleteLeftovers()` → `{deleted, refused}`. The classes are `TestMedia` (the MediaStore work), `TestMediaGuard` (the delete rule) and `TestMediaHandler` (the channel). The release and profile `src/` folders hold only a `DebugHooks` that does nothing. (Ruling B gave `register` one argument; it now takes the application context too, in all three source sets, because the debug one needs a `ContentResolver`.)
  2. **Its own folder.** `insertVideo` always writes to `Movies/TakeTray Test/`, as a pending row (`IS_PENDING` 1), then the bytes, then `IS_PENDING` 0, as a camera app does. A failure on the way deletes the half-made row.
  3. **An owner-only delete.** `deleteVideo` looks the row up first (pending and trashed rows included) and deletes it only if `TestMediaGuard.allowsDelete(relativePath, ownerPackage, ownPackage)`: the folder is exactly `Movies/TakeTray Test/` (same case, no sub-folder) and `OWNER_PACKAGE_NAME` is exactly this app (`app.taketray.dev`; the release app `app.taketray` is a different owner). Anything else, including an unknown folder or owner, throws `TestMediaRefused`; Dart gets the error code `refused` and nothing is deleted. The files belong to TakeTray Dev, so deleting them asks the user nothing.
  4. **Cleaned up, always.** `TestMedia.start()` (Dart) deletes what a crashed run left (`deleteLeftovers`, own rows only) and fails with a clear message if the folder holds a file this app does not own. It registers `addTearDown` to delete every video the test inserted, which runs after a failure too. Deleting a row that is gone is not an error.
  5. **Privacy (D-10).** Nothing here logs or returns a display name, a path or a URI (the id of an inserted video is returned, and is allowed). An unexpected error reaches Dart as `native-error` with the method and the exception's class name. All I/O runs on `Dispatchers.IO`.
  6. **Absent from release.** `tool/check_release_permissions` also fails if the release APK's `classes*.dex` contains `TestMedia` or `test_media`, or if R8's `mapping.txt` names `TestMedia`. R8 renames classes in the dex (a class named `TestMedia` becomes, say, `lh`), so the dex alone shows the channel name `test_media` (a string, never renamed) but not the class name; the mapping file holds the class name. The scan proves itself first: it must find `app/taketray/MainActivity` in the dex.
  7. **The video.** `tool/make_test_video` (ffmpeg) makes a 2 second, 160x90 H.264 test pattern without audio and writes `app/patrol_test/fixtures/test_video.dart` as a base64 constant. Re-running it with the same ffmpeg gives the same bytes (one thread, no metadata, `-fflags +bitexact`); another ffmpeg or libx264 build can give other bytes, which does not matter. It does not touch the output when ffmpeg is missing.
  8. **The phone rule.** This is the exception globals.md allows: Patrol tests create and then delete their own test videos in `Movies/TakeTray Test/`, with the owner's go. No adb is involved.
- Why: the scan, the folder query, the generation baseline and the thumbnails depend on how the real MediaProvider behaves, which Robolectric cannot show (D-67). Using the owner's real clips would put them at risk and make the tests depend on what the owner has filmed. An owner check on top of the folder check means a bug or a mistake in a test can only ever remove a file this very test run made. Compiling the channel into debug builds only, and checking the release dex, keeps a way to write to the media library out of the app that ships.
- Alternatives considered: `adb push` and `adb shell rm` into shared storage (the phone rule forbids adb for shared storage, and it would not make an owned row); a plugin to write to MediaStore from Dart (a dependency in the release build); deleting by display name (a name can match someone else's file); a folder check without the owner check (a file the user dropped into the folder would be deletable); `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` for the test build (a permission the app must never have).
- Consequences: the debug build has code that can write to the media library, which is why every path to it is the guarded one. A file in the test folder that TakeTray Dev does not own (for example after the dev app was uninstalled: Android may then clear the owner of its files; not tested) cannot be deleted by the app: `TestMedia.start()` then fails with a message to delete the folder by hand. The limited-access Patrol test is written but skipped, and stays skipped until it verifies that the tile it picks in the system picker is the inserted test video (the first tile can be an owner video); `--dart-define=LIMITED_ACCESS=true` only turns it on for developing that check, and One UI's picker has not been driven yet; the owner does those steps by hand until then (`docs/dev-setup.md`, Phase 3 check, step 10). A class-name check on the dex needs R8's mapping; if release builds ever stop using R8, the dex check alone covers both names.

### D-78 · Add earlier videos: the user picks a from-to date range, and is asked before anything is added; "Last 2 days" and "Last 7 days" are gone, Today stays
