- Date: 2026-10-29 · Phase: 3 · Task: 13 (owner request, 2026-10-29)
- Amended by D-79 (built in Task 13b): point 2's stock `showDateRangePicker` and its `DatePickerThemeData` are gone, replaced by TakeTray's own picker (`showTtDateRangePicker`), which reopens on the last dates chosen in the sheet; the card row has a slot of its own (`rows`); the rows of point 1 are off, not faded, while busy; point 4's dates carry the year when a date is not in this year (the controller's ruling, kept in one helper, `ttDayText`); and the Consequences about the stock picker's 49 x 42 dp cells and `expectDatePickerAccessible` no longer apply (that helper is removed). Points 3, 5, 6 and 7 and the counting and confirming are unchanged.
- Decision: the sheet has two rows, built from the existing components and Flutter's own date range picker (the prototype has no date picker, so the design is ours).
  1. **Rows.** **Today** keeps its count ("none", "1 new", "N new") and adds at once, as before. **Choose dates…** has a chevron and no count, opens the picker, and is disabled while the Today count loads, while the picked dates are counted and while an add runs. The rows "Last 2 days" and "Last 7 days", and everything behind them, are removed (a feature removal the owner asked for, not a loosening: their tests went with them).
  2. **Picker.** `showDateRangePicker`: help text "Choose dates", confirm button "Find videos", `initialDateRange` null, `firstDate` ten years before today, `lastDate` today (future days cannot be picked), `currentDate` today on `clockProvider`. A one-day range (from == to) is allowed. It is themed only through `TakeTrayTheme`'s `DatePickerThemeData` (tokens, no raw values in features): the full-screen picker on `surface` with no tint, the days between the two chosen ones in `primaryContainer`, the header headline one size down (`titleMedium`) so "Start Date – End Date" fits 360 dp, and the dialog layout of larger screens in `surfaceContainerHigh` with `TtMetrics.dialogRadius`. Everything else (selected days in `primary`, today's ring, text styles) is Material 3 reading the TakeTray scheme and text theme. There is a widgetbook use case ("Theme › Date range picker") and a golden for light and dark (`test/theme/date_picker_golden_test.dart`).
  3. **Local calendar days, both inclusive.** `earlierWindowBounds(range, now)` returns `(startUtc, endUtc)`: the start is local midnight of the "from" day, the end is local midnight of the day after the "to" day (exclusive). Both are built with `DateTime(y, m, d + n)`, never `add(Duration(days: 1))`, so a daylight-saving change lands on the right midnight (25-hour 2026-10-25 and 23-hour 2027-03-28 in the London step). Only the year, month and day of the dates are read. `from` after `to` throws `ArgumentError`. A `to` after today is clamped to today (no video can be in the future; the picker never offers one), and then a `from` after today throws `ArgumentError`. Today is the same window as today..today. `shotInWindow(v, startUtc, endUtc, now:)` is `shotAt` not before the start and before the end, and `isUsable`.
  4. **Count, then ask.** After "Find videos", `EarlierVideosController.countIn(range)` runs `MediaScanner.countEarlierIn(range)` on the media queue: full access only (0 without it), pending and zero-length rows left out, a known clip left out (the fingerprint-checked lookup, Ruling U). The sheet shows a skeleton in the Choose dates… row meanwhile. Then a `TtDialog`: **N ≥ 1**: title "{count, plural, one{1 video found} other{{count} videos found}}", body "{count, plural, one{Add it to To tag?} other{Add them to To tag?}}", buttons **Cancel** and **Add**. **N = 0**: title "No new videos", body "No new videos between {from} and {to}." (the dates by `ttDayText`: the phone's `MMMd`, with the year when not this year, D-79; a one-day range: "No new videos on {day}."), one **OK**. Cancel, OK, and tapping outside close the dialog and leave the sheet open, so the user can choose other dates.
  5. **Add** runs `addEarlier(range)` for the same dates (the same code as Today), then the existing snack "N clips added to To tag · Undo", where N is what was really inserted, so if the videos changed since the count the snack shows the real number. Undo and `markEarlierAdded` are as before. Nothing added (everything went away since the count): no snack, the sheet stays.
  6. **Errors.** A failed count shows the existing count-failure state ("Couldn't check your videos." and Try again), and Try again counts the same dates again (a second tap before the screen redraws does nothing: `_find` and the button ignore a tap while a count runs, so two dialogs cannot stack). A failed add shows "Couldn't add videos. Try again.". `countIn`, `add` and `undo` rethrow after logging the type (D-75) and the sheet catches every one; nothing is uncaught.
  7. **Logs** name only the kind of range ("today" or "dates") and counts, never the dates (D-10).
- Why: the owner wants to bring in videos from any past days, not only two fixed windows, and to see how many will be added before they are. Local calendar days with both ends included is what "from the 20th to the 24th" means to a person. A ten-year floor keeps the picker usable without hiding any plausible video. Counting first and then confirming makes a wrong range cost nothing: a big add is not undone by hand clip by clip. Keeping Today one tap keeps the most common case fast. Building on Flutter's themed picker gives a calendar that is accessible, localised and tested, for a theme and no custom widget.
- Alternatives considered: keeping the fixed windows beside the picker (the owner asked for them gone, except Today); a count in the picker's own confirm button (it cannot show a count that depends on a query); adding at once with only Undo (the owner asked to confirm first); a custom calendar (a lot of code to get accessibility and localisation right); no floor, or a short one (a video from years ago would be out of reach).
- Consequences: the date format has no year, as the owner asked, so a range that crosses years prints "Dec 30 and Jan 2" (the picker itself shows the year). One query per volume covers a range of any length; a 10-year range asks MediaStore for every video in the watched folders since then (the phone does the filtering; the app narrows by the end bound in Dart). The picker is Flutter's, so its day cells are 49 x 42 dp, below the 48 dp tap target the component tests demand, and no theme can change that; its tests (`expectDatePickerAccessible`) keep the label and text-contrast checks, skip the tap-target size, and skip the contrast of days that cannot be picked (disabled text, which WCAG 1.4.3 exempts) and are run with both days chosen, because Flutter draws the "Start Date" and "End Date" hints at 38 % opacity, below 3:1 on any colour. The app-wide Material `DatePickerThemeData` also covers any later date picker.

### D-79 · Add earlier videos, redesigned and built: TakeTray's own date range picker with 48 dp days, a card row of its own, off rows that keep AA contrast, and a year only when it is not this year
- Date: 2026-10-29 · Phase: 3 · Task: 13b · Screens 38–40 in `design/prototype/index.html`; handoff `design/handoff/add-earlier-videos.md`
- Amended by D-90: point 4's show-rule (on, full access, `earlierAdded` false) is now "access is full and the Shooting state is known", on **and off**. The off card renders its `rows` too, in the off card's hairline look, and the off row's line is "From your camera folder".
- Decision: the flow keeps D-78's rules (Today adds at once; chosen dates are counted, then confirmed; Cancel and OK go back to the sheet; Undo; the error snacks; the `MediaWorkQueue`; the window bounds; the 10-year floor; no future days) and changes how it looks and is built:
  1. **Picker.** `showTtDateRangePicker(context, firstDate:, lastDate:, currentDate:, initialDateRange:, labels:)` in `taketray_ui` is a full-screen dialog route on `surface` (it rises 24 dp and fades over `medium2`; no travel under "remove animations"). Its page, `TtDateRangePickerPage`, has an app bar (Close, "Choose dates", Clear only while days are chosen), a summary that is a polite live region, the quick ranges (Yesterday, Last week, This month, Last month: `TtPillTabs` with `wrapAtLargeText`, which only fill the calendar), the calendar, and a fixed "Find videos" bar (a large button, off until a day is chosen). It returns `DateTimeRange?`: null for Close or back, and `end == start` for one day (also when only the first day was chosen). Focus moves to the title when it opens (`TtAppBar.titleFocusNode`, and `wrapTitle` so the title is read in full at 2× text) and back to the opener when it closes. "Last week" is the whole week before this one, from the locale's first day of the week.
  2. **Calendar.** `TtDateRangeCalendar` (with `TtDateRangeCalendarController` and `TtDateRangeCalendarLabels`) shows one month: 48 × 48 dp days in 7 columns, a weekday row from `MaterialLocalizations.firstDayOfWeekIndex`, a month header (name button, ‹ ›, and a swipe: a fling, or a drag of one day's width), and the year and month grid (the years from `lastDate`'s back to `firstDate`'s, newest first, then the 12 months; a month with no available day is off). The tap rules: the first tap picks the first day, the second the last (the same day again is one day), a tap before the first day (while the last is still to be chosen) moves the first, and a tap after a full range starts again. The ‹ and › buttons stop at the months of `firstDate` and `lastDate`. Days of the previous and next month fill the first and last rows and can be chosen. It has 12 dp sides (`space3`) in the picker, the one place that is not the 16 dp gutter, because 7 × 48 = 336 dp fits 360 dp. Semantics: a table of rows of cells (Flutter has no grid and gridcell roles; `SemanticsRole.table`, `row` and `cell` are the nearest), each day labelled by its full date, then "first day", "last day", "in the range" or "the only day", then "today" and "not available", and chosen days selected; years are radio buttons in a radio group; months say "has chosen days" and "not available".
  3. **The stock picker is removed.** `showDateRangePicker`, the `DatePickerThemeData` in `TakeTrayTheme` and its test, widgetbook use case ("Theme › Date range picker") and macOS and CI goldens (`date_picker_*.png`), and the app's `expectDatePickerAccessible`, are deleted: a removal, not a loosening. Every behaviour test that drove the stock picker now drives the new one. The new goldens are `date_range_calendar`, `date_range_picker` and `earlier_rows`, and `shooting_mode_card` changed.
  4. **Card.** `TtShootingModeCard.referencesRow` became `rows: List<TtCardRow>` (0 to 2 rows, an assertion; References first, then Add earlier videos, then the strip). The Add earlier videos row has the history icon, "Add earlier videos", the line "Shot before Shooting mode was on" and a chevron, and its show-rule is unchanged (on, full access, `earlierAdded` false). Phase 4/5 References will be the first row.
  5. **Sheet and rows.** `TtListRow` got `tall` (72 dp), `off` (no tap; a disabled button with its `semanticLabel`; title `onSurfaceVariant` at weight 400; no opacity, so `TtMetrics.listRowDisabledOpacity` is deleted), and `focusNode`. New trailing parts: `TtRowCount` ("N new" in a `secondaryContainer` pill at least 28 dp high; "none" or "Adding…" as plain text; outlined in an off row), `TtRowSpinner` (20 dp, 2 dp stroke, a quarter arc standing still under "remove animations") and `TtSkeletonCount` (48 × 16 dp, 8 dp radius). Today shows its date (`MMMEd`) and its count (`stackTrailingAtLargeText`); Choose dates… shows "Up to 10 years back", and "{dates} · Checking…" or "{dates} · Adding…" with a spinner in place of the chevron while it works. Every row is off while the count loads, the dates are counted or an add runs. The count error is `TtBanner(kind: warning)` under the subtitle with Try again, and the rows stay (Today "not checked" and off, Choose dates… usable).
  6. **Reopening.** The picker opens on the last dates chosen with Find videos in this sheet (`_lastRange`), and on nothing the first time and in every new sheet. Closing the picker does not change it.
  7. **Dates follow the phone, and the year is shown only when needed (the controller's ruling).** `ttDayText(context, day, today:)` is the one helper: `MMMd` for a day of this year, `yMMMd` for another, with no-break spaces so a date never wraps. To undo the ruling, change that one function. The app also gets `localeListResolutionCallback: resolvePhoneLocale`: the app lists only `en`, so Flutter would give every English phone the plain `en` ("Oct 8"); now an English (India) phone resolves to `en_IN` ("8 Oct"). It changes every date and time in the app to the phone's region, which the owner's brief asked for.
  8. **No new tokens.** Every size comes from existing tokens or a named `TtMetrics` entry (`calendar*`, `rowCount*`, `rowSpinner*`, `listRowTall*`, `skeletonCount*`).
- Why: the owner wanted the date range flow designed properly. The stock picker's day cells are 49 × 42 dp (D-78), under the 48 dp target, and no theme can change that. Ten years is 120 months, so a jump to month and year beats a long scrolling list. A fading off row fails AA, which the owner asked for, disabled days included. The year rule matters because "8 Oct" can be the wrong year ten years back. Reopening on the last dates makes the fix after "No new videos" one tap.
- Alternatives considered: keeping the stock picker (fails 48 dp); a vertical list of months like the stock full-screen picker (slow to cross years); hiding future days instead of striking them (the calendar would look cut short); the error as a new callout variant (the warning banner already exists); a `LayoutBuilder` for the pill grid (the golden tests and any parent that asks for intrinsic sizes cannot measure it, so it is a custom layout).
- Consequences: a custom calendar means our own tests for semantics, locale (first day of the week, month names, date formats) and large text, which D-78 avoided; they are in `date_range_calendar_test.dart` and `date_range_picker_test.dart`. The handoff's example "30 Dec 2025 and 2 Jan 2026" shows the year on a date of the current year; the rule as written ("only when a date is not in this year") gives "Dec 30, 2025 and Jan 2" in 2026. The picker's title wraps at large text and its bar grows (`TtAppBar.wrapTitle`), as the prototype's `.fs2 .appbar { height: auto; min-height: 64px }` does; every other bar keeps its clamp and ellipsis (D-50). The quick ranges are optional and can be dropped. The spinner's own label is in the row's label, so a screen reader hears the row's "Choose dates. … · Checking…" and not the spinner separately (as the prototype's disabled button also hides it). On the phone: the focus return, TalkBack reading the calendar's table and the swipe are checked by the owner in the Phase 3 check, steps 6 and 9 (`docs/dev-setup.md`).

### D-80 · Phase 4 scope: what the reel screens build now and what waits; Collecting; no duplicate reel names; a 1,000-character description; Undo on every remove, move, add and delete; Home newest first
- Date: 2026-10-30 · Phase: 4 · Task: 1 · Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-phase-4-reels.md`
- Decision:
  1. **Built in Phase 4:**
     - Home: the Shooting mode card, the clips-waiting banner, status filters with counts, reel cards and the New reel button;
     - create, edit, status and delete of a reel;
     - reel detail: shooting days, "in N reels" badges, missing tiles, and multi-select with Add, Move and Remove;
     - the full-screen clip viewer.
  2. **Hidden until their phase** (hidden, not disabled):
     - Edit video, the VN banner, the card's VN pill, the delete dialog's VN line, and Share (Phase 8);
     - Add videos (Phase 7);
     - the reference badge on the card, the reference link in the form, and the references section on reel detail (Phase 9);
     - the reel picker's tag mode (Phase 5).

     The viewer shows three actions (Add to reel, Move, Remove).
  3. **Collecting** replaces the prototype's "Recording" everywhere (spec §3). The descriptions are "Still shooting clips for it", "All the clips are in" and "Posted on Instagram".
  4. **No duplicate reel names** (owner, 2026-10-30). Two reels cannot share a name, ignoring case and the spaces at either end, and the form says "You already have a reel called {name}". This amends D-56; Task 5 enforces it in `ReelsRepository`. Backup import and Undo of a delete still restore a duplicate rather than lose data.
  5. **A description is at most 1,000 characters**, checked in the repository and in the form.
  6. **Undo** follows every remove, move, add-to-reel and reel delete, and keeps working after leaving the screen until the snack closes. A status change shows "Marked as {status}" with no Undo, as in the prototype.
  7. **Home order:** newest created first (ties: highest id first), as the prototype's `routes.home` sorts. `ReelsRepository.watchAll` (by last update) is unchanged.
  8. **New copy approved by the owner** (2026-10-30): "Couldn't open the camera.", "They stay in this reel too.", and "Added to {A}, {B} and {n} more".
- Why: the spec's Phase 4 row covers Home, the reel forms, reel detail and the viewer. The hidden pieces need the editor handoff, the folder browser, references or tagging, which their own phases build. A button that does nothing would be worse than no button. The owner chose to block duplicate names because two reels with the same name would be confused in the picker and on Home. The prototype undoes every removal, and the design prompt asks for Undo on remove actions.
- Alternatives considered:
  - showing the later actions disabled: they would look broken for several phases;
  - allowing duplicate names (D-56's original choice): the owner declined it;
  - no cap on the description: a pasted wall of text would make reel detail and backups unwieldy.
- Consequences: the viewer's action bar has three columns until Phase 8 adds Share. The repository gains a `duplicate` name problem.

### D-81 · Design-system debts paid before the reel screens: the package composes no user-facing string, one font-weight helper, `TtCardRowKind`, and text that wraps or moves at 2×
- Date: 2026-10-30 · Phase: 4 · Task: 2
- Decision:
  1. **The package takes its words from the caller.**
     - `TtTextField.counterText` is a `String Function(int length, int max)`, required (an assert) when `maxLength` is given.
     - `TtProgressBar.percentText` is a `String Function(int percent)`, required when `value` is given.
     - `TtReelCard.referencesSemanticLabel` is required when `referenceCount > 0` (an assert); the "just the count" fallback is gone.
     - `TtAddReferenceTile.semanticLabel` is required (it was `'$title. $subtitle'`).
     - `TtGlassPill.semanticLabel` and `TtShootingModeCard.semanticLabel` are required. The card's label is a group around the card ("Shooting mode, on" or "..., off", the app's `shootingModeCardSemantics` select); the switch, the camera button and the rows keep their own nodes.
  2. **`TtTextStyles.weight(FontWeight)`** returns a `TextStyle` with `fontWeight` and the `wght` variation set together. Every component merges it (`style.merge(TtTextStyles.weight(TtMetrics.badgeFontWeight))`), and the text-weight metrics are `FontWeight`s now. A test scans `lib/src` and fails if any file but the helper and the generated type scale builds a `FontVariation`. `TtTextStyles`' own constructor is no longer `const`, because its styles are made with the helper.
  3. **`TtCardRowKind { link, strip }`** is a required argument of `TtCardRow`. Before, a row was a to-tag strip when it had a `trailingText`; now the kind says so, a strip may have no trailing text, and a link with one is an assertion error.
  4. **`TtTextField`:** the error is also the field's semantics hint (as Material does for its own `errorText`); the error icon scales with the text; and swapping the controller keeps the text. From the field's own controller to the caller's, the caller's text stays, or, if it is empty, the typed text is copied in. From the caller's to the field's own, the caller's text is copied. From one caller's controller to another, both are left alone.
  5. **At 2× text:**
     - the missing-clip tile keeps its 3:4 shape. The label wraps over as many lines as it needs, between words only. From a text scale of 1.5, or whenever the word `removeLabel` does not fit the tile on one line (decided in layout by `FitOrFallback`, which works inside `IntrinsicHeight`), the Remove button is an icon (`Symbols.playlist_remove_rounded`, a 48 dp target) with the tooltip and semantics label "Remove" (`removeLabel`); otherwise it is the text button, so the word is never cut short or ended with an ellipsis. `FitHeight`, a small render box that does not use a `LayoutBuilder` (so it works inside `IntrinsicHeight`), is only a last-resort net: it shrinks the content when it is still taller than the tile, or when a single word is wider than it, and it reports a scaled baseline. In a three-column cell on 360 dp (about 107 x 142 dp) the icon, three label lines and a 48 dp button need about 180 dp of height, so at 2× the content is shown at about 0.7 of its size (the macOS golden shows it); a later pass can let the grid rows grow with the text (`docs/later.md`);
     - the stat strip puts its action under the text from a text scale of 1.5 (`TtMetrics.largeTextScale`), as `TtBanner` does, so the text keeps its two lines;
     - on a reel card the pills are in the column above the empty art instead of floating over it, so the film icon is always below them;
     - the pill-tab "long label" test now checks with `wrapAtLargeText` that every tab is on the screen, and a second test that the scroller scrolls.
  6. `TtMenuButton` sets its "open" flag after the frame when `MenuAnchor` calls `onClose` during a build. The doc comments that said "glass circle" are fixed. The status pill's caret gap was already 6 dp (`TtMetrics.statusPillCaretGap`); a test pins it.
  7. **Widgetbook tests:** `catalog_use_cases_test.dart` checks that each tap selected its use case (`WidgetbookState.useCase.name`). `catalog_coverage_test.dart` checks per class, with an allow-list of the data classes that the catalogue deliberately does not show (`TtMenuItem`, `TtNavItem`, `TtPillTab`, `TtDialogAction`, `TtClipMark`, the two labels classes and the calendar controller).
- Why: the first reel forms and cards use all of these, and each was a small defect or a gap a review had found: a screen reader missed a field's error, a swapped controller lost the text, English words and `%` were fixed inside a package whose app is translated, and a weight set in only one of the two places looks wrong in a variable font. A `trailingText` that also chose the look hid the real choice.
- Alternatives considered:
  - a locale-aware formatter inside the package (`intl` for the percent): the app already owns all its words in `app_en.arb`, so one more place would split them;
  - a `TtTextStyles.weight` that returns a `FontWeight`: the two properties would again be set apart;
  - letting the missing tile grow taller at large text: a grid cell is a fixed size, and a `LayoutBuilder` could not be used inside the golden harness' `IntrinsicHeight`;
  - a text "Remove" button at large text: it broke the word in the middle ("Rem / ove") in a 107 dp tile;
  - leaving the pills floating over the empty art and moving the icon: the caption already shares the column with the art, so the pills do too.
- Consequences: every caller of the changed constructors was updated (`shooting_mode_section.dart`, the Widgetbook use cases, the package tests). The Shooting mode card's label gets a new key, `shootingModeCardSemantics`. `TtTextField` copies the typed text into a caller's empty controller after the frame (the caller's listeners are not told in the middle of a build). The macOS goldens of the clip tile, reel card, stat strip and text field changed; the CI set is made by the controller afterwards.

### D-82 · New components for reels and the viewer; the FAB contract: `TtNavPill.fabBottom` and `TtFab.liftBy`
- Date: 2026-10-30 · Phase: 4 · Task: 3
- Decision:
  1. **The FAB contract.**
     - The shell does not host the FAB. Each screen puts its own `TtFab` in a `Stack`, and `TtNavPill.fabBottom(context)` says where: the pill's `reservedHeight` (its 64 dp, the 10 dp gap under it and the bottom safe area) plus one 16 dp step (`TtSpacing.m`), the gap a FAB keeps from the screen's edge. Without the pill the `bottom` is 16 dp. (The prototype's `fab(..., { nav: true })` says 96 on its own taller nav bar; the real number comes from the real pill.)
     - **`TtFab` follows the snack by itself.** `TtSnack` reports its own height (a size-reporting render object, `SnackHeightReporter`) into a `ValueListenable<double>` that the package exposes as `TtSnackHeight.of(context)`: one value for each `ScaffoldMessenger`, 0 while no snack shows. It reports only when it is inside a `SnackBar` (a panel drawn on its own in a catalogue or a golden is not a snack that is showing), and 0 when it leaves; a snack that replaces another is not reset by the old one going. `TtFab` listens and is drawn `snack height + spacing.s` (12 dp) higher while a snack shows, over `motion.medium1` with `emphasizedDecelerate`, back down when it goes, and jumping under "remove animations". So screens need no glue. The first version used a fixed 64 dp (the prototype's number), which left a 2 to 3 line snack at 2× text over the button.
     - `TtFab.liftBy` is now an optional override: null (the default) follows the snack, a number is a fixed lift in dp (0 for never). The lift is a paint-time `Transform`, so the space the button takes does not change and hit tests and the semantics rectangle follow it. A consequence for tests: `tester.getRect(find.byType(TtFab))` is the place it takes, not where it is drawn. Find it by its label (`find.text('New reel')`) or by the `Material` inside it. The FAB's root is the `Transform`, so its semantics node is found as a descendant (`find.descendant(of: find.byType(TtFab), matching: find.bySemanticsLabel(...))`).
     - A snack that is closing (its exit animation) still counts until it has left the tree, so the button comes down as the snack finishes leaving, not when it starts to.
     - Why not read the `ScaffoldMessenger`: Flutter's snack bar API does not expose the snack's height. Why measure instead of a number: a snack's height depends on its message, the width and the text scale.
  2. **`TtMenuItem`** gains three optional fields, so every existing caller compiles unchanged: `subtitle` (a `bodySmall` line in the variant colour; the item is then at least 60 dp, with 8 dp above and below at large text), `checked` (a `bool?`) and `iconFilled`. With `checked` not null the item is one of a set of choices: a screen reader says `menuitemradio` (`SemanticsRole.menuItemRadio`, `inMutuallyExclusiveGroup`, checked or not); the checked one is drawn on `secondaryContainer`, in `onSecondaryContainer`, with a check after the label. The semantics sit inside the item's `child`, because Flutter's `MenuItemButton` merges everything below it into one node. `TtMenuPanel` (what `TtMenuButton` and the golden draw) shows all three.
  3. **`TtReelHeader<T>`** (the top of the reel page): the name as a wrapping heading in `tt.text.detailTitle` (30/36, 800), then a meta row (`Wrap`) of the large `TtStatusPill`, `metaText`, and an optional `missingText` with an error icon, then the optional Shot ideas block. The header owns the status menu (`statusItems`, `onStatusSelected`), so a screen does not have to wire a `TtMenuButton` around the pill. All text comes from the caller. The block is clamped to two lines, and "Show more" / "Show less" appear **only when the text needs more than two lines at the width it has and the screen's text scale**. That is measured with a `TextPainter` (`maxLines: 2`, `didExceedMaxLines`) during layout (`internal/text_overflow_switch.dart`), not counted in characters (the prototype's 90 is a stand-in). The block remembers whether it is open (`shotIdeasOpen` is only the start). The button is a 48 dp target announced with its expanded state.
  4. **`TtReelTag`, `TtReelTagRow`, `TtViewerAction` and `TtViewerActions`** (the viewer's dark chrome). They use the video colours (`onVideo`), so they look the same in both app themes, and are meant to sit under a `TtVideoSurface`. `TtViewerActions` takes 3 or 4 actions (an assert; Phase 4 passes 3), equal columns, each at least 64 dp high and a full column wide. When even the widest single word does not fit its column (four actions at 2× text), **every** action shrinks by the same scale (`EqualColumns`, a small layout box that also gives the actions one height), so no label is smaller than another and no word breaks. The viewer reuses `TtPlayerHeader`, `TtStageArrow`, `TtScrubBar` and `TtVideoSurface`.
  5. **`TtReelPickRow`** (the picker's row: a 40×52 cover or the film icon, the name on one line, the count, then `TtCheckMark` when `multi` and `TtRadioMark` when not) is announced as a checkbox or a radio with its checked state; its label is the caller's `semanticLabel`. **`TtReelPickNewRow`** is the "New reel" row: a 40 dp `secondaryContainer` box with a plus, and a primary label.
- Why: Tasks 6 to 10 use all of these, and each needs a decision made once: where the FAB sits, how it gets out of a snack's way, and when "Show more" appears. A character count would show the button for text that fits (a short line of wide letters wraps to three) and hide it for text that does not (narrow letters, or 2× text), so the measure uses the real font, width and text scale.
- Alternatives considered:
  - reading the snack's height from the `ScaffoldMessenger`: not exposed, and it would tie the FAB to one way of showing a snack;
  - hosting the FAB in the shell: each tab has a different FAB (or none) and the reel page has none above the pill;
  - a `LayoutBuilder` to measure the Shot ideas: it cannot answer the intrinsic size questions the golden harness (and any `IntrinsicHeight`) asks, so a small render object chooses between the two layouts instead. It builds both children but lays out only the chosen one, tells `find` which one is shown (so a test sees one text, not two), and puts the other under an `ExcludeFocus` (one frame after the choice changes), so a hidden "Show more" is not a keyboard stop;
  - an animated padding for the lift: it would change the size the FAB takes.
- Consequences: new metrics in `TtMetrics` (`menuItemTallHeight`, the reel header, viewer and pick row numbers). New files `reel_header.dart`, `viewer_chrome.dart`, `reel_pick_row.dart` and Widgetbook use cases for each (and a "Status menu" use case and a "snack showing" knob). The one existing assertion that looked the FAB's semantics up with `find.byType(TtFab)` now looks for the node inside the FAB and checks its label, that it is a button and that it has a tap action, because the FAB's root is a `Transform`. The macOS menu golden changed (a status menu scenario). The CI goldens are made by the controller afterwards.

### D-83 · The time where a clip was shot: offsets from the phone's own zone rules in one batched native call, a 4,096-entry cache, the phone's zone as the fallback; shooting days by local calendar date
- Date: 2026-10-30 · Phase: 4 · Task: 4
- Decision: a clip's time and its shooting day are shown in the zone where it was shot (spec §8.3a, §8.4).
  1. **Native offsets.** A new Pigeon method `zoneOffsetsMs(zoneIds, instantsMs)` (`@async`, so Kotlin answers on `Dispatchers.IO` like the MediaStore reads) returns, for each pair, `ZoneId.of(id).rules.getOffset(Instant.ofEpochMilli(ms))` in milliseconds (`ZoneOffsets.offsetsMs`; each zone's rules are looked up once per batch). An id that `ZoneId.of` rejects (`DateTimeException`: unknown, empty, malformed) gives null at its own index. Lists of different lengths throw `IllegalArgumentException`, which reaches Dart as `native-error` (`guarded`, D-66). `MediaBridge.zoneOffsetsMs` has the same shape; `FakeMediaBridge` answers from a fixed table (`zoneRules`: UTC, Asia/Kolkata, and Europe/London's rule since 1996) and logs each request.
  2. **`ClipTimeService.localTimes(clips)`** (`data/media/clip_time.dart`, `clipTimeServiceProvider`, kept alive) returns a `ClipLocalTime` per clip id: `wallClock`, `zoneId` (the zone used) and `isPhoneZone`. It reads the phone's zone once per call (`currentZoneId`), then asks in **one** batch for every `(zone, date taken)` that is not cached, the zone being the clip's `timeZoneId` or, when null, the phone's. A clip whose zone the phone does not know (a null answer) uses the phone's zone: only then does a second batch ask for the phone's zone at those instants; the null answer is cached, so the next call needs no call for it. No clips, no call.
  3. **The cache** is in memory, keyed by `(zone id, instant in ms)` (both: the offset of one zone changes with the instant), and keeps the newest 4,096 entries, least recently used first out (a `LinkedHashMap` in use order; a hit counts as a use). A batch larger than the cache still answers every clip of that call from its own answers. Two calls at the same time can both ask; nothing is shared between them.
  4. **The fallback.** If `zoneOffsetsMs` fails (the first batch or the phone-zone one), times out (15 s, `defaultBridgeTimeout`) or answers with the wrong length, the clips it was for use the phone's zone through Dart's own local time (`toLocal()`), with `zoneId` the phone's id and `isPhoneZone` true; nothing is cached and the next call asks again. If `currentZoneId` fails, a clip with no zone (or an unknown one) uses Dart's local time with `zoneId` null and `isPhoneZone` true, and a clip with its own known zone is still converted, with `isPhoneZone` false (it cannot be compared with the phone's). Each failure is a warning. An unknown zone is an info line with the count, logged only when the bridge reported it in this call: a cached unknown answer logs nothing, so To tag's redraws stay quiet (fix round 1). Logs hold counts and error codes only, never a zone id or a city (D-10). Nothing throws.
  5. **`wallClock` is a UTC-flagged `DateTime` whose fields are the wall clock** (controller's ruling; the plan's comment said a non-UTC one). A local `DateTime` would be re-read in the phone's zone: a wall clock that falls in the phone's spring-forward gap (01:30 on 28 March 2027 in London) cannot exist locally and would move by an hour, and a test would depend on the machine's zone. Callers read its fields or format it as it is (`wallClockTimeText`, `ttDayText`); they never call `toLocal()` on it or compare it with an instant. `isPhoneZone` is true when the zone used is the phone's current zone, including a clip whose own zone is the phone's.
  6. **Shooting days** (`domain/rules/shooting_days.dart`, controller's ruling A): `groupByShootingDay<T>(items, clipOf, times)` is generic, so Task 8 can pass reel clip rows with `clipOf` and the tests pass plain clips with `(c) => c`. `ShootingDay<T>` holds `date` (`DateTime.utc(y, m, d)` of the `wallClock`), `number` and `clips` (in the order given). Groups are sorted by date; a clip whose local date is earlier than the clip before it (a later instant further west) goes in its own date's group. A clip missing from `times` uses its phone-local date, so none is dropped. `dayNumber(firstDay, day)` is the calendar days between the two dates, plus 1, counted between `DateTime.utc` dates (24 hours apart, always): Day 1 is the earliest date, and three empty days give Day 1 then Day 5.
  7. **`zoneCity(zoneId)`** (`domain/rules/zone_city.dart`) is the last `/` segment with `_` as a space ("Isle of Man"), or null for an id with no `/`, an `Etc/` id or an empty segment. The viewer (Task 10) shows "{city} time" only when `!isPhoneZone` and the city is not null.
  8. **To tag** (amends D-76 points 4 and 5): `toTagTimesProvider` (`features/tagging/view_models/to_tag_times.dart`) follows the To tag list and calls `localTimes`; the tile shows `wallClockTimeText(wallClock)` with the phone's 24-hour setting. The times never hold the grid back (fix round 1): the skeletons wait only for the list and the Shooting state, as in Phase 3, and a tile whose time is not known yet (the first answer, or a new clip while a new list's times are read), or times that failed, shows the phone's time (`timeOfDayText`), then switches to the time where it was shot when the answer arrives. Waiting would have kept skeletons up for as long as a hung channel takes to time out (two 15 s timeouts in a row, `currentZoneId` then `zoneOffsetsMs`, and a third for an unknown zone). `timeOfDayText` and `wallClockTimeText` share one formatter.
  9. **Tests.** `ZoneOffsetsTest` (Robolectric, all four SDK levels): London summer and winter, the exact clock-change instant, Kolkata's half hour, invalid ids at their own index, the length check, empty lists, extreme instants, and every zone the phone knows against `java.util.TimeZone` (`offsets come from the phone's zone rules`). `MediaHostApiImplTest` checks the method runs off the main thread and that a length mismatch is `native-error`. `shooting_days_test.dart`, `clip_time_test.dart` and `shooting_time_text_test.dart` are also in `DST_TESTS` (`tool/check` runs them with `TZ=Europe/London`): there a day count made from local `DateTime`s gives the 23-hour spring-forward day 0 days (so Day 2 twice), and a wall clock passed through `toLocal()` moves out of the spring gap (01:30 shows 02:30). CI's own zone, UTC, has no clock change, so neither fails there. The brief's October case alone cannot catch the first: the 25-hour day still truncates to one day.
- Why: videos do not record their zone, and the zone timeline (§8.3a, D-71, D-72) gives each clip one at detection. Turning a zone into an offset needs the zone rules: the phone already has them, current (Android updates its tz data on its own), so TakeTray bundles no tz database and has nothing to keep up to date. One batch per screen keeps a 300-clip reel to one platform round trip, and the cache makes a list redraw cost none. A failure must never hide clips or crash a screen; the phone's zone is the same fallback the timeline uses (§8.3a).
- Alternatives considered: the `timezone` package with its bundled database (a copy that goes stale, about 400 KB, and a second source of truth next to the phone's); one bridge call per clip (hundreds of round trips for a reel); asking for the phone's zone at every instant in the first batch so that an unknown zone never needs a second call (doubles every batch and fills the cache with entries that are almost never used, for a case that should not happen: the zone ids come from the phone itself); a local `DateTime` for the wall clock (see point 5); grouping by UTC date or counting days in 24-hour periods (spec §8.4 says calendar days).
- Consequences: Task 8 (reel detail) and Task 10 (viewer) read `ClipTimeService` too, each through its own view model; `ttDayText(context, shootingDay.date, today:)` formats a day header. A clip whose zone is unknown on this phone shows the phone's time without saying so. If the phone's time-zone data changes while the app runs, cached offsets stay until they fall out of the cache or the app restarts. A tile of a clip shot abroad shows the phone's time for a moment (a few milliseconds on a phone; up to 30 s or more if the channel hangs, until the timeouts) before it switches to the time where it was shot. A zone id renamed in the tz data (`Europe/Kiev` / `Europe/Kyiv`) compares unequal to its alias, so `isPhoneZone` can be false for the phone's own zone (`docs/later.md`, Phase 11).

### D-84 · Reel data for the screens: duplicate names rejected (amends D-56), a 1,000-character description, summaries in one statement, `ReelClip`, move adds first, and undo records restored with the same ids
- Date: 2026-10-30 · Phase: 4 · Task: 5
- Amended by the Phase 4 final-review fix wave (2026-10-31): point 1, the Reel details form follows `rename`'s own-name rule too (D-85); point 8 and new point 9 (`undoAll`, times to the millisecond) below. (Fix round 1 of the wave also left out a delete's VN copy rows whose clip could not come back; fix round 2 reverted that, per D-54: every copy row comes back, see point 9 (c).)
- Extended by D-94: a fifth record, `InboxUndo`, for skip, skip all and restore, restored only while each row is exactly as the action left it.
- Decision:
  1. **Duplicate names** (D-80 point 4; amends D-56). `ReelsRepository.create` and `rename` throw `InvalidInputException(ReelNameProblem.duplicate)` when another reel's name has the same `reelNameKey` (`domain/rules/text_limits.dart`: `normalizeName`, then Dart's `toLowerCase`). The names are read and compared in Dart inside the write's transaction: SQL's `lower()` folds only ASCII, so "CAFÉ" would pass next to "Café". `rename` skips the check when the new name has the reel's current key (its own name in any case), so a reel that shares its name with an imported or restored one can still be saved (fix round 1). The length checks run first, so an empty or too long name still reports `empty` / `tooLong`. Backup import (`BackupService`) and Undo of a delete write rows directly and never check: a restore must not lose a reel because a name was taken meanwhile. Nothing switched exhaustively on `ReelNameProblem`.
  2. **Description cap** (D-80 point 5): `reelDescriptionMaxLength = 1000`, counted with `String.characters` after `normalizeName` (`checkReelDescription`, `ReelDescriptionProblem.tooLong`), in `create` and `setDescription`, before anything is written. 1,000 emoji pass; 1,001 characters do not.
  3. **`watchReelSummaries()`** gives `ReelSummary { reel, clipCount, missingCount, liveDurationMs, cover }` for every reel, newest created first (ties: highest id first). It is **one** SQL statement per emission whatever the number of reels: an `agg` CTE (count, missing count, duration of the clips that are not missing) and a `cover` CTE (`ROW_NUMBER()` over each reel's clips that pass `hasPicture`, by `date_taken_utc` then id) `LEFT JOIN`ed to `reels`. The cover rule `hasPicture` (`data/database/clip_rules.dart`: not missing, source `mediaStore`, a volume, a number above 0) is now shared with `watchFirstShowableClip`, which gained the number-above-0 test (a detached clip is missing anyway, so nothing visible changes). It is rendered into the custom SQL through Drift's `GenerationContext` on the alias `c`, so the rule is written once.
  4. **`watchReelClips(reelId)`** gives `ReelClip { clip, reelCount, addedAt }` in the same order as before (date taken, then id); `reelCount` is a `COUNT` subquery over `clip_reels` in the same statement. `ReelClip` works with `groupByShootingDay` through `clipOf: (rc) => rc.clip` (D-83).
  5. **`moveClips(from, to, ids)`** adds the target links first (`addedAt` now, an existing target link kept), then removes the source links and runs the orphan sweep in the same helper `removeClipsFromReel` uses, so a clip only in the source is never swept. Clips the source does not have are ignored; `from == to` throws `ArgumentError`; an unknown reel throws `StateError` and writes nothing.
  6. **Undo records** (`domain/models/undo_record.dart`): a sealed `UndoRecord` with `RemoveUndo`, `MoveUndo`, `AddUndo` and `DeleteReelUndo`, returned by `removeClipsFromReel`, `moveClips`, `addClipsToReel` and `ReelsRepository.delete`. Each is a snapshot read in the action's own transaction and holds only plain values (`ClipLink`, `InboxEntry`, `ReelTouch`) and domain models (`Clip`, `Reel`, `ReelReference`, `VnSession`, `VnCopy`), never Drift rows. The swept clips are read by `AppDatabase.selectOrphanClips` (the same predicate as `deleteOrphanClips`) just before the sweep. A remove records the links with their `addedAt`, every field of the swept clips and the reel's `updatedAt` before and after; a move the links it created (not the ones that were there), the removed source links and both reels' `updatedAt`; an add the links it created, the `inbox` rows it deleted (state and `addedAt`) and the `updatedAt`; a delete the reel row, its links, references, VN session and copy rows and the swept clips. An unknown reel id gives a record that restores nothing.
  7. **`undo(record)`** (`UndoWriter.undo`, on both repositories) runs in one transaction (flow: "Undo"):
     - a swept clip comes back **with its old id and every field**, unless the same clip already holds that id, or another clip holds its identity now (same volume and MediaStore number above 0, or same picker URI) **with the same fingerprint** (`sameFingerprint`, `domain/rules/fingerprint_match.dart`: the D-69 fields of `sameVideo`, size above 0 and equal, the same duration and the same `dateTakenUtc` instant, the display name ignored): then that clip is linked and nothing is inserted, so one video never has two rows. A holder with another fingerprint is another video (a rebuild gave it the number): a MediaStore clip then comes back with its old id **detached and missing** (`mediaStoreId = -id`, `missing`, `lastVerifiedAt` = now, the D-71 shape), so its reel shows the missing tile and never the other video; a picked clip, which has nothing else to be found by, is skipped with a warning. A different clip holding the old id (backup import only) is skipped with a warning, never linked (fix round 1);
     - links come back with their old `addedAt` (only where reel and clip exist and the link is not there) and take the clip out of To tag and Skipped;
     - `updatedAt` goes back only while the reel still holds the value the action wrote;
     - a move's or an add's created links are deleted only if they still have the action's `addedAt` and the clip keeps another place (a link or a list), so undoing a move or an add **never deletes a clip** (an add: a clip removed from its other reel since keeps the added link). An add first puts its `inbox` rows back for clips that are in no reel except through its own links and in no list, so those clips have a place before their link goes. No sweep (controller's ruling, fix round 1; the brief's "then sweeps orphans" is dropped);
     - a delete's reel is inserted with its old id, with no name check, then clips, links, references (old ids), VN session and copies;
     - a record whose reel is gone (deleted meanwhile) restores nothing for that reel, logs a warning with the id, and does not throw; a delete record whose reel id exists already restores nothing.
     - **Idempotent**: an `Expando` marks a record once its undo commits; a second undo of the same object (or a double tap queued behind the first) is a no-op, and the restore steps are themselves insert-if-absent. A failed undo rolls back and clears only its own mark, so it can be tried again.
  8. **`addClipsToReels(Set<int> reelIds, Iterable<int> clipIds)`** (added by the parked-items batch, 2026-10-31; D-88 uses it). The same add as `addClipsToReel`, for every reel of the set (in the set's iteration order, so a `LinkedHashSet` keeps the order picked) in **one** `_db.transaction`; both methods share one private body (`_addToReel`) that runs inside the caller's transaction. It returns one `AddUndo` for each reel, in that order. A reel or clip that does not exist, or any failed write, throws `StateError` (or the write's error) and **nothing is added to any reel**: the transaction rolls back, so no half-done add is ever visible and the callers need no compensating undo. The `inbox` rows are deleted by the first reel's add and recorded in its record, so the later records carry none; **undo the list last to first** (`ReelSnacks.showUndoAll` already does), which puts the `inbox` rows back by the first record only after the later links are gone, and never deletes a clip (point 7). An empty set gives an empty list and writes nothing. The log line holds counts only.
  9. **Final-review fix wave (2026-10-31).** (a) **`undoAll(List<UndoRecord>)`** (`UndoWriter`, on both repositories; `undo(record)` is `undoAll([record])`) undoes a list **last to first in one transaction**: every record or none. A record undone already is skipped; on a failure every mark set by the call is cleared, so the same list can be undone again. (b) **Times to the millisecond.** `Clock.storedNow()` is `now()` truncated to milliseconds, UTC, the precision `UtcMillisConverter` stores. Every repository that writes the clock's time into a row takes it from there (`ClipsRepository`, `ReelsRepository`, `ReferencesRepository`, `VnRepository`, `UndoWriter`), so the records and the models they return hold exactly the stored value. Before this, `AddUndo.createdLinks[].addedAt` kept the real clock's microseconds, `_hasOtherLink` (a comparison in Dart) took the add's own link for another one, and undoing an add never put a To tag or Skipped row back on a phone; the tests hid it with a whole-minute clock, and now run with a clock 123 µs past the minute. Chosen over comparing in SQL, which would fix that one query and leave microsecond values in the records for the next comparison in Dart to trip over; and over truncating in `Clock.now()`, which would also change times that are not database rows (the Shooting state's `since` keeps its microseconds through its JSON, D-71). `PhoneStateRepository` still uses `now()` for that reason. (c) A delete's **VN copy rows all come back** (`clipId: ids[copy.clipId] ?? copy.clipId`), also one whose clip could not come back (`_restoreClip` gave null): D-54 makes `vn_copies.clip_id` deliberately not a foreign key, because the row is the app's only record of a copy file in `Movies/TakeTray/` and must outlive its clip, and clip ids are `AUTOINCREMENT` and never reused, so the old id can never point at another clip. (Fix round 1 left such rows out with a warning; the re-review reverted that, since it would lose track of the file.) The test `undo delete keeps every VN copy row, also one whose clip cannot come back (D-54)` holds it.
- Why: the owner ruled out duplicate names (D-80). The screens need the counts and cover for every card without one query per reel (25 reels would be 25 queries on every change), and the badge and day grouping need `reelCount` and `addedAt` with the clips. Undo must put back exactly what the action changed (design-prompt §5, Review Focus 1): a clip removed from its only reel is deleted by the orphan rule (D-56), so the record must hold the whole row, read in the same transaction so nothing can change in between; the old id keeps its thumbnail key, its VN copy rows and every other reference to it valid. Linking a clip that holds the identity now is the only way to restore without a second row for one video (the unique keys would refuse the insert anyway). Restoring `updatedAt` only while the action's value is there keeps a later rename's time. Moving by adding first means the global sweep (D-56) never sees a moved clip without a place. Undoing a move never deletes a clip, because its source reel may have been deleted since.
- Alternatives considered: comparing names in SQL with `lower()` or `COLLATE NOCASE` (ASCII only); a unique index on the name (a schema change, and it would make backup import fail on old files); re-inserting a swept clip with a new id (breaks the thumbnail key, VN copy rows and any record still pointing at it); recording only ids and re-reading clips at undo time (the rows are gone by then); an undo that re-runs the inverse action (`addClipsToReel` for a remove: new `addedAt`, no swept clips); a cover chosen in Dart from all clips (reads every clip of every reel on each change); a mutable "used" flag on the record (records stay plain values; the `Expando` lives in the data layer and does not keep a record alive); undoing a move by deleting every target link of the moved clips (would delete links that existed before).
- Consequences: Task 7 shows "You already have a reel called {name}" for `duplicate` and the counter for the description; Tasks 6–10 read `watchReelSummaries` and `ReelClip`. Every caller of the four actions gets a record to hand to its Undo snack. A video that came back under the same number or URI is linked only when its fingerprint matches; a renamed video still matches (the name is ignored), and a video re-encoded in place does not (it comes back as the missing tile). A picked clip is never a cover (no picture to ask for, D-74) although it can be played. Names that differ only in Unicode normalisation (a precomposed "é" against "e" + combining accent) are not duplicates. The undo mark is per record object and in memory, and it is what makes a second undo a no-op: records are never rebuilt from their values (the snack holds the one the action returned), and the insert-if-absent steps alone would not be enough (a `DeleteReelUndo` undone again after the reel was deleted a second time would bring it back).

### D-85 · Create, edit, status and delete: the form and its controller, snacks on the root messenger, a delete that goes to Reels with Undo, and three package additions
- Date: 2026-10-30 · Phase: 4 · Task: 7 · Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-phase-4-reels.md` (the plan names this D-86; Task 6 had not run, so this took D-85 and Task 6 takes D-86)
- Amended by the Phase 4 final-review fix wave (2026-10-31): (a) in edit mode the form's duplicate check skips the reel's own name in any case (`reelNameKey(name) == reelNameKey(current.name)`), as `ReelsRepository.rename` does (D-84 point 1), both in the first check and in the backstop handler, so a reel that shares its name with a restored or imported one can still save its description or status; (b) `ReelSnacks.showUndoAll` undoes its whole list with one `ReelsRepository.undoAll` call, one transaction (D-84 point 9): a failure undoes nothing and shows "Couldn't undo. Try again." (it used to try every record in its own transaction, so a failure part way left some undone); (c) the dead helper `showUndoSnack` is deleted (only a test used it; screens call `ReelSnacks.of(context)`).
- Decision:
  1. **The sheet** (`features/reels/views/reel_form_sheet.dart › showReelForm(context, {Reel? reel})`) is `sheets.reelForm` without the reference link (Phase 9) and the "from player" variant (Phase 5). New: "New reel", the name ("Reel name", hint "For example: London Food", counter `n/40`, autofocus, Enter saves) and "Shot ideas (optional)" (multiline, "Only you see this.", max 1,000, the counter shows only from 900 characters, as an empty string below that), **Cancel** and **Create reel**. Edit ("Reel details"): the same, filled in, plus "Status" (three radio rows, each a `TtRadioMark`, the pill and what it means) and **Save**. New reels are always Collecting. After a save the sheet closes and the root messenger says "{name} created" or "Saved". The sheet sits on `TtSheet`, which adds `viewInsets.bottom`, so the keyboard never covers the fields.
  2. **`ReelFormController`** (`@riverpod`, family on `int? reelId`, auto-dispose) keeps a `ReelFormState` (`nameError`, `descriptionError`, `clashingName`, `saving`). `save(name:, description:, status:)` checks the name and the description both at once, so one tap shows every problem, then writes. A problem is not thrown: `save` returns null and the state holds it, for the view to turn into the prototype's `validName` words ("Give this reel a name", "Use 40 characters or fewer", "You already have a reel called {name}"). The name is checked against the reels as they are now (`watchAll().first`), and `ReelsRepository`'s own `duplicate` is the backstop when another reel takes the name between that check and the write (the form then shows the same message). The reel named in "You already have a reel called {name}" is the other reel's name **as stored** (`clashingName`), not what was typed. Anything else that goes wrong (a database error, the reel gone) is logged by type and rethrown, and the sheet shows "Something went wrong. Try again." **inside the sheet** (point 10) and stays open with the text kept. The form checks the live list through `watchAll`, not `watchReelSummaries` (the brief named the summaries): the form needs only names, and the summaries run a join for every reel.
  3. **Single-flight saves.** A second `save` while one runs returns the first call's future (`_inFlight`, cleared when it ends, whether it returned or threw). A double tap on Create reel makes one reel, and the Save of an edit writes once. The edit writes only what changed (rename, `setDescription`, `setStatus`), name first, because it is the write that can still be refused: a refused name saves nothing else. Editing keeps the reel's own name, in any case, without a clash (`rename` allows it too, D-84). The controller sets its state only while it is mounted, so a sheet dragged away during a save does not make the save throw; the save still finishes and the snack still shows. A `finally` on `_save` clears `saving` whichever way it ends (parked-items batch): the handler for a refused name reads the reels again, and if that read throws, the error goes to the caller but the form is not left saving for ever.
  4. **The status menu** is `reelStatusItems(l10n, current)`: a list of `TtMenuItem<ReelStatus>` (icon filled, `subtitle` the description, `checked`) that Task 8 passes to `TtReelHeader.statusItems`, with `onStatusSelected: (s) => changeReelStatus(context, reel, s)`. `changeReelStatus` writes the status and shows "Marked as {status}" with **no** Undo (D-80). Choosing the status the reel already has changes nothing and says nothing (the prototype writes and announces it anyway; a "Marked as Collecting" for a reel that was Collecting is noise). The same file has `statusKindOf`, `statusName`, `statusDescription` and `statusIcon`, which Home (Task 6) and reel detail (Task 8) use: **Collecting, Ready to edit, Published** and their lines are in `app_en.arb` (`statusCollecting`... `statusPublishedDescription`).
  5. **Delete** (`delete_reel_dialog.dart › showDeleteReelDialog(context, reel)`): a `TtDialog` with the delete icon, "Delete {name}?", the safe line (`TtDialogNote`: "Your videos stay in your gallery. Only this reel and its tags are removed."; the VN line is Phase 8), **Cancel** and **Delete reel** (danger). Confirming runs `ReelActions.confirmDelete(id)` and **returns the `DeleteReelUndo`**. `ReelActions` (kept alive, a plain class) is single-flight per reel id: two confirms at once delete once and share the record. A reel that was already gone gives an empty record and no snack. Then the snack "{name} deleted" with **Undo** is shown, then the app goes to `Routes.reels` (the reel list; reel detail does not exist yet). The snack, the router and the container are looked up **before** anything is awaited, so the screen that opened the dialog may be gone when the delete ends. A failed delete shows "Something went wrong. Try again." and stays where it is.
  6. **Snacks on the root messenger.** `AppShell` has no `ScaffoldMessenger` of its own: the shell's `Scaffold` and every pushed page use the one `MaterialApp.router` makes. That is the messenger a snack must be on to outlive its screen (a page that wraps itself in its own `ScaffoldMessenger` takes its snacks with it when it pops). `rootMessengerKeyProvider` (`features/shell/views/root_messenger.dart`, kept alive, a provider and not a global so each `ProviderScope` and each test has its own key) is handed to `MaterialApp.router(scaffoldMessengerKey:)` in `app.dart`, and `rootMessengerOf(context)` looks the messenger up (falling back to the `ScaffoldMessenger` above the context for a test app without the key). `ReelSnacks.of(context)` (`features/reels/views/undo_snacks.dart`) captures the messenger, the container, the words and the screen-reader flag once, and then needs no context: `show(message)` and `showUndo(message, record)`. **`showUndoSnack(context, message, record)`** is `ReelSnacks.of(context).showUndo(...)`, for the screens of Tasks 8 to 10. It lives in `views/` (it touches the context and the words); the work Undo does is `ReelsRepository.undo(record)`, which restores every kind of record. If Undo fails, **"Couldn't undo. Try again."** is shown: the ARB key `earlierUndoFailed` was renamed `undoFailed`, since it is no longer only for earlier videos. `add_earlier_videos.dart` still uses its own context-bound snack (the Add earlier videos sheet closes itself, and its Undo is of another kind).
  7. **Three additions to `taketray_ui`** (the dialog had no icon and no safe line, and a snack could only be shown from a live context): `showTtSnackOn(messenger, ..., persist:)` (the context-free half of `showTtSnack`, which now calls it; the snack bar around a `TtSnack` is now transparent in the theme, so the call needs no colour of its own), `TtDialog.icon` (a glyph above a centred title, `.dico`) and `TtDialogNote` (the check-shield and the success colours, `.safe`). `showTtDialog` takes `icon`. The package tests, a dialog golden scenario ("icon and note", macOS set) and the Widgetbook knobs (icon, note) are updated; the CI goldens are made by the controller.
  8. **New copy** (not in the owner's approved list; it is the failure line, so it needs a look): **"Something went wrong. Try again."** (`reelActionFailed`), for a failed save (in the sheet), delete or status change (snacks). The other new strings are the prototype's, verbatim (with "Recording" as Collecting).
  9. **`pumpScreen`** (the test helper) now has a `/reels` route (`reelsRouteMarker`) and passes the root messenger key to its `MaterialApp.router`, as the app does.
10. **Rule (fix round 1): a sheet's own failure is shown in the sheet; snacks only after it closes.** A sheet is modal, so a snack shown while it is open lies behind the sheet and its scrim (`hitTestable()` finds none of it; `find.text` alone finds covered widgets, which hid this in the first tests). A failed save therefore shows `SheetFailure` (`features/reels/views/sheet_failure.dart`) in the sheet's footer, above the buttons: a warning `TtBanner` (error container, error icon, so never colour alone, and a live region, so announced), with the sheet kept open and the text kept. It goes at the next Save attempt (and returns if that fails too) and at the next edit (typing, or choosing a status). `SheetFooter(failure:, buttons:)` is the footer to give `TtSheet.footer`; Tasks 8 and 9 reuse it for their sheets. No new `taketray_ui` component was needed (`TtBanner` already has its goldens, Widgetbook use case and accessibility tests); `SheetFailure` and `SheetFooter` have their own widget and `expectTtAccessible` tests. The success snacks ("{name} created", "Saved") are still on the root messenger and are shown after the sheet has closed. If the sheet was dragged away while the save ran and the save then fails, nothing is modal any more, so that one failure is a snack (`onFailedAfterClose`). A failure of an action whose dialog or menu has already closed (a failed delete, a failed status change) is a snack, because nothing modal is open then.
- Why: the owner asked for no duplicate names and Undo on every delete (D-80), and "Undo after leaving the screen" is only true if the snack is on a messenger that does not leave. A repository that throws for a problem is right for the data layer, but a form needs all its problems at once and in words, so the controller turns them into state. A double tap on Save or Delete is the commonest way to make two of something; the single-flight is what makes it one, whatever the buttons do. Writing only what changed keeps a refused rename from leaving half an edit.
- Alternatives considered:
  - a `ScaffoldMessenger` of the shell's own (a sub-tree messenger): it would not be the one pushed routes and the dialogs sit under, and the key makes "the root one" an explicit fact;
  - a global `GlobalKey` in a top-level `final`: two `MaterialApp`s alive at once (a test that builds a second app) would share it;
  - `ScaffoldMessenger.of(context)` after the pop: the context is gone, and the page's own messenger with it;
  - a single repository transaction for an edit (`updateDetails`): one more method and tests for a case that cannot half-fail except on a broken database, where the name-first order already keeps the refusable write first;
  - checking the form's name live while typing: the prototype shows the message on Save, and an error on the first keystroke is a scold;
  - showing the snack before the sheet closes: it would sit under the scrim for the 200 ms of the close.
- Consequences: Task 6 (Home) calls `showReelForm(context)` for **New reel** and uses `statusKindOf`, `statusName` and `reelStatusItems`; Task 8 puts `reelStatusItems` and `changeReelStatus` into `TtReelHeader`, calls `showReelForm(context, reel: reel)` and `showDeleteReelDialog(context, reel)` from the reel's ⋮ menu, and uses `showUndoSnack` for its clips; Tasks 9 and 10 use `showUndoSnack` too. A user who types a 41st character is stopped by the field, so "Use 40 characters or fewer" appears only if the repository refuses a long name (it should not); it is mapped anyway. A name that is only an emoji sequence the field counts differently from the repository would be a bug in one of the two (both use `characters`). On a phone with a screen reader on, the Undo snack stays until it is used or replaced (D-48). Hidden until their phase: the reference link (Phase 9) and the VN line of the delete dialog (Phase 8).

### D-86 · Home, the Reels tab: the order, the filters, the empty states, the midnight tick, the camera snack, and a stand-in reel page
- Date: 2026-10-31 · Phase: 4 · Task: 6 · Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-phase-4-reels.md` (the plan and the brief call this D-85; Task 7 took D-85 first, so Task 6 is D-86)
- Amended by the Phase 4 final-review fix wave (2026-10-31): a reel card opens its page through `OpenOnce` (`features/reels/views/open_once.dart`), so a double tap opens one reel page. The guard is a short window after the tap (`kThemeAnimationDuration * 2`, a `Timer` cancelled in `dispose`), long enough for the new page to cover the old one. It is **not** released by the push's future (fix round 1 did that): go_router completes that future only on a pop, and deleting a reel leaves its page with `router.go(Routes.reels)`, so after a delete no card opened again until a restart (fix round 2). `ReelsScreen` became a `ConsumerStatefulWidget` for it.
- Decision:
  1. **The screen** (`features/reels/views/reels_screen.dart › ReelsScreen`, which replaces `ReelsPlaceholderScreen`; `/reels` points at it). From the top: a large `TtAppBar` "Reels"; `ShootingModeSection` (the access banner, the Shooting mode card and the clips-waiting banner, D-76, reused as it is); when at least one reel exists, the filter chips; then the reels or an empty state; a `TtFab` "New reel" only while reels exist. Under the grid there is room for the nav pill and the FAB (`TtNavPill.fabBottom + TtMetrics.fabHeight + spacing.m`), with no reels only for the pill (`reservedHeight`). The FAB is a `PositionedDirectional` in a `Stack` at `TtNavPill.fabBottom(context)` from the bottom (D-82) and rises over a snack by itself.
  2. **Chips.** `TtPillTabs<ReelFilter>`: "All {n}", "Collecting {n}", "Ready to edit {n}", "Published {n}" (the prototype's "Recording" is Collecting, D-80). The counts are over all reels, whatever the filter. A screen reader hears "{name}, {n} reels" (`semanticLabel`). They scroll sideways when they do not fit, as `.chips` does in the prototype (at 360 dp, "Published" is past the edge at 1× text). Choosing one narrows the cards and keeps the order. Hidden when there is no reel at all.
  3. **Cards.** `SliverGrid.builder`, two columns, `spacing.s` (12 dp) between, `spacing.gutter` at the sides, `TtMetrics.reelCardAspect` (4:5). A card is a `TtReelCard` with: the cover; the status pill (`statusKindOf`, `statusName`, D-85); the meta "{n} clips · {m:ss}" (the length of the clips that are not missing, `formatClipDuration`, the To tag helper, so `m:ss` and `h:mm:ss`; a reel with no clips says "0 clips"; a reel whose clips are all missing says "2 clips · 0:00"); "{n} missing" when any; the empty art "No clips yet" without a cover; and the label "{name}. {status}. {n} clips[, {m} missing]" with plurals ("1 clip"). **Left out until their phase:** the VN pill (Phase 8) and the references badge (Phase 9). Tapping a card does `context.push(Routes.reel(id))`. 25 reels build a screenful of cards.
  4. **Empty states.** No reel at all: the `noReels` illustration, "Plan your first reel", the text, a **New reel** button (`showReelForm`), no FAB, no chips. A filter with none: the `emptyReel` illustration, "No reels marked {status}", the line for that status ("New reels start as Collecting.", "When a reel has all its clips, mark it as Ready to edit.", "When you post a reel, mark it as Published from its page."), and a tonal **Show all reels** that sets All. The chips and the FAB stay. While the first list is being read (and if the stream fails, there is no designed error state) nothing is shown below the Shooting mode card, so a phone with reels never flashes "Plan your first reel".
  5. **`HomeController`** (`view_models/home_controller.dart`, `@riverpod`, auto-dispose) is an `AsyncNotifier<HomeState>`: `HomeState { reels (the filtered list), filter, counts, covers }` and `hasReels`. It watches `reelSummariesProvider` (`ReelsRepository.watchReelSummaries`, D-84; one provider so a test can give it summaries) and `thumbnailCacheProvider`. The filter is a field of the notifier: `setFilter` re-composes the state from the last list at once, and the filter survives a new list (a reel was added) and a cache that becomes ready. `ReelFilter { all, collecting, ready, published }` in `reel_filters.dart` has the pure `filterCounts` (every filter is in the map, with 0) and `applyFilter` (keeps the order it was given, which is createdAt descending then id, D-84). The filter is not stored; a restart starts at All. **Covers** use `clipThumbnail` (D-76) at one size for every card, `homeCoverSize` = 240 logical pixels: a card is 4:5 and 160 to 190 dp wide, so its height is at most about 240, and one size keeps the thumbnail cache from holding a file per screen width. Until the cache is ready a cover is the "no picture yet" provider (the card shows its empty art) and is replaced when the cache arrives.
  6. **`localDayProvider`** (`core/midnight_tick.dart`, auto-dispose): a `Stream<DateTime>` of local midnight of today, now and again at each next local midnight. The timer is `DateTime(y, m, d + 1) - now` from `clockProvider`, so a 23- or 25-hour day (the London clock changes) ends at midnight, not after 24 hours. When the timer fires and the clock still shows the same day (a moment early, or the clock was set back), nothing is emitted and it is set again; it never repeats a day. The timer is cancelled when nothing watches the provider. `ShootingModeSection` and `ToTagScreen` `ref.watch` it, so "On since {time}" and "Shooting since {time}" get their date at midnight (a rebuild reads the clock again). Nothing else is rebuilt. **It also re-checks the day when the app comes back (`AppLifecycleState.resumed`, parked-items batch):** on Android a timer can fire late, or not at all before the user looks, after the phone slept through midnight. A `WidgetsBindingObserver` (added with the provider, removed with it) calls the same `tick()`, which now cancels the old timer first and sets a new one from the clock as it is then, so a resume emits the new day at once, emits nothing on the same day, and never leaves two timers. A zone change while the app was away is picked up the same way (the day is read in the zone the phone has now).
  7. **The camera snack.** `shooting_mode/views/open_camera.dart › openCameraOrSay(context, ref)` calls `ShootingModeController.openCamera()` and, when it is false, shows "Couldn't open the camera." (owner-approved, 2026-10-30) on the root messenger (D-85), looked up before the call. Every **Open camera** button uses it: the Shooting mode card, To tag's strip and To tag's "All caught up" button. The FAB rises over it like any snack.
  8. **A stand-in reel page.** The reel page is Task 8. So that a card has somewhere to go, `/reels/:id` is a child route of `/reels` (`Routes.reel(id)`, `Routes.reelIdParameter`) showing `ReelDetailPlaceholder` (a `Scaffold` with an app bar and nothing else; Task 8 replaces the file's use in `app_router.dart`). An id that is not a positive whole number (`/reels/abc`, `/reels/0`, `/reels/1.5`, a number too big for an int) redirects to Reels and logs the same "Unknown location" line as any unknown location; an id that is a number but names no reel is Task 8's to handle (today it shows the stand-in; amended by D-87: the real page sends the app to Reels).
  9. **The tab keeps its scroll position** because the shell keeps the tab built (D-76: `StatefulShellRoute.indexedStack`); a test scrolls it, goes to Settings and back. That test is about the shell, not the screen (it is named so, parked-items batch). A whole-app test (`reels_tab_test.dart`) also shows the camera snack above the nav pill and the FAB and touches it; there the Shooting mode switch makes a blinking dot, so nothing settles: the test pumps the snack in by hand (the frame that builds it, then time).
- Why: the owner wants Home to match the prototype with the Phase 4 names (D-80) and without the parts that belong to later phases. Keeping the filter in the controller and the narrowing in two pure functions makes the rules (counts that do not change with the filter, the order that is not changed) testable without a widget. The tick is a provider, not a timer inside a widget, so two screens share one timer and a test can fake the time. The camera snack is one function so a new Open camera button cannot forget it.
- Alternatives considered:
  - a `ScaffoldMessenger.of(context)` for the camera snack: it would be the screen's, and a tab switch right after the tap would take it away;
  - a cover size computed from the screen width (what To tag does for its tiles): the controller would need the layout, and the cache would hold a file per width;
  - a `Timer.periodic` of one minute that rebuilds the card: it wakes the phone 1,440 times a day for a text that changes once;
  - `Timer(Duration(hours: 24))`: wrong on the two London clock-change days of the year, and drifts if the app was started late in the day;
  - a skeleton for Home while it loads: the prototype has none, and the first read is a few milliseconds;
  - showing a list-error state: there is no copy or design for one, the stream is a local database read, and To tag does the same;
  - the filter in the URL or stored: neither the prototype nor the owner asked for it.
- Consequences:
  - `ReelsPlaceholderScreen` is gone; its tests and the Patrol tests refer to `ReelsScreen`.
  - Widget tests that pump a screen with `pumpScreen` get a midnight tick that never fires (an inert `localDayProvider`), because the test binding fails a test that ends with a timer pending, before any tear-down runs. A test about the tick passes `midnightTick: true`, which also disposes the container as the tree comes down (use it only with fake view models; a real database needs `testApp`). `tearDownApp` disposes the containers of the tree it takes down, for the same reason.
  - `fake_async` is a dev dependency of `app` (it was already in the lock file as a dependency of `flutter_test`), for the tick's test.
  - `midnight_tick_test.dart` is in `DST_TESTS` in `tool/check`: its London cases run with `TZ=Europe/London`.
  - The two `docs/later.md` items (the camera snack and the midnight rebuild) are done and deleted.
  - Home shows a reel's cover from the **oldest** clip that has a picture (D-84); the prototype uses the first live clip. They are the same when clips are in shooting order.
  - Phase 8 and 9 add the VN pill and the references badge to the card (`TtReelCard.vnLabel`, `referenceCount`), and they change the card's label ("Editing in VN").

### D-87 · Reel detail: the route inside the Reels tab, the check on open, shooting-day headers, a missing tile that is removed with Undo, the name in the body, and a missing tile at its real size
- Date: 2026-10-31 · Phase: 4 · Task: 8 · Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-phase-4-reels.md`
- Amended by the Phase 4 final-review fix wave (2026-10-31): a tile opens the viewer through `OpenOnce` (the same short window as Home's cards, D-86), so a double tap opens one viewer. Not the push's future: the viewer's fallback leaves it with `context.go` (D-89), which never completes it. The viewer is on the root navigator, so the reel page's `ModalRoute.isCurrent` stays true under it and cannot be the guard either.
- Decision:
  1. **Routes** (`router/app_router.dart`, `router/routes.dart`). `/reels/:id` builds `ReelDetailScreen` (it replaces D-86's stand-in, which is deleted). It stays a child of `/reels`, so the nav pill stays and the Reels tab stays selected, as in the prototype. A clip's location is a child of it: `Routes.clip(reelId, clipId)` is `/reels/{reelId}/clip/{clipId}`, which builds `ClipViewerPlaceholder` (a stand-in, Task 10 replaces it, as Task 6 did for the reel page). The reel's path parameter is still called `id` (`Routes.reelIdParameter`; the brief's `:reelId` is a way of writing it); the clip's is `Routes.clipIdParameter`. A clip id that is not a positive whole number is as unknown as a reel id like that: it goes to Reels and logs the same "Unknown location" line. **A good number that names no reel, or a reel deleted while its page is open, goes to Reels too** (D-86's carry-over): `ReelDetailController`'s state is `null`, and the screen, in the frame after it sees that, calls `context.go(Routes.reels)` once. **Back** pops, or goes to Reels when nothing can pop (a page opened by a link).
  2. **`ReelDetailController`** (`features/reels/view_models/reel_detail_controller.dart`, `@riverpod`, family on `int reelId`, auto-dispose, `retry: noRetry`) builds `ReelDetailState? { reel, days: List<ShootingDay<ReelClip>>, clipCount, missingCount, liveDurationMs, times }`. It awaits two stream providers, `reelByIdProvider` (`watchById`) and `reelClipsProvider` (`watchReelClips`, D-84), so every change shows. `null` means the reel is gone; loading is the provider's own loading state. **Two differences from the brief's interface:** there is no `descriptionOpen` (`TtReelHeader` keeps whether Shot ideas is open, and has no callback for it, so a copy in the state would be one nobody could change), and there is a `times` map (the screen needs each clip's `ClipLocalTime`).
  3. **Verify on open** (spec §8.3, "verified when a reel is shown"). The first time `build` has the reel's clips, once per open and only if there are any, it starts `MediaVerifier.verify(clips)` (D-72: on the shared `MediaWorkQueue`, full access only, an error never means missing) without awaiting it, inside `guarded`. A video that is gone is flagged, the list stream emits again and the tile turns into a missing tile. A later change of the list or of the times does not verify again (a field of the notifier, which Riverpod keeps across rebuilds); closing and opening the page does. The log line holds counts only. **Not done:** verifying again when the app returns to the front while the page is open (To tag does, D-75). A video deleted meanwhile shows as missing at the next open.
