  4. **The times never hold the tiles back.** `reelClipTimesProvider` asks `ClipTimeService.localTimes` (D-83) for the reel's clips and is **read, not awaited**, by the controller: the first state has `times` empty, its days are grouped with the phone's own zone (`groupByShootingDay`'s fallback, Dart's `toLocal`) and its tiles show the phone's time (`timeOfDayText`); when the answer arrives the state is built again with `times`, the days are regrouped by the date where each clip was shot and the tiles show `wallClockTimeText(localTime.wallClock)`. A clip whose time is not known yet (a new one) uses the phone's. **The wall clock is never converted** (`toLocal` is not called on it): its fields are the time.
  5. **Day headers** (controller ruling G: they follow the prototype, with the weekday). "Day {n} · {date}" with "{k} clips" after it (`TtDayHeader`), where `n` is `ShootingDay.number` (calendar days from the first day, D-83) and the date is `ttDayWeekdayText(context, day.date, today:)`, a new helper next to `ttDayText` in `taketray_ui` (`internal/day_text.dart`): `MMMEd` in the phone's locale for a day of this year ("Wed, Oct 28" on en-US, "Wed, 28 Oct" on en-IN) and `yMMMEd` for another year ("Tue, Dec 30, 2025"; en-IN writes "Tue, 30 Dec, 2025"), with every space a no-break space, as in D-79, so a date never wraps in the middle. `today` comes from `localDayProvider`, so the year follows the calendar at New Year. `ttDayText` itself is unchanged (the date range picker, Add earlier videos and the rest keep "Oct 28"). The format uses the app's locale (`Localizations.localeOf`), whose date data Flutter loads for the locales the app resolves (D-79). The first version used `ttDayText` without the weekday; the controller overruled it.
  6. **Tiles.** A three-column lazy `SliverGrid` for each day (`SliverLayoutBuilder` gives the real width), `TtClipTile` with the time, the length (`formatClipDuration`), `reelCount` (the layers badge from 2), `horizontal` when `width > height`, and the label "Clip, {time}, {duration}" or "Clip, {time}, {duration}, in {k} reels". **Tap** pushes `Routes.clip(reelId, clipId)`; **long press** calls `ReelDetailScreen.onClipLongPress(clipId)`, which is null until Task 9 starts choosing clips with it. 300 clips over 20 days build a screenful, not 300.
  7. **A missing clip** is a `TtMissingClipTile` "Missing from phone" with **Remove** (`clipRemove`), which calls `ReelDetailController.removeMissing(clipId)` and shows "Missing clip removed" with **Undo** on the root messenger (`ReelSnacks`, D-85). `removeMissing` removes the link only when the clip is **in this reel and flagged missing** at that moment (a verify may have found the video again while the tile was still on the screen); otherwise it changes nothing and returns an empty record, and the screen shows nothing. It is single-flight per clip id (a double tap shares one future and one record). A failure is logged by type and thrown; the screen shows "Something went wrong. Try again." (D-85). The record is `RemoveUndo` (D-84): Undo puts back the same link with its `addedAt`, and the same clip with its id if the orphan sweep deleted it. A missing tile has no tap: the viewer never plays a clip flagged missing (spec §4).
  8. **The name is in the body.** The app bar has **Back** and the ⋮ menu and **no title** (the prototype's `title: ''`), so the name is not clamped at large text; `TtReelHeader` shows it over as many lines as it needs. That is `docs/later.md`'s "app bar titles clamp at 1.5×" item, done for this screen.
  9. **A missing tile at its real size** (`docs/later.md`'s item from Task 2). `TtMissingClipTile.heightFor(context, width:, label:, removeLabel:)` (a `taketray_ui` addition, no look changes) measures what the tile needs at the phone's text scale: the paddings, the 26 dp icon, the label wrapped at the tile's inner width with the same style, and the Remove button (48 dp at large text). A day that has a missing tile gets `mainAxisExtent = max(3:4 height, heightFor)`: at 2× text on 360 dp that is about 192 dp, not 142. The other tiles of that day sit at the top of their cell at their 3:4 shape (an `Align`), the missing tile fills its cell and is not shrunk. Days with no missing tile keep the plain 3:4 grid, and at 1× nothing changes. **Not solved:** at about 2.5× and above a single word wider than the tile ("Missing") is still shrunk to fit (D-81), because that is a width problem; Android's largest text size is 2×.
  10. **Header and menus.** `TtReelHeader<ReelStatus>`: the name; the status pill that opens `reelStatusItems` and chooses with `changeReelStatus` ("Marked as {status}", D-85); "{n} clips · {m:ss}" (the length of the clips that are not missing) or "No clips yet"; "{m} missing"; and Shot ideas with Show more / Show less. The ⋮ menu: **Reel details** (`showReelForm(context, reel:)`), **Select clips** and **Delete reel** (`showDeleteReelDialog`, D-85, which goes to Reels with Undo). **Select clips** calls `ReelDetailScreen.onSelectClips`, null until Task 9 fills it in (Task 9 can also replace both callbacks with its own selection controller and delete them). Left out and hidden (D-80): the VN banner, the references section, Add videos and the Edit video button.
  11. **Loading and empty.** Loading (and a first read that fails: there is no designed error state, as on To tag and Home): a skeleton day header and 9 skeleton tiles, read as "Loading". No clips: the `emptyReel` illustration, "No clips yet", "Tag clips from To tag, or add videos from your phone." and one tonal **Go to To tag** with the tag icon (Add videos is Phase 7, so there is one button; "add videos from your phone" is the brief's sentence and names a button that is not there yet).
- Why: the reel page is where the owner sees whether a reel is ready, so it has to be honest: a video that is gone must show as gone (the check on open), a clip shot abroad must be on the day and at the time it was shot, and nothing a person has to read may be cut off at the largest text size. The check runs without holding the page up and without being able to fail it, as To tag's does. The two removals the brief asks to guard (a tile that was found again, and a double tap) cost three lines each in the controller and are the kind of race Review Focus 4 names.
- Alternatives considered:
  - **Keeping `descriptionOpen` in the controller state**: it would need a callback in `TtReelHeader`, so that the state could be told, only to give the header back what it already remembers.
  - **Waiting for the times before showing the tiles** (so no tile ever changes its time): a hung bridge would keep skeletons up for the 15 s timeouts; To tag was changed away from this in D-83.
  - **Verifying in the notifier's `build` every time it runs**: it runs again for every list and time change, which would start a verify per change, and a verify changes the list.
  - **A taller grid for every row at large text** (`mainAxisExtent` on every day): ordinary tiles would be stretched, or have empty bands, to make room for a tile that is not there.
  - **A `Scale`/`FittedBox` for the missing tile** (what D-81 did): the text is 0.7 of its size at 2×, which is what the owner's note called wrong.
  - **`context.pop()` for a reel that is gone**: the page may have been opened by a link, with nothing under it; `go` always lands on Reels.
  - **A `ref.listen` for the redirect**: it does not fire for a value already there at the first build; checking in `build` and scheduling one `go` for after the frame handles both.
- Consequences:
  - `ReelDetailPlaceholder` and its file are gone. D-86 point 8's "an id that names no reel is Task 8's" is done here.
  - Task 9 fills `onSelectClips` and `onClipLongPress` (or replaces them), adds the selection bar, and may add its own state to the screen. Task 10 replaces `ClipViewerPlaceholder` in `app_router.dart`; the viewer must still not play a clip flagged missing.
  - `TtMissingClipTile.heightFor` is public API of `taketray_ui`, with tests, a macOS golden scenario ("in a cell grown for the text") and a Widgetbook knob. The CI goldens (`goldens/ci/missing_clip_tile_*.png`) are regenerated by the controller.
  - The reel page's tile times and day headers are covered by `reel_detail_screen_test.dart` and, for the controller, `reel_detail_controller_test.dart`; `tool/check`'s coverage for `features/*/view_models/` stays above 80 %.
  - The test helper `pumpScreen` has a `/reels/:id/clip/:clipId` route (`clipRouteMarker`), and `test/support/reel_videos.dart` puts videos on the fake phone and in a reel.

### D-88 · Multi-select on the reel page and the reel picker: what can be chosen, add and move, a new reel inline, the failure in the sheet, and the snack copy
- Date: 2026-10-31 · Phase: 4 · Task: 9 · Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-phase-4-reels.md`
- Amended by the Phase 4 final-review fix wave (2026-10-31): the snack's Undo for an add to several reels runs the records last to first in **one** transaction (`undoAll`, D-84 point 9): all of them come back or none, and the same list can be undone again after a failure. `ClipActionOutcome.clipCount`'s comment now says what it holds (an add: every clip asked for; a move or a remove: the links taken out of the source).
- Decision:
  1. **`ReelSelection`** (`features/reels/view_models/reel_selection.dart`, `@riverpod`, family on `int reelId`, auto-dispose with the page) holds `ReelSelectionState { selecting, selected }`. `start(id)` (long press), `toggle(id)`, `selectAll()`, `clear()` as briefed, and **`begin()`**, which is **Select clips** in the ⋮ menu: choosing with nothing chosen yet (the prototype's `startSelBtn`; the bar says "0 selected" and the three actions are disabled). Rules:
     - **Only a clip that is in this reel and not missing can be chosen.** `start` and `toggle` ignore a missing clip, a clip of another reel and any clip before the reel's clips have been read; `selectAll` takes every clip that is not missing and chooses nothing for a reel that has only missing ones.
     - **Choosing ends when the last chosen clip is un-chosen** (`toggleSel` in the prototype). `begin` is the only way to be choosing with nothing chosen.
     - **It follows the reel's clips** (`ref.listen(reelClipsProvider)`): a chosen clip that goes missing (a verify) or leaves the reel is dropped; if that empties a selection that had clips, choosing ends. Without this an action could be run on a clip that is not there.
  2. **The page** (`ReelDetailScreen`). The test-only hooks `onSelectClips` and `onClipLongPress` are gone (the constructor is `ReelDetailScreen(reelId:)`); the router-built screen supports selection. While choosing, the app bar is `TtAppBar(selection: true, liveRegion: true)`: close (`Close`), "{n} selected" (**read out by a screen reader each time the count changes**: `TtAppBar.liveRegion`, added by the parked-items batch, makes a selection bar's title a live region and is ignored on any other bar), **Add to another reel** (`library_add`), **Move to another reel** (`drive_file_move`), **Remove from this reel** (`playlist_remove`) and a ⋮ with **Select all** only (Edit video and Share are Phase 8, hidden, D-80). Tiles get `selected` (the check and `toggled` semantics); a tap or a long press chooses or un-chooses and **does not open the viewer**. A `PopScope(canPop: !selecting)` makes Back and the system back gesture end choosing instead of leaving the page. The missing tile is not selectable and its **Remove** still works while choosing. The action buttons are disabled with nothing chosen.
  3. **`ReelPickerMode { add, move }`** and `showReelPicker(context, mode:, clipCount:, excludeReelId:, perform:)` (`views/reel_picker_sheet.dart`) return the ids picked or null. Tag mode is Phase 5.
     - **Title and line:** "Add clip to reels" / "Add {n} clips to reels" with "It stays in this reel too." / "They stay in this reel too." (owner-approved); "Move clip to" / "Move {n} clips to" with "It leaves {reel}." / "They leave {reel}." (plural keys; the reel's name is read from `reelByIdProvider`, "this reel" if it cannot be).
     - **Rows:** first **New reel** (`TtReelPickNewRow`), then a **Collecting** section, then **Other reels** (Ready to edit and Published), each only when it has reels, newest first (`pickerSections`, a pure function). **The reel the clips are in is left out.** A row is `TtReelPickRow` with the cover (`clipThumbnail` at `homeCoverSize`, so the cache holds one file per cover for Home and the picker), "{n} clips" and the label "{name}, {n} clips". Add is **multi-select** (check boxes, picks kept in tap order), move **single-select** (radio buttons; a new pick replaces the old, tapping the pick again keeps it): `togglePick`.
     - **Buttons:** **Cancel**, and **Add** (with nothing picked) / "Add to 1 reel" / "Add to {n} reels", or **Move**; the confirm button is disabled until a reel is picked and while a confirm runs.
  4. **A new reel inline.** **New reel** becomes a `TtTextField` "New reel name" with a tonal **Create** (the prototype's `pkname`). It reuses Task 7's `ReelFormController(null)` (`save` checks empty, too long and duplicate against the reels as they are now, is single-flight, and keeps the problem in its state) and the one `reelNameErrorText` that the New reel sheet also uses, so the words ("Give this reel a name", "You already have a reel called {name}") exist once. A refused name shows under the field and the field stays; a typing clears it. On success the reel is created, **picked** (add: joins the picks; move: becomes the only pick, `pickedAfterCreate`), and the field closes. Any other failure ("Something went wrong. Try again.") is shown in the sheet.
  5. **The sheet's own failure is told in the sheet (D-85).** The brief says the caller runs the action; the sheet's failure must still be in the sheet, so `showReelPicker` has an optional **`perform(List<int> reelIds)`**: confirm runs it, closes with the ids only when it ended well, and on a throw stays open with its picks and shows `SheetFailure` ("Something went wrong. Try again.") until the next pick or confirm. The caller (the page) passes `perform` and shows the **success snack after the sheet has closed**. If the sheet was dragged away while the action ran, `perform` shows the snack itself when it finishes (or the failure snack), because nothing is modal any more. Without `perform` the sheet only returns the ids.
  6. **`ReelClipActions`** (`view_models/reel_clip_actions.dart`, kept alive) wraps the repository calls and returns a `ClipActionOutcome { records, reelNames, clipCount, isEmpty }` for the snack:
     - `addToReels(clipIds:, reelIds:)` makes **one** `ClipsRepository.addClipsToReels` call (D-84 point 8, parked-items batch) for the reels in pick order and gives one `AddUndo` for each. The repository adds to every reel in one transaction, so **if one reel fails, nothing was added to any**, with no compensating undo (the first version added reel by reel and undid the done ones on a failure; that code is gone). A reel that is gone throws `StateError` before anything is written (the names are read first), or from inside the transaction if it vanished after that.
     - `moveToReel(fromReelId:, toReelId:, clipIds:)` is one `moveClips` (`MoveUndo`); `removeFromReel(reelId:, clipIds:)` is one `removeClipsFromReel` (`RemoveUndo`; `clipCount` is the links removed).
     - Logs hold ids and counts only.
  7. **Snacks, all on the root messenger with Undo** (`ReelSnacks`, D-85): add "Added to {A}", "Added to {A} and {B}", "Added to {A}, {B} and {n} more" (owner-approved; four reels is "Added to A, B and 2 more"); move "Moved to {A}"; remove "{n} clips removed from {reel}. The videos are still in your gallery." and, for one clip, "1 clip removed from {reel}. The video is still in your gallery." (the brief asks for a plural key; the singular sentence is the prototype's own wording for one video). **`ReelSnacks.showUndoAll(message, records)`** is new: one **Undo** undoes every record last to first (an add to three reels is three records), tries all even if one fails, and shows "Couldn't undo. Try again." if any did. `showUndo` is `showUndoAll` with one record. When nothing changed there is nothing to undo: an add of clips every chosen reel has already shows its plain snack with no Undo; a **move that moved nothing** (every chosen clip had left the reel meanwhile) and a remove of clips the reel does not have show **nothing** (parked-items batch: the move used to say "Moved to {A}" with no Undo for a move that did not happen). After every action the selection ends. Undo works after leaving the page (the snack, the container and the words are looked up before anything is awaited).
  8. **A tap on Remove twice removes once** (`_removing` on the page), and **a tap on Add or Move twice opens one picker** (`_picking`, set around `pickAndPerform`, released when it ends; parked-items batch). Choosing is kept when a remove fails, so it can be tried again.
- Why: moving and adding are the commonest things a creator does after shooting, and each can lose or duplicate a video if done carelessly, so every rule is in a pure function or a view model with a test: what can be chosen, which reels are offered, that move is single-select, and that a failed multi-reel add leaves nothing behind. The sheet shows its own failure because a snack under a modal scrim is not seen (D-85). One `reelNameErrorText` and one controller keep the inline field from drifting away from the New reel sheet.
- Alternatives considered:
  - the picker returning only the ids and the caller running the action: the failure would have to be a snack behind the closed sheet, or the sheet would close before the work is known to have worked; `perform` keeps the brief's return value;
  - one `UndoRecord` that holds several (a new sealed subtype): the data layer would learn a UI shape; a list in the snack is enough;
  - one repository method for "add to several reels" in a single transaction: first rejected (the repository was fixed by D-84 and the compensating undo was small; the cost was that the user could see the first reel change for a moment on a failure), then **done** in the parked-items batch as `addClipsToReels` (D-84 point 8), because an undo that itself can fail is a weaker promise than a transaction that cannot half-commit;
  - a `PopScope` per tile or a selection route: choosing is a state of this page, not a place;
  - showing the selection actions as a bottom bar: the prototype and the brief use the app bar.
- Consequences:
  - D-87's `onSelectClips` and `onClipLongPress` no longer exist.
  - `taketray_ui` gained `TtAppBar.liveRegion` (a bool, false by default, honoured only with `selection: true`), with a unit test of the semantics flag, a knob in the Widgetbook use case and `expectTtAccessible` for the live selection bar. It changes no pixel, so no golden changed.
  - `reelNameErrorText` moved out of the New reel sheet's `build` and is public in `reel_form_sheet.dart`.
  - The picker has no Widgetbook story or golden: it is a feature sheet built from existing components (`TtSheet`, `TtReelPickRow`, `TtReelPickNewRow`, `TtGroupLabel`, `TtTextField`), none of which changed. The viewer (Task 10) uses `showReelPicker` for its Add and Move, with the same `ReelClipActions`.
  - The tests' `flagForTest` moved from `reel_detail_screen_test.dart` to `test/support/reel_videos.dart`, because the selection tests use it too.

### D-89 · The clip viewer: the original plays from its `content://` address, one player for the page showing, the leave rule, the can't-play state, the city label, and `video_player` 2.14.1
- Date: 2026-10-31 · Phase: 4 · Task: 10 · Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-phase-4-reels.md`
- Amended by the Phase 4 final-review fix wave (2026-10-31): point 5's swipe, `onPageChanged` now **pauses the outgoing page's player** (it keeps its frame until the swipe settles), so two videos are never heard at once while the finger holds the swipe past the middle; and a swipe that turns back before it settles **plays the returning page's player again** (it is still open, and was just paused). Neither opens a player (`ref.exists` first).
- Decision:
  1. **The address** (`platform/media_bridge.dart › clipVideoUri(Clip)`, pure, no channel). A MediaStore clip plays `content://media/{volumeName}/video/media/{mediaStoreId}` (built with `Uri(pathSegments: ...)`, so odd characters are encoded), only when `mediaStoreId > 0` and the volume is not empty. A picked clip plays its `pickerUri` as stored (`Uri.tryParse`: an address that does not parse is none). Everything else gives `null`, **and so does a clip flagged `missing`**, whatever its identity says: MediaStore may have given the number to another video, and playing it is the worst bug (spec §4). Each source uses only its own identity (a stray `pickerUri` on a MediaStore clip is ignored). The video plays straight from the gallery and is never copied (D-10).
  2. **The route** (`router/app_router.dart`). `/reels/:id/clip/:clipId` is still a child of the reel's page in the Reels branch (so Back returns to it and the branch keeps it), with `parentNavigatorKey` set to the router's own root navigator key: the viewer is drawn **above the tab shell, with no nav pill**. The key is made inside the `appRouter` provider, so two routers never share one. `ClipViewerPlaceholder` is deleted.
  3. **`ClipViewerController`** (`view_models/clip_viewer_controller.dart`, family on `(reelId, clipId)`, auto-dispose, no retry) builds `ClipViewerState? { reel, clips, index, times }` from the same streams as the reel page (`reelByIdProvider`, `reelClipsProvider`, `reelClipTimesProvider`, D-87). **The pager is the reel's clips that are not missing, in the reel's order.** It opens on `clipId` or on nothing: if that clip is missing, not in the reel, or the reel is gone, the state is `null` and the screen goes back; it never opens another clip. It stays on its clip **by id** (the index is looked up each time, so a clip joining or leaving elsewhere does not move it). `goTo(i)` is the page changing. **The leave rule** (the prototype's `viewerAfterLeave`; a clip flagged missing while it is being watched leaves the pager the same way, so the viewer moves to its neighbour, and that neighbour **autoplays** like any page that is shown): when the clip on screen leaves the pager (removed or moved here, removed by another screen, or turned missing by a verify) the viewer goes to the clip that now stands in its place (the next one), the one before it when the last was left, and with none left the state is `null` and the screen goes back. It follows the database, so Undo while the viewer is open puts the clip back in the pager without moving the viewer. **Two differences from the brief's interface:** the reels the clip is in are a separate provider, `clipReelNamesProvider(clipId)` (`watchReelIdsOf` plus `watchAll`, names in the order the reels were made), because they depend on the clip on screen and the controller's family key is only the clip it opened on; and the times are the reel's whole map, as on the reel page.
  4. **The player** (`view_models/clip_player.dart › clipPlayerProvider(Uri)`, auto-dispose, no retry). One `VideoPlayerController.contentUri` (ExoPlayer) for the page showing: it is made when the page is shown, opened, set to the mute switch's volume and **played at once**, and disposed by the provider when the page changes (the screen stops watching the old address) and when the screen is popped. A page that was never shown never makes one, and the other pages are black. A video that cannot be opened makes the future throw (`AsyncError`), logged by exception type only. **Pause on `paused`** (and `hidden`, which comes first): the provider adds its own `WidgetsBindingObserver`, removed with it, which also remembers that the app is in the background (from `WidgetsBinding.instance.lifecycleState` at the start, then each change) so that **a video that finishes opening while the app is away is not started** (fix round 1: with the plugin's own check off, `play()` after an `initialize()` that outlived `paused` would have been heard from the background), and the plugin's own lifecycle observer is switched off (`VideoPlayerOptions(allowBackgroundPlayback: true)` only does that; on Android it makes nothing play in the background), because the plugin resumes a video that was playing when the app returns, and a video the user left should not start by itself. **Mute** (`viewerMutedProvider`, kept alive, not stored): starts off (sound on), the toggle sets the volume of the playing video and the next one opens the same way, until the app restarts.
  5. **The screen** (`views/clip_viewer_screen.dart`), always dark: `Theme(TakeTrayTheme.video())` around a `Scaffold`, whose body is a `Stack` of a `TtVideoSurface(scrims: true)` holding the `PageView`, the header and the ‹ › arrows. The Scaffold exists **for the snacks**: they are shown by the Scaffolds under the root messenger (D-85), and the tab shell's is covered by this route, so without its own an Undo snack would be invisible until the viewer closed. The controls are its **bottom bar**, so a snack comes up above them and leaves Add/Move/Remove in reach. The controls sit outside the surface: its scrims are painted over its child, which dimmed the white labels below the contrast limit.
     - **Swipe (parked-items batch).** `onPageChanged` fires at the middle of a swipe and the viewer's index moves then, but the page going out is still half on screen. `_outgoingClipId` (set in `onPageChanged`, cleared when the pager's `ScrollEndNotification` arrives) keeps that page's `_Stage`, with its player, until the swipe settles, so it does not turn black at the midpoint; the incoming page opens its player at once as before. For that moment two players live; once settled only the page showing has one. The pages' own Remove buttons act on **their** clip (`_remove(clipId)`), never on "the current one", so the outgoing page's can't-play Remove cannot take out the clip that came up. A stale `_outgoingClipId` is never kept (a drag back short of the middle starts no player).
     - **The "In" row (parked-items batch)** keeps the names it showed while the clip that just came up has not answered yet (`clipReelNamesProvider` is a provider for each clip), so it does not vanish for a frame at each page change. It shows the previous clip's reels for that frame, then the right ones.
     - **The stage button (parked-items batch)** is announced with a **state** ("Playing" / "Paused", the semantics `value`) and a **hint** for what a tap does ("Pause" / "Play"), after its label "Clip {i} of {n}" (`viewerStagePlaying`, `viewerStagePaused`, `viewerStageHintPause`, `viewerStageHintPlay`).
     - **Header** (`TtPlayerHeader`): Back; "{i} of {n} · {reel}" over "{day} · {time}", from `ClipLocalTime.wallClock` as it is (`ttDayWeekdayText`, the weekday as the prototype's `fmt.day`, and `wallClockTimeText` with the phone's 24-hour setting; `toLocal()` is never called on it, D-83), plus " · {city} time" **only when `!isPhoneZone && zoneCity(zoneId) != null`**; the mute toggle ("Mute" / "Unmute"). **The time line is empty until `ClipTimeService` has answered**: the phone's time would be the wrong one, so unlike the tiles (D-87) it does not show it first. **If reading the times fails** (`reelClipTimesProvider` is an error, not loading; `ClipViewerState.timesFailed`) the line shows the **phone's** time and day (`dateTakenUtc.toLocal()`, as the reel page's tiles do), with no city, so it is not empty for ever (parked-items batch).
     - **Stage:** each page is the video fitted whole (`AspectRatio(value.aspectRatio)`, a vertical and a horizontal video), a tap plays or pauses (a play mark shows while paused), a swipe or an arrow moves (`animateToPage`; `jumpToPage` when the phone removes animations, because a zero duration is an assertion error), and an arrow is disabled at its end. The stage has the label "Clip {i} of {n}" for a screen reader.
     - **Bottom:** `TtScrubBar` (position and length from the player, seek by tap or drag; before the player is open, 0:00 and the clip's own length, no seek), "In" with a `TtReelTag` for each reel the clip is in, and `TtViewerActions` with three actions (Share is Phase 8, D-80). The bottom scrolls rather than push the controls off a small screen (at most 60 % of the height).
  6. **Can't play.** A player error while opening, an error while playing (`value.hasError`), or no usable address shows the error icon, "This video can't be played", "It may be damaged, or in a format your phone doesn't support. The file is still in your gallery." and a tonal **Remove from reel**. No other clip is played in its place (the test lists the addresses the fake platform was asked for). The icon is the 32 dp large icon, not the prototype's 48: there is no 48 dp token and the viewer adds no component.
  7. **Actions.** **Add to reel** and **Move** open the picker through `pickAndPerform` (`views/reel_picker_sheet.dart`, fix round 1; the one function the reel page's Add/Move uses too: the picker, `perform` inside the sheet, the snack after it closes, the dragged-away case), with one clip and the current reel excluded; **Remove** runs `ReelClipActions.removeFromReel` and shows "Removed from {reel}. The video is still in your gallery." with **Undo** on the root messenger. A second tap on Remove while it runs does nothing; a failure shows "Something went wrong. Try again.". After Move or Remove the pager changes by the leave rule, and the screen follows its index (`jumpToPage` after the frame). **A second press on Add or Move while the picker is opening does nothing** (`_pickerOpen`, parked-items batch). **Going back waits for the picker** (`_pickerOpen`): the viewer and the picker are routes of the same navigator, so a pop while the picker is up would close the picker, not the viewer. When the last clip is moved, or the clip goes while the picker is open, the picker finishes first and then the viewer goes back. Undo after the viewer closed still restores (the snack, the container and the words are looked up before anything is awaited).
  8. **`video_player` 2.14.1** (`video_player_android` 2.12.2, which uses ExoPlayer; published 2026-10-02, 29 days before this task; the newest 2.x that was at least two weeks old). `video_player_platform_interface ^6.9.0` is a dev dependency, for the fake platform the tests install. Release manifest: `INTERNET` is still removed (`tools:node="remove"`, D-31) and `tool/check_release_permissions` ends with `RELEASE PERMISSIONS OK`, test-code dex check included. **The plugin's ExoPlayer (media3) merges `WAKE_LOCK` and `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` into the release manifest** (fix round 1: they are removed with `tools:node="remove"` next to the `INTERNET` line in `AndroidManifest.xml`, and both are in `tool/check_release_permissions`'s forbidden list, checked in the merged manifest and again in `aapt2 dump permissions`). `video_player_android` never sets a wake mode and media3 treats the network state as optional; Task 11 proves playback on the phone, and if it fails there the removal is reverted.
  9. **Tests.** The unit and widget tests never touch a player: `test/support/fake_video_player.dart › FakeVideoPlayerPlatform` extends `VideoPlayerPlatform` (players, a `live` list so a leak shows, a call log, failing addresses, `hold`, `failPlayer`). Patrol on the S24 (Task 11) proves real playback. Because the old player is disposed on the event loop after the frame that let go of it, widget tests that look at `platform.live` settle in real time as well (`runAsync(settle)` after `pumpAndSettle`).
- Why: the viewer is where the owner checks a take, so what it plays must be the clip it says, and nothing it cannot play may fall back to another video (Review Focus 3). Building the address from the stored identity only, and not at all for a missing clip, makes that a property of one pure function. Making the pager a view of the database gives the leave rule, the skipping of missing clips and Undo-while-open for free, with no copy of the list to keep right. One player at a time keeps ExoPlayer from holding several decoders on a phone, and a player owned by a provider is released by the same mechanism that stops the page watching it, on both a page change and a pop.
- Alternatives considered:
  - **One player per page in the `PageView`:** two or three decoders alive, and the neighbours playing sound while off screen.
  - **A controller `index` that is the source of truth, moved by the leave logic in the screen:** the list changes (Undo, a verify, another screen) would each need handling by hand; deriving the index from the clip's id handles them all.
  - **Keeping the plugin's lifecycle handling:** it would resume a video the user had left when the app came back.
  - **Showing a missing clip's can't-play state with a pager of one:** the reel page's missing tile has no tap, so the viewer is reached for a missing clip only by a race or a link; going back is simpler and as safe.
  - **A poster (the thumbnail) behind the video:** nicer on a swipe, but a second place that could show the wrong picture; black until the player is open is plain and honest.
  - **Leaving `WAKE_LOCK` and `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` in the manifest** (the first version): an offline app that plays local files has no use for either, so they are removed (point 8).
- Consequences:
  - `ClipViewerPlaceholder` and its file are gone; `pump_screen.dart`'s `/reels/:id/clip/:clipId` marker route stays for the reel page's tests.
  - Task 11 proves on the S24: the `content://media` address plays (MediaStore and a picked clip), swipe, arrows, tap, seek, mute, pause on Home, the can't-play state for a damaged file, and the release manifest has no `INTERNET`.
  - The arrows are centred on the whole screen, not inset 120 dp at the top and 240 dp at the bottom as in the prototype; the video is not shown under a bottom scrim as in the prototype (the controls sit above their own surface).
  - The viewer has no golden and no Widgetbook story: it is a feature screen built from existing components (`TtPlayerHeader`, `TtStageArrow`, `TtScrubBar`, `TtReelTagRow`, `TtViewerActions`, `TtVideoSurface`), none of which changed.
  - A clip in 25 reels shows 25 tags; at 2× text the bottom scrolls.
  - `docs/later.md`: the zone-alias item (D-83) still applies ("Kiev time" can show for the phone's own zone).

### D-90 · Add earlier videos stays on the Shooting mode card whenever access is full, with Shooting mode on or off, and is no longer one use per session (owner request)
- Date: 2026-10-31 · Phase: 4 · Final-review fix wave (owner request, 2026-10-31; the controller agreed) · Amends D-73 and D-79
- Decision:
  1. **When the row shows.** `ShootingModeSection` puts the "Add earlier videos" `TtCardRow` (link) in the card's `rows` whenever `access?.level == full` and the Shooting state was really read (`shootingState.hasValue`), with Shooting mode **on or off**. It is hidden in Manual mode (limited or denied access), while the access level or the Shooting state is loading, and when the Shooting state could not be read (the card then shows "off" so its switch works, but nothing is known). It **stays after an add**.
  2. **On:** the same row as before: the history icon, "Add earlier videos", "Shot before Shooting mode was on", a chevron. **Off:** the same row with the line **"From your camera folder"** (`earlierCardRowSubtitleOff`, screen reader "Add earlier videos, from your camera folder", `earlierCardRowLabelOff`). The copy is the controller's call; the owner can veto it.
  3. **`TtShootingModeCard` off renders `rows`**, under the top part, each in the off card's hairline look: no frosted band, a `hairline` line above, the title in `onSurface`, the line and the glyphs in `onSurfaceVariant` (a private `_OffCardScope` tells a link `TtCardRow` it sits on an off card; a row outside a card, or on the on card, looks as before). The off card still has no to-tag strip and no camera button. Tests in `shooting_mode_card_test.dart`, macOS goldens `shooting_mode_card_off_rows_{light,dark}.png` (1× and 2×), and the Widgetbook use case's row knob works in both states.
  4. **The one-use mark is gone.** `PhoneStateRepository.markEarlierAdded`, its call in `MediaScanner.addEarlier` and the `earlierAdded` field of `ShootingState` are removed. Stored JSON that still has `earlierAdded` (true or false) reads as before: json_serializable ignores the unknown key, and the next write of the state drops it.
  5. The To tag ⋮ menu item is unchanged.
- Why: the owner added videos, the row vanished, and it came back only after turning Shooting mode off and on, which they found wrong. Earlier videos are worth adding whether or not a Shooting session runs, and a second add after a first is normal (another day, a video the first range missed).
- Alternatives considered: keeping the row only while on (the owner asked for off too); bringing it back after Undo (still one use); a different off look, such as the on card's frosted band on the light card (no contrast on a light surface); keeping `earlierAdded` stored but unused (dead state).
- Consequences: on a 600 dp test screen the empty Reels tab's **New reel** button is now below the fold with full access (the off card is a row taller); `reels_tab_test.dart` scrolls to it. The CI goldens for `shooting_mode_card_off_rows` must be made by the "update goldens" workflow and pulled with `tool/pull_ci_goldens` (no `goldens/ci/` image was touched here). Patrol's `media_scan_test.dart` uses the To tag menu, not the card, so it is unaffected.

### D-91 · Phase 5 scope: To tag with scenes, the tag player, Skip, Skip all and Skipped; sticky Pick several; Ready and Published reels folded; Undo goes back to the clip; the prototype's video scrim
- Date: 2026-10-31 · Phase: 5 · Plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-31-phase-5-tagging.md` (owner approved 2026-10-31)
- Decision:
  1. **Built now:** To tag v2 (scenes and day headers, the summary row, Tag scene, Start tagging, tap a tile to tag from it, long-press multi-select with Tag and Skip, the ⋮ menu with Add earlier videos, Select clips, Skipped (n) and Skip all, the Skip all dialog with Undo, an error state); the tag player (muted autoplay, one tap tags and moves on, Pick several, long-press a chip, Same as previous, Skip, + New reel, "n of N" with the scene, previous and next without tagging, Undo, All done); the Skipped list (by day, Restore one or several, with Undo); the reel picker's tag mode (parked in D-80).
  2. **Waits:** Add videos and the photo picker in Manual mode (Phase 7); the new-clips notification and the evening reminder (Phase 6); editing (Phase 8).
  3. **Owner rulings (plan review, 2026-10-31):**
     - **Pick several is sticky** (spec §3): it stays on from clip to clip until Cancel; the picks clear at each clip. This overrides the prototype, which resets it.
     - **Other reels** holds Ready to edit and Published reels, folded by default; Collecting reels are always open; with no Collecting reel, Other reels is open. As the reel picker does (D-88).
     - **Undo in the player goes back:** it puts the clip back in To tag, and the open player returns to that clip.
     - **The video scrim** uses the prototype's values (top 0.78, bottom 0.6, solid fade), not the tokens' (0.7 / 0.55). (D-92 corrects the labels: the prototype's `.vtop` is 0.6 and `.vbot` 0.78 with the solid part, so the top is 0.6 and the bottom 0.78; the tokens had top 0.55 and bottom 0.7.)
     - **To tag's error state:** "Couldn't load your clips", "Your videos are safe in your gallery.", **Try again**.
  4. **The clip on screen leaving To tag** without the player acting (a verify, a tag from elsewhere) moves the player on to the next waiting clip. Clips that arrive while the player is open are not added to its session.
- Why: tagging is the app's core loop (spec §4: fast, no lost tags, never the wrong clip). Sticky Pick several helps runs of clips that go into two reels; Undo that goes back lets the user correct a wrong tap at once.
- Consequences: Tasks 2–10 of the plan each add their own decision from D-92 on.

### D-92 · Player chrome debts: the scrub bar's words and touch area, the prototype's video scrim, one painter and one scrim for the package, the snack's shadow, the nav badge's whole label
- Date: 2026-10-31 · Phase: 5 · Task: 2 · Amends D-47 and D-48, corrects a label in D-91
- Decision:
  1. **Scrub bar semantics (D-81).** `TtScrubBar` takes `semanticLabel` ("Playback position"), `semanticValue` ("1:12 of 4:32") and, when it can seek, `semanticIncreasedValue` and `semanticDecreasedValue` (an `assert` requires them with `onSeek`). The slider's value is the caller's sentence, no longer a name made of the two times with a slash. A screen reader's increase and decrease still call `onSeek` with the position moved by `TtMetrics.scrubSemanticStep` (5 % of the clip), clamped to 0 and 1. The clip viewer builds the sentences from `viewerScrubLabel` and `viewerScrubValue` (`app_en.arb`); the value after a step is the position 5 % further or back, written like the time (nearest whole second).
  2. **Scrub row: 48 dp to touch, 22 dp drawn.** The CSS `.scrub` is `padding: 10px 16px 0` around a 12 px line, 22 px. The bar stays 48 dp high (the gesture area) and draws a `TtMetrics.scrubRowHeight` (22 dp) row, with the track, fill and thumb, in the middle of it. The gesture area reaches `TtMetrics.scrubTrackOverhang` (10 dp, the large thumb's radius) past each end of the track, so the thumb at 0 % or 100 % is fully inside it; a tap in that margin seeks to the end. The 10 dp gap to the times is now spent by that margin (the row has no spacing), so the drawn track keeps the CSS gap. The redundant `SizedBox` is gone. A halo (large) is paint only and is not part of the gesture area.
  3. **Video scrims are the prototype's, by the owner's ruling (D-91).** `design/tools/build_tokens.py`: `scrimTop` `rgba(0,0,0,0.6)` (proto.css `.viewer .vtop`) and `scrimBottom` `rgba(0,0,0,0.78)` (`.viewer .vbot`), from 0.55 and 0.70; regenerated (JSON, CSS tokens, `tokens.g.dart`). The bottom scrim is solid: clear at its inner end, full from 30 % of the way up (`linear-gradient(transparent, rgba(0,0,0,.78) 30%)`), `TtMetrics.videoScrimBottomSolid = 0.7`; the top fades linearly. The contrast note in `build_tokens.py` now gives the worst case: white footage under the top scrim leaves #666666 (5.7:1 for white), under the bottom #383838 (12:1). **The owner's words in D-91 had the labels the other way round** ("top 0.78, bottom 0.6"): the CSS says top 0.6 and bottom 0.78, and the old tokens were top 0.55 and bottom 0.70, so the numbers were always read as the CSS's pair, bottom first. This follows the CSS (the owner's rule: the prototype's values win). If the owner meant top 0.78, it is two numbers in `build_tokens.py`.
  4. **The painters and scrims live once, in `components/internal/`.** `dashed_border.dart`: `DashedBorderPainter(color, radius, width, dash, gap)`, used by the missing clip tile and the add-reference tile (both had a private copy that differed only in its constants). `scrim.dart`: `Scrim(color, top, solid)`, a gradient that fills its parent from one edge, with no touches and no semantics, used by `TtVideoSurface` (placed with `Positioned`) and the clip tile (`FractionallySizedBox`). The reel card's scrim stays its own (it uses `mediaScrimEnd`, a token of its own) and so does the pill tabs' stadium-shaped dashed painter (another path). The goldens did not change from this move alone (proved before any look change). `tag_panel.dart` has no dashed painter, only the highlight, which stays.
  5. **Snack shadow.** The CSS has one: `.snack { box-shadow: var(--elev-3) }`, and `.style-tt .snack` changes only the fill and the corners. `TtSnack` casts it: `design/tools/build_tokens.py` now also writes the elevation tokens as `TtElevationTokens.level0..level5` (the layers of each CSS `--elev-N`), and the snack uses `level3`. The 94 % fill lets a little of the shadow show through, which CSS would clip; at 6 % it is hardly seen.
  6. **Nav badge label.** The package already took the whole label (`TtNavItem.badgeSemanticLabel`, replacing the tab name). The app's string was the part that dropped the name ("7 clips to tag"): `toTagBadgeSemantics` is now "To tag, {count, plural, =0{no clips} =1{1 clip} other{{count} clips}}", and `TtNavItem`'s doc says the label must begin with the tab's name.
  7. `video_surface_test` runs `expectTtAccessible`.
- Why: a screen reader said "1:12 / 4:32" as the slider's name and had no value to announce when it stepped; a 48 dp row drawn 48 dp looked unlike the prototype and its thumb sat half outside the area that takes the finger; the two scrim values and the CSS disagreed, and the owner picked the CSS; two copies of a painter and of a scrim are two places to fix the next defect (the dashed "New reel" chip of Task 3 needs the painter); the To tag tab said only "7 clips to tag", not which tab; the snack floated flat on bright video.
- Alternatives considered: one callback `String Function(double progress)` for all three semantic values (one argument, but the brief names `semanticValue`, and a plain string is easier to read at the call site); shrinking the layout row to 22 dp and widening the hit area with a hit-test override (Flutter does not hit-test outside a box, a custom render object for one bar); putting the overhang as `Row` spacing and a transparent `Padding` (the gesture area would then end at the track); a Dart-side copy of the elevation values in `TtMetrics` (a second place for a design token); also removing the stadium painter of the pill tabs (a different path, not a copy).
- Consequences: every caller of `TtScrubBar` passes three or four more strings (the clip viewer, Widgetbook and the goldens did; the tag player, Task 7, will). The quick row (**Skip** and **Same as previous**) is not a package component: the player screen (Task 7) builds it with `.qrow`'s padding, 12 dp above and below and 16 dp at the sides, and, because the scrub bar is 26 dp taller than the CSS row (48 against 22), may take that much off the space between them. The macOS goldens of `video_surface`, `snack`, `player_chrome` (a new scenario, scrub at 100 %) and the theme swatches changed; the controller makes the CI set.


### D-93 · The tag panel's slots and cap, the Other reels toggle, the New reel chip, the done screen, and an info bar that was already there
- Date: 2026-10-31 · Phase: 5 · Task: 3 · Amends D-47
- Decision:
  1. **`TtTagPanel` is a semantics group and takes five slots.** `semanticLabel` is required (the caller's "Reels", D-81): `Semantics(container: true, explicitChildNodes: true)` around the whole panel, so the header, every chip and the footer stay separate nodes inside a group with that name. The scrolling part holds, in order, 8 dp apart: `emptyText` (a centred `bodyMedium` message across the row, 8 dp in at the sides, 8 above and 4 below, as the prototype's no-reels line), `chips` (two to a row), `toggle` (a `TtOtherReelsToggle`), `foldedChips` (two to a row). The folded chips are shown only while `toggle.expanded` is true or when there is no toggle, so what is on screen cannot disagree with what the toggle announces; the app passes them every time and the panel decides. A `TtNewReelChip` with `fullWidth` ends the row before it and takes a row of its own, so an odd number of chips before it leaves no hole. At a text scale of 1.5 and up (D-47) every slot is one per row. The two-column rows live in one private function, `_gridRows`, used for both groups.
  2. **The cap is the D-47 rule, now tested with the slots.** At most `TtMetrics.tagPanelMaxHeight` (340 dp) times the text scale, up to `tagPanelMaxHeightLarge` (520 dp at 2x); the panel also gives way to a shorter parent (a `ConstrainedBox` takes the smaller of the two). Everything between the header and the footer scrolls, the toggle and the folded chips included, and the header and footer stay put. The later item "budget its height cap of up to 520 dp" is closed by the test `panel caps its height at 2x and scrolls` (30 chips, the new reel chip, the toggle and 10 folded chips on a 780 dp screen: 520 dp, the header at the same place after the scroll, the last chip tappable). The package does not know the rest of the player; the screen (Task 7) leaves the video what the cap leaves it.
  3. **`TtOtherReelsToggle`** (`label`, `expanded`, `onToggle`; in `tag_panel.dart`): a button announced as expanded or collapsed (`Semantics.expanded`), named by its label, which the caller writes ("Other reels (4)" folded, "Other reels" open). A `labelMedium` label in `onSurfaceVariant` and an 18 dp `expand_more` or `expand_less`. The row is drawn 36 dp high (`otherReelsToggleHeight`) inside a 48 dp box that takes the whole tap (the same trade as the small button, whose layout is 48 dp with a 36 dp look), so the panel is 12 dp taller with a toggle than the CSS's inline `min-height: 36px`.
  4. **`TtNewReelChip`** (`title`, `subtitle`, `semanticLabel`, `fullWidth`, `onTap`; in `reel_chip.dart`): a `TtReelChip` without a fill: the same 58 dp box and cover position (so it lines up in a row), a plus on the secondary container, the title in primary over the subtitle in `onSurfaceVariant`, and the dashed outline from `internal/dashed_border.dart` (1.5 dp, dash 4.5, gap 3, outline colour, 18 dp radius). The outline is painted over the chip's 1 dp inset, half a dp less than the CSS's 1.5 dp border, to keep the heights equal. `fullWidth` centres the cover and the title (the content is centred in the padded area, 2 dp left of the middle, as in CSS) and the panel gives it a whole row; the prototype shows no subtitle there, and the caller leaves it out. The semantic label is `title` unless given.
  5. **`TtTagDone` and `TtTagDoneRow`** (`tag_done.dart`): `.done-scr`. A 96 dp success circle with a 48 dp check that pops in from 40 % (`long2`, emphasized decelerate; no motion under "remove animations"), `headlineMedium` title, `bodyLarge` body at 75 % white, the summary (max 300 dp wide, 8 % white, 22 dp radius, 1 dp dividers at 10 % between rows and none after the last) and two buttons: a large filled one and a text one drawn white (the theme's primary replaced by `onVideo` for that button only, the CSS `.done-scr .btn.text`). It wraps itself in `TtVideoSurface`, so it is dark and black whatever the app theme is (the player screen no longer has to remember). Rows are `MergeSemantics`, read as the name then the count; the badge is excluded from semantics; the title is a heading. Content is centred and the whole screen scrolls (the CSS did not, but 25 reels or 2x text must not push the buttons out). It adds no top space for the status bar: the screen wraps it in a `SafeArea`. With no rows the summary is left out.
  6. **`TtInfoBar` already existed** (`banners.dart`, from the Phase 4 groundwork: an 18 dp icon, a wrapping `bodySmall` line, 20 dp radius, with tests for the box, the wrap at 2x and accessibility, a golden and a Widgetbook use case), so no `info_bar.dart` was made: a second widget, or moving the class and its catalogue entry, would be the only change. The Skipped list (Task 9) uses it as it is.
  7. **`TtSceneHeader`'s selection variant** needed no code (its `action` is a widget): a test now holds a text small `Select all` / `Unselect` button at the end of the row, its tap, 2x and accessibility, and the Widgetbook use case has a knob for it.
  8. **The 164 dp chip scenarios at 2x** (later item): the panel gives a chip 336 dp at 2x, never 164. `TtScenario` has a `largeWidth` (used from scale 1.5), so the `reel_chip` goldens draw each chip at 164 dp at 1x and 336 dp at 2x, and the separate "full-width row" scenario was dropped as redundant. The overflow test runs at 336 dp (the panel's row) and keeps 164 dp at 2x as a squeeze test named as one. `ttGoldenTest` takes `brightnesses`; `tag_done` makes only the dark image, as the screen is always dark. The panel's golden keeps both of its images, as before.
- Why: the player needs the folded Other reels group, the dashed New reel chip and the no-reels state (spec §3, D-91) and a done screen; an unlabelled panel was a pile of buttons to a screen reader; a 36 dp row with a 36 dp target fails Android's 48 dp guideline; and a 164 dp chip at 2x was a picture of something the app cannot show.
- Alternatives considered: a `folded` flag on the panel plus a callback (two sources for one state; the toggle already carries it); the toggle inside the chip list (it would take half a row); a separate `fullWidthChips` list on the panel (the chip knows it is full width, so one flag, not two); drawing the toggle exactly 36 dp tall with a 48 dp hit area through a custom render object (a lot of code for 12 dp); making `TtTagDone` a plain column and leaving the theme to the caller; moving `TtInfoBar` to its own file.
- Consequences: every caller of `TtTagPanel` passes `semanticLabel` (Widgetbook's video surface use case and the goldens did; the player, Task 7, will). The macOS goldens of `reel_chip` and `tag_panel` changed, `new_reel_chip` and `tag_done` are new; the controller makes the CI set.

### D-94 · Skip, Skip all and Restore return an `InboxUndo`; Undo puts back only rows still exactly as the action left them; skip all reads and writes in one transaction; tagging reuses `addClipsToReels`
- Date: 2026-10-31 · Phase: 5 · Task: 4 · Extends D-84
- Decision:
  1. **`InboxUndo`** (`domain/models/undo_record.dart`), a fifth `UndoRecord`: `before` (the `inbox` rows the action moved, as they were: clip id, state, `addedAt`, by clip id, an unmodifiable list) and `after` (the state the action set). `isEmpty` is `before.isEmpty`.
  2. **`ClipsRepository.skipClips(ids)`, `restoreClips(ids)` and `skipAllPending()`** replace Phase 2's `skip` / `restore`, which returned nothing and had no caller in `lib/` (their test callers now call the new methods; the old tests stay). Each is one transaction that reads the rows it is about to move and then moves only those: `skipClips` the given ids that are `pending`, `restoreClips` the given ids that are `skipped` (batches of 500, `ClipsRepository.idBatch`), `skipAllPending` every `pending` row. A clip in the other state, in a reel (no `inbox` row) or unknown is left alone. **Only `state` is written: `addedAt` keeps when the clip arrived** (To tag's order and the Skipped list's days do not depend on a skip). Nothing to move writes nothing and returns an empty record. Log lines hold counts only (D-10).
  3. **Skip all reads inside its transaction**, and the `UPDATE … WHERE state = 'pending'` must write as many rows as the `SELECT` read (else `StateError`, rolled back, as `sweepOrphans` does). Drift runs a statement from another caller (a scan's `addCheckedClips`) after the open transaction, so a clip a scan saves meanwhile is either in the record (saved first) or stays `pending`; it is never skipped without being in the record, where Undo could not bring it back. The test `skip all takes every pending in one transaction` starts a real scan from outside the transaction's zone between the read and the write and gives it 200 ms to run there; it runs after.
  4. **Undo of an `InboxUndo`** (`UndoWriter._undoInbox`, through `undo` and `undoAll` like the others, idempotent by the D-84 `Expando`): for each entry, `UPDATE inbox SET state = before.state, added_at = before.addedAt WHERE clip_id = … AND state = after AND added_at = before.addedAt`. A row goes back **only while it is exactly as the action left it**. The brief's rule was "still in state `after`"; the `addedAt` match is added because the action moved only the state, so a row in `after` with another `addedAt` was written by something else since (`untagToInbox` puts a clip back in To tag as `pending` with `addedAt` = now: undoing an older restore must not pull it into Skipped). A clip tagged since has no row and keeps its reel (an update never inserts, so a clip is never in a reel and a list at once, D-57); one restored or skipped again since is in the other state; a clip a scan added after skip all is not in the record. Undo never touches a reel, a link or a clip.
  5. **Tagging reuses `addClipsToReels`** and its `AddUndo` unchanged (D-84 points 7–9): the player's tag of one clip into one or more reels is that call, and its Undo puts the clip back in To tag with its old `addedAt` (pinned by `undo add after tag puts the clip back in To tag with its addedAt`). An add and a skip undone together go through one `undoAll`, one transaction (`undoAll of an add and a skip is one transaction`).
- Why: spec §4, no lost tags and never the wrong clip; Review Focus 4 (undo after things moved on). A record read outside the transaction could miss a row that the write then moved, and that clip would stay in Skipped with no Undo. Writing `addedAt` on skip would reorder To tag on restore and break the "only rows still as the action left them" test, which relies on the action leaving `addedAt` alone.
- Alternatives considered: keeping `skip` / `restore` and adding record-returning twins (two ways to do one thing, one of them without Undo); updating skip all by the ids read (also correct inside the transaction, but the state predicate plus the count check states the invariant directly and needs no batches for 300 clips); an upsert of `before` on Undo (would give a tagged clip a second place); checking only the state on Undo, as the brief wrote (lets an old restore's Undo pull a clip that went to a reel and back into Skipped); a "skipped at" column (a schema change; v1 stays).
- Consequences: Task 6 (the player) gets an `InboxUndo` from `skipClips` and `AddUndo`s from `addClipsToReels` for its snacks; Task 8 (To tag) uses `skipClips` and `skipAllPending`, Task 9 (Skipped) `restoreClips`. Schema stays v1. `untagToInbox` is still not called by any screen.
